=== PROPOSAL 1: Break-even-pair characterization of the loss ratio: LR is a ratio of two normalized (signed) Choquet premium functionals iff its cash profile is a Möbius map and it aggregates comonotone risks by premium-weighted averaging; Wang–Young–Panjer (monotone distortion) tier obtained by adding monotonicity of the two break-even functionals, with no continuity axiom at all (Insurance-pricing axiomatics scholar (premium-principle characterizations: Wang–Young–Panjer, Wang 1996 layer densities, Furman–Zitikis weighted premiums, Haezendonck–Goovaerts/Bellini–Rosazza Gianin implicit premia, Gerber/Deprez–Gerber translation axioms)) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless probability space. L^∞ = essentially bounded random variables (losses: larger = worse), sup norm ‖·‖_∞. Domain of the loss ratio: 𝒳 := L^∞_+ \ {0} = {X ∈ L^∞ : X ≥ 0 a.s., P(X>0) > 0}. Note 𝒳 is closed under positive scaling, comonotone sums, and upward cash shifts X ↦ X + c, c ≥ 0 (but not under downward shifts; Assa's LR(X − m) is recovered in the proof through the cash-additive extension of the break-even pair to L^∞). X, Y are comonotone if (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 a.s.⊗a.s.; constants are comonotone with everything. H := {h : [0,1] → ℝ of bounded variation, h(0) = 0}. For h ∈ H and X ∈ 𝒳 the survival-form Choquet integral is C_h(X) := ∫_0^∞ h(P(X > x)) dx; on L^∞ it is extended as the signed Choquet integral I_h(X) := ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X > x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X > x)) dx (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020, Def. 1 and Remark 1; I_h = C_h on 𝒳). A 'normalized distortion pair' is (h,g) ∈ H×H with h(1) = g(1) = 1 and g > 0 on (0,1]; it is a 'Wang–Young–Panjer pair' if moreover h and g are nondecreasing (then h, g : [0,1] → [0,1] are distortion functions in the sense of Wang–Young–Panjer 1997 / Yaari 1987). LR : 𝒳 → ℝ is an arbitrary map (the loss ratio). A 'break-even pair' for LR is a pair of maps a : 𝒳 → ℝ (the risk functional ρ) and b : 𝒳 → (0,∞) (the premium functional Π) with LR = a/b on 𝒳. The fair set is N := {X ∈ 𝒳 : LR(X) = 1}. Insurance reading of the two cash axioms used below: 'adding a sure loss c to a policy adds c to its premium and c to its risk assessment' (Gerber 1974 axiom (iii), Deprez–Gerber translation invariance, Wang–Young–Panjer normalization H(1)=1), and 'the loss ratio of a book of comonotone (layered) risks is the premium-weighted average of the lines' loss ratios' (Wang 1996 layer-premium-density aggregation, Furman–Zitikis weighted-mean form).
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A1) [Cash-additive break-even pair / Möbius cash profile] LR admits a break-even pair (a,b), LR = a/b on 𝒳 with b > 0, such that a(X + c) = a(X) + c and b(X + c) = b(X) + c for all X ∈ 𝒳, c ≥ 0. INTRINSIC READING: (A1) holds iff for every X ∈ 𝒳 the cash profile c ↦ LR(X + c), c ≥ 0, is a Möbius (linear-fractional) map with unit-slope numerator and denominator, LR(X + c) = (c + a(X))/(c + b(X)) with b(X) > 0; off the fair set N = {LR = 1} the parameters are uniquely determined by LR (a(X) = minus the zero, b(X) = minus the pole of the profile, and cash additivity of a, b is then automatic: compare the profiles of X and X + c), so on 𝒳 \ N this is a statement about LR alone. Consequences: LR(c) = 1 for constants, and c ↦ LR(X + c) is monotone with limit 1 ('translation to break-even', F1). (A2) (A2) [Law invariance] a(X) = a(Y) and b(X) = b(Y) whenever X and Y have the same law. Off N this is equivalent to law invariance of LR (if X =_d Y then X + c =_d Y + c for all c ≥ 0, so the two cash profiles coincide and so do their zero and pole). (A3) (A3) [Premium-weighted comonotone averaging] For all comonotone X, Y ∈ 𝒳: b(X + Y) = b(X) + b(Y) and LR(X + Y) = [b(X)·LR(X) + b(Y)·LR(Y)] / [b(X) + b(Y)]. Since a = LR·b, (A3) is equivalent to comonotone additivity of BOTH a and b. It implies the two-sided comonotone mediant inequality min{LR(X),LR(Y)} ≤ LR(X+Y) ≤ max{LR(X),LR(Y)} of F1 (a weighted average lies between its terms), and conversely, under (A1), the mediant inequality implies (A3) for every comonotone pair with LR(X), LR(Y) strictly on the same side of 1 (proof in the sketch). (A3) is the cardinal (Aczél/Sincov) form of Chew–Dekel betweenness: the weights are revealed to be premium shares. (A4) (A4) [Regularity of the break-even pair — one of two alternatives] (A4-c) [signed tier] a and b are uniformly continuous on (𝒳, ‖·‖_∞); or (A4-m) [insurance tier] a and b are monotone: X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ a(X) ≤ a(Y) and b(X) ≤ b(Y). (A4-m) implies (A4-c): by (A1), X ≤ Y + ‖X−Y‖_∞ gives a(X) ≤ a(Y) + ‖X−Y‖_∞ and symmetrically, so a and b are 1-Lipschitz. In the insurance tier NO continuity axiom whatsoever is needed. (A5) NOT ASSUMED (and shown to be consequences): scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X) (follows from positive homogeneity of signed Choquet integrals, WWW Lemma 2(iii), or from Schmeidler's Choquet representation in the monotone tier); pointwise monotonicity of LR (impossible unless LR ≡ 1, F2); any property of the associated family {ρ_β} (it is derived: ρ_β = (a − βb)/(1−β)); quasi-convexity; elicitability/identifiability.
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: Residual pair-level content on the fair set N = {LR = 1} (where the cash profile is identically 1 and LR cannot see (a,b)): axioms (A1)–(A4) are stated for SOME break-even pair (a,b), i.e. the hypothesis is existential ('LR admits a break-even pair with properties (A1)–(A4)'), in the same way as the Orlicz/Haezendonck–Goovaerts premium is defined through the existence of a solution of its level-crossing equation (I[5], Bellini–Rosazza Gianin 2008). Off N the pair is uniquely determined by LR, so there the axioms are axioms on LR; on N they constrain the choice of (a,b). The theorem then proves that the pair is unique whenever LR ≢ 1, so no additional free parameter survives. | Positivity b > 0 on 𝒳 (every non-null loss carries a strictly positive premium; equivalently g > 0 on (0,1], which excludes VaR-type premiums g = 1_{(α,1]}). This is a non-degeneracy requirement making LR finite; it is a property of the pole of the cash profile, hence of LR off N. | (A4-m) monotonicity of a and b (insurance tier) is a genuine pair-level hypothesis: by F2 no property of LR itself can detect it (LR = C_h/C_g with h non-monotone has exactly the same LR-level behaviour as a monotone one; see counterexample 5). It is a hypothesis only on the two ENDPOINTS a = ρ_0 and b = lim_{β→∞} ρ_β of the associated family, never on the intermediate non-monotone members. | No hypothesis is placed on the associated family {ρ_β}; Assa's association LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 and the signed-Choquet form of every ρ_β (F7) are conclusions.
CONCLUSION: THEOREM. Let LR : 𝒳 → ℝ. (A) [signed tier] The following are equivalent: (I) LR admits a break-even pair (a,b) satisfying (A1), (A2), (A3), (A4-c). (II) There is a normalized distortion pair (h,g) — h, g of bounded variation on [0,1], h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1, g > 0 on (0,1] — such that LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx for all X ∈ 𝒳. (B) [insurance / Wang–Young–Panjer tier] The following are equivalent: (I_m) LR admits a break-even pair satisfying (A1), (A2), (A3), (A4-m). (II_m) (II) holds with (h,g) a Wang–Young–Panjer pair, i.e. h and g nondecreasing distortion functions [0,1] → [0,1] with g > 0 on (0,1] (ρ = C_h a distortion risk measure, Π = C_g a Wang–Young–Panjer premium principle). Moreover, inside (A): a is monotone ⇔ h nondecreasing, b is monotone ⇔ g nondecreasing (WWW Lemma 2(i)). (C) [uniqueness] If LR ≢ 1 then the pair (h,g) in (II) is unique, and the break-even pair is unique and equals (a,b) = (C_h, C_g) on all of 𝒳 (including the fair set N); explicitly h(p)/g(p) = LR(1_B) and g(p) = (φ_B(t) − 1)/(t(LR(1_B) − φ_B(t))), φ_B(t) := LR(1 + t·1_B), for any B with P(B) = p and LR(1_B) ≠ 1, the remaining p being recovered from nested layers 1_B + 1_D. If LR ≡ 1 then (II) holds with every h = g and uniqueness fails. (D) [associated family — Assa's association is a theorem] Extend a, b to L^∞ by a(X) := a(X + M) − M (M > ‖X‖_∞), likewise b; then a = I_h, b = I_g on L^∞, and for β ≠ 1 the β-capital m_β(X) := the unique m with (a(X) − m)/(b(X) − m) = β (i.e. the cash m solving LR(X − m) = β in the extended sense) equals ρ_β(X) := (a(X) − β b(X))/(1 − β) = I_{(h − βg)/(1−β)}(X): a law-invariant, comonotone-additive, positively homogeneous, cash-additive (unit slope) signed Choquet integral, Lipschitz with constant TV((h−βg)/(1−β)) ≤ (TV h + β TV g)/|1−β|, generally NOT monotone (monotone iff h − βg is monotone, WWW Lemma 2(i)), with ρ_0 = a = ρ and lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = b = Π, and LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 for all X ∈ 𝒳, β ≠ 1 (plus LR(X) = 1 ⇔ a(X) = b(X)). (E) [consequences on LR] Under (I): LR is scale invariant, law invariant, satisfies translation-to-break-even, the two-sided comonotone mediant property, is ‖·‖_∞-continuous on {b ≥ δ}, and is never pointwise monotone unless h = g (F2). (F) [supplements, each an iff on the endpoints, none derivable from LR] a subadditive ⇔ h concave; b subadditive ⇔ g concave (WWW Thm 3); LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⇔ h ≤ g on [0,1] (WWW Lemma 1 after cash-additive extension); for β ≠ 1, the sublevel set {X ∈ L^∞ : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} (extended LR ≤ β) is convex ⇔ ρ_β quasi-convex ⇔ h − βg concave (WWW Thm 3 (i)⇔(v), Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang Prop. 2.7 since ρ_β(1) − ρ_β(0) = 1 ≠ 0), so the extended LR is quasi-convex ⇔ h concave and g convex, and quasi-concave ⇔ h convex and g concave (this corrects F4's 'actuarial picture': ρ = E, g concave gives a quasi-CONCAVE loss ratio); in the WYP tier with h concave, (a,b) is a (coherent risk measure, coherent premium) pair iff g is also concave, and then ρ_β = (1−β)^{-1}(∫ES_α dμ_h − β∫ES_α dμ_g) is a signed Kusuoka mixture (Kusuoka Thm 7 / Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93, via Shapiro's uniqueness of μ_h, μ_g). VARIANTS: drop (A2) and strengthen (A4-c) to 'bounded variation + outer continuity' on the bounded F-measurable functions (or keep (A4-m) with no continuity): LR = ∫X dν_1/∫X dν_2 for two capacities ν_1(A) = a(1_A), ν_2(A) = b(1_A) (Schmeidler 1986; CMMM 2012 Cor. 18); replace L^∞ by a law-invariant convex cone 𝒳' ⊃ L^∞ (e.g. L^p) and add 'continuity at infinity' of a, b: same conclusion with distortion riskmetrics (Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 Thm 1).
PROOF SKETCH: Citation key (digest sections): C[n] = Choquet/Schmeidler scholar; I[n] = Insurance-pricing scholar; S[n] = Signed-Choquet/R. Wang strand; K[n] = Kusuoka/coherent strand; A[n] = Acceptability-index strand; R[n] = Return/star-shaped strand; E[n] = Elicitability strand; P[n] = Performance-ratio strand; D[n] = Decision-theory strand; H[n] = Author strand. The deep tools are: WWW Thm 1 (C[4] = S[1] = K[8] = E[5]: on atomless L^∞, law-invariant + comonotonic-additive + uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous ⇔ signed Choquet integral I_h, h ∈ H, with Lemma 1 (uniqueness of h), Lemma 2 (i) monotone ⇔ h increasing, (ii) I_h(X+c) = I_h(X) + c·h(1), (iii) positive homogeneity, Lemma 4 Lipschitz constant TV(h)); Schmeidler 1986 (C[1], read verbatim as CMMM 2015 Thm 2: monotone + comonotonic additive on bounded measurable functions ⇔ Choquet integral w.r.t. the unique capacity μ(A) = V(1_A), no continuity); and the law-invariance step capacity ⇒ distortion (C[7] Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i); E[8] Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1).

STEP 0 (preliminaries on (A1)). If LR = a/b with a, b cash-additive (slope 1, c ≥ 0) then LR(X+c) = (a(X)+c)/(b(X)+c). Conversely, if (a+c)/(b+c) = (a'+c)/(b'+c) for all c ≥ 0 with a ≠ b, cross-multiplication gives ab' = a'b and a + b' = a' + b; if a' = b' the profile would be ≡ 1 forcing a = b, contradiction; hence with d := a − a' = b − b' we get d·b' = d·a', so d = 0. Thus off N the pair is unique, and comparing the profiles of X and X + c yields a(X+c) = a(X)+c, b(X+c) = b(X)+c there. Also LR(c·1) = (a(1)c' + ...) — more directly: the profile of X = 1 is (a(1)+c)/(b(1)+c) and has limit 1, while scale invariance is not yet available; we only need LR(1) = 1 later, which follows from positive homogeneity (Step 3) and a(1) = b(1) = 1 (Step 4).

STEP 1 (extension of the break-even pair to L^∞). For X ∈ L^∞ put a(X) := a(X + M) − M, b(X) := b(X + M) − M for any M > ‖X‖_∞ (so X + M ≥ 1 ∈ 𝒳 — X + M is a.s. ≥ M − ‖X‖_∞ > 0). Well defined: for M' > M, a(X+M') = a((X+M) + (M'−M)) = a(X+M) + (M'−M) by (A1). It agrees with a on 𝒳 (take M = 0) and is cash-additive on L^∞ for all real c. Comonotone additivity transfers: if X, Y ∈ L^∞ are comonotone, so are X+M, Y+M' ∈ 𝒳 (constants are comonotone with everything), and a(X+Y) = a((X+M)+(Y+M')) − M − M' = a(X) + a(Y) by (A3). Law invariance (A2), uniform continuity (A4-c) (use a common M) and monotonicity (A4-m) transfer verbatim. [This is the Bielecki–Cialenco–Zhang cash-shift recovery P[2] Thm 4.6 / Assa Prop. 1 read as a definition: a(X) = −inf{c : LR(X + c) ≥ 0-level}… i.e. the extended pair is the unique cash-additive hull; cf. the Haezendonck–Goovaerts cash-additive hull I[5].]

STEP 2 (signed tier, (I) ⇒ (II)). By Step 1 the extended a and b are law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous on L^∞ of an atomless space, so by WWW Thm 1 (C[4]) there are unique h, g ∈ H with a = I_h, b = I_g. Cash additivity of a with slope 1 and WWW Lemma 2(ii) (I_h(X+c) = I_h(X) + c h(1)) give h(1) = 1; likewise g(1) = 1. Positivity: for p ∈ (0,1] pick B with P(B) = p (atomless) and get g(p) = I_g(1_B) = b(1_B) > 0. On 𝒳 the signed form reduces to the survival form (the negative-part integral vanishes for X ≥ 0), so LR(X) = a(X)/b(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X). (Uniform continuity is used exactly once — to force h, g ∈ H; WWW Remark 3: with mere norm continuity the representation persists with h possibly of unbounded variation.)

STEP 3 (positive homogeneity and scale invariance are consequences). I_h(λX) = λ I_h(X), λ > 0 (WWW Lemma 2(iii); CMMM Lemma 14(iii), C[2]), hence LR(λX) = LR(X): Assa's Prop. 1 equivalence 'ρ_β positively homogeneous ⇔ LR scale invariant' is recovered, and (A1)-(A3)-(A4) already contain scale invariance.

STEP 4 ((II) ⇒ (I), signed tier). Put a := C_h, b := C_g on 𝒳 (restrictions of I_h, I_g). (A1): for X ≥ 0, c ≥ 0, ∫_0^∞ h(P(X+c > x))dx = ∫_0^c h(1)dx + ∫_c^∞ h(P(X > x − c))dx = c + C_h(X); b > 0 on 𝒳 because X ∈ 𝒳 has ess sup X > 0 and g(P(X>x)) > 0 for 0 ≤ x < ess sup X. (A2): I_h is law-invariant by definition. (A3): I_h, I_g are comonotonic-additive (Schmeidler/Yaari; WWW Thm 1 'if' direction; I[9] Wang 1996 property (vi) in the insurance setting), so b(X+Y) = b(X)+b(Y) and a(X+Y) = a(X)+a(Y), i.e. LR(X+Y) b(X+Y) = LR(X) b(X) + LR(Y) b(Y), which is the premium-weighted averaging. (A4-c): |I_h(X) − I_h(Y)| ≤ TV(h)‖X−Y‖_∞ (WWW Lemma 4; Wang–Wang–Wei ASTIN Remark 2, C[5]).

STEP 5 (insurance tier (B)). (I_m) ⇒ (II_m): by Step 1 the extended a, b are monotone and comonotonic-additive on L^∞ (bounded measurable functions modulo null sets; Schmeidler's theorem is applied on B(Ω,F) through any version — or directly in the Riesz-space form CMMM 2015 Thm 6, C[3]). Schmeidler 1986 (C[1]; I[3]) gives a(X) = ∫ X dν_a with the unique capacity ν_a(A) = a(1_A), ν_a(Ω) = a(1) = 1 (cash additivity: a(1) = a(0) + 1 and a(0) = 0 by comonotone additivity a(0) = 2a(0)). Law invariance and atomlessness make ν_a(A) a function of P(A) alone: h(p) := a(1_A), P(A) = p, is well defined, nondecreasing (monotonicity of a), h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1, and ν_a = h∘P, so a = C_h (C[7] Prop. 2(i); E[8] Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1; the '≥' and '>' forms differ only at countably many x). Same for b = C_g; g(p) = b(1_B) > 0 for p > 0. No continuity hypothesis has been used, exactly as in Wang–Young–Panjer Theorem (I[1]) whose truncation-continuity axiom (A4) is only needed for unbounded risks (Greco, C[10]). (II_m) ⇒ (I_m): Step 4 plus WWW Lemma 2(i) / Wang–Wang–Wei Prop. 2(ii) (h, g nondecreasing ⇒ I_h, I_g monotone). The 'moreover' clause is WWW Lemma 2(i) applied to the pair obtained in Step 2.

STEP 6 (uniqueness (C), i.e. F3 in the signed normalized class). Suppose C_h/C_g = C_{h'}/C_{g'} on 𝒳 for two normalized distortion pairs, and h ≠ g (equivalently LR ≢ 1). Bernoulli losses: LR(1_B) = h(p)/g(p) =: r(p) for P(B) = p, so h/g = h'/g'. Two-layer losses 1 + t1_B ∈ 𝒳: φ_B(t) := LR(1 + t1_B) = (1 + t h(p))/(1 + t g(p)); solving, g(p) = (φ_B(t) − 1)/(t(r(p) − φ_B(t))) whenever r(p) ≠ φ_B(t), which (since h = r g) holds iff h(p) ≠ g(p), i.e. r(p) ≠ 1. Hence g = g' and h = h' at every p with r(p) ≠ 1 (such p exist because h ≠ g). At p with r(p) = 1 take q with r(q) ≠ 1 and the nested layer X = 1_B + 1_D (D ⊂ B, P(B) = p, P(D) = q, or B ⊂ D if q > p), which is in 𝒳 and comonotone: LR(X) = (h(p) + h(q))/(g(p) + g(q)) = (g(p) + h(q))/(g(p) + g(q)) ≠ 1, and g(p)(1 − LR(X)) = LR(X) g(q) − h(q) determines g(p) = h(p). So (h,g) = (h',g'); then any break-even pair satisfying (I) equals (C_h, C_g) by Step 2, including on N. If LR ≡ 1, (A1)–(A4) hold with a = b = C_h for every BV h with h(1) = 1 (and with every nondecreasing h in tier (B)).

STEP 7 (associated family (D)). With the extended a = I_h, b = I_g, linearity of h ↦ I_h (C[4]; author strand H[1]) gives (a − βb)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} =: ρ_β, whose distortion has value 1 at 1, so ρ_β is cash-additive with unit slope, positively homogeneous, law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, Lipschitz with TV((h−βg)/(1−β)) (WWW Lemma 4; ASTIN Remark 2), and monotone iff h − βg is monotone (WWW Lemma 2(i)) — this is F2/F7 made precise. Solving (a(X) − m)/(b(X) − m) = β gives m = (a − βb)/(1−β) = ρ_β(X), Assa's Prop. 1 (with the sign convention m = ρ_β for X − m, as the author strand notes). For X ∈ 𝒳 and β ≠ 1: LR(X) = β ⇔ a(X) = β b(X) ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 (b > 0). ρ_0 = a; (a − βb)/(1−β) → b as β → ∞.

STEP 8 (mediant inequality ⇒ (A3) on same-side pairs; role of betweenness). Assume (A1) and the two-sided mediant inequality of F1 for comonotone pairs. Let X, Y ∈ 𝒳 be comonotone with LR(X), LR(Y) < 1. For β ∈ [max(LR(X),LR(Y)), 1) there are c, c' ≥ 0 with LR(X+c) = β = LR(Y+c') (each profile increases continuously from LR(·) to 1); X+c and Y+c' are comonotone, so the mediant inequality forces LR(X+Y+c+c') = β. Writing out the three Möbius identities: a(X) − βb(X) = (β−1)c, a(Y) − βb(Y) = (β−1)c', a(X+Y) − βb(X+Y) = (β−1)(c+c'); subtracting, a(X+Y) − a(X) − a(Y) = β[b(X+Y) − b(X) − b(Y)] for every β in an interval, hence both brackets vanish. The same works for LR(X), LR(Y) > 1 (profiles decrease to 1). Thus in the one-sided regimes (h ≤ g, LR ≤ 1: 'actuarial regime'; h ≥ g: 'conic regime') the ordinal betweenness/mediant property of F1 plus the Möbius cash axiom (A1) already yield the cardinal axiom (A3) away from the fair set; (A1) is precisely the cardinalization that Chew's weighted utility (D[1]), Dekel's implicit betweenness (D[2]) and Fishburn's transitive SSB theorem (D[3]) lack (they give LR = ψ(ρ/Π) with (ρ,Π) only projectively unique), and the premium weights b(X)/(b(X)+b(Y)) are the Sincov/Aczél odds of D[8]. Pairs straddling 1 and pairs on N are not covered by this argument, which is why (A3) is stated as the axiom.

STEP 9 (supplements (F)). WWW Thm 3 (C[4]) for I_{h−βg}: concave distortion ⇔ convex ⇔ quasi-convex ⇔ subadditive; since ρ_β(X+c) = ρ_β(X) + c, every sublevel set of ρ_β is a translate of {ρ_β ≤ 0}, so convexity of the extended sublevel set {LR ≤ β} ⇔ h − βg concave (Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang Prop. 2.7, S[7], gives quasi-convex ⇔ convex directly because ρ_β(1) − ρ_β(0) = 1 ≠ 0). Letting β run over [0,∞): h concave and g convex (β = 0 and β → ∞). Ordering: LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⇔ C_h ≤ C_g on 𝒳 ⇔ (cash-additive extension) I_h ≤ I_g on L^∞ ⇔ h ≤ g (WWW Lemma 1, Wang–Wang–Wei Prop. 2(i)). Coherent case: Kusuoka Thm 7 / JST Thm 2.3 / Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 (K[2], K[5]) and Shapiro Thm 3.1 (K[11]) for uniqueness of the spectral measures.
MINIMALITY: 1. (A1) Möbius/unit-cash-additive break-even structure is necessary and is the cardinal normalization. Counterexample: LR_2(X) := (C_h(X)/C_g(X))^2 for a WYP pair with h ≠ g. LR_2 is law-invariant, scale-invariant, Lipschitz on {C_g ≥ δ}, satisfies translation-to-break-even and the two-sided mediant INEQUALITY (a monotone transform preserves it), but its cash profile ((a+c)/(b+c))^2 is a degree-2 rational function of c, not a Möbius map, so no break-even pair exists and LR_2 is not a ratio of two Choquet integrals (if it were, Step 0 would force a Möbius profile). More generally every ordinal transform ψ(C_h/C_g), ψ ≠ id, satisfies all LR-level properties of F1 except (A1): (A1) is exactly what pins down 'the' loss ratio among its monotone transforms (the gap left open by Chew/Dekel/Fishburn). Weakening (A1) to slopes κ_a, κ_b > 0 (a(X+c) = a(X) + κ_a c) enlarges the class to λ·C_h/C_g, λ = κ_a/κ_b > 0, and destroys uniqueness and LR(1) = 1; dropping cash additivity altogether but keeping (A3) still gives a(X+c) = a(X) + c·a(1) (constants are comonotone with everything, rational homogeneity from additivity, reals by (A4)), so (A1) is really only the normalization a(1) = b(1) = 1, i.e. 'no loading on sure losses' (Gerber (iii), WYP H(1) = 1).
2. (A2) law invariance is necessary. Counterexample: LR(X) = E_P[X]/E_Q[X] for an equivalent Q ≠ P (or any ratio of Choquet integrals w.r.t. two non-distorted capacities): (A1), (A3), (A4-m) hold, (A2) fails, and the representation is by capacities ν_1, ν_2 (Schmeidler C[1]; CMMM Cor. 18, C[2]) that are not of the form h∘P. (A2) is exactly what converts capacities into distortions of P (C[7]).
3. (A3) premium-weighted comonotone averaging (equivalently comonotone additivity of a and b) is necessary, and the mediant INEQUALITY alone does not replace it. Counterexample: a := max(C_{h_1}, C_{h_2}), b := C_g with h_1 ≠ h_2 nondecreasing normalized and LR := a/b. Then (A1) (max of two unit-cash-additive maps is unit-cash-additive), (A2), (A4-m) hold, b is comonotone additive, but a is not: for nested layers 1_B + 1_D with h_1(p) > h_2(p), h_1(q) < h_2(q), a(1_B + 1_D) = max_i(h_i(p) + h_i(q)) < max_i h_i(p) + max_i h_i(q). Since (a,b) is unique off N (Step 0) no other break-even pair can rescue it, and LR is not a ratio of Choquet integrals. This example also violates the mediant inequality on pairs with equal ratios and different maximizers (LR(X+Y) < LR(X) = LR(Y)), consistent with Step 8; an example satisfying the inequality but not the averaging would have to live on pairs straddling break-even or on the fair set — I could not construct one, and I conjecture that under (A1), (A2), (A4) the inequality form suffices (open).
4. (A4-c) uniform continuity is necessary in the signed tier, in the precise sense that 'uniform' cannot be weakened. Counterexample: h(t) := t + (t/2) sin(π/t) (h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1, continuous, of unbounded variation near 0), g(t) = t; a := ∫_0^∞ h(P(X > x))dx is finite on L^∞, law-invariant, comonotonic-additive (the Lebesgue–Stieltjes layer argument needs only bounded measurable h), unit-cash-additive; (A1)–(A3) hold for LR := a/E[X], but a is not uniformly continuous (Lipschitz constant TV(h) = ∞; WWW Remark 3: for positively homogeneous functionals uniform continuity ⇔ Lipschitz) and the conclusion fails: h ∉ H, so a is not a signed Choquet integral in the sense of (II) (uniqueness h(p) = a(1_B) shows no BV distortion represents it). Thus (A4-c) is exactly the bounded-variation requirement; by CMMM 2012 Thm 13/Lemma 14 (C[2]) and CMMM 2015 Lemma 18 (C[3]) it may be replaced by 'a and b are of bounded variation (= differences of two monotone comonotone-additive functionals)', which is the intrinsic substitute for monotonicity.
5. (A4-m) monotonicity cannot be derived from any LR-level axiom (F2) and is needed for tier (B). Counterexample: h(t) = t + 2t(1−t) (h(1) = 1, h'(t) = 3 − 4t < 0 near 1, so h is not monotone and a = I_h is not monotone), g(t) = t: LR = I_h/E satisfies (A1)–(A3), (A4-c), is not in the WYP class, and no property of LR distinguishes it from a WYP ratio (the LR-level axioms are identical). Conversely pointwise monotonicity of LR cannot be used at all: by F2 (author strand: 1_B ≤ 1_B + 1_D ≤ 2·1_B forces h/g constant) it holds only when h = g.
6. b > 0 (g > 0 on (0,1]) is needed for LR to be finite; dropping it (e.g. g = 1_{(α,1]}, VaR-type premium) gives LR(1_B) = +∞ for P(B) ≤ α; the theorem survives on {b ≠ 0} with LR valued in [−∞, ∞] but the Möbius-profile uniqueness and F3 then need b(X) ≠ 0 case distinctions.
7. Atomlessness is needed for g(p) = b(1_B) to be defined for all p and for uniqueness (on spaces with atoms the representation is by capacities and LR only sees h, g on the attainable set of probabilities).
8. Not needed (redundant) and therefore omitted: scale invariance (Step 3), continuity in tier (B) (Step 5), any Fatou/weak* axiom (Drapeau–Kupper–Reda Prop. 2.2, A[5]: law-invariant norm-continuous functionals have it automatically), quasi-convexity, and any hypothesis on the intermediate members ρ_β.
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) Fair-set gap: on N = {LR = 1} the break-even pair is invisible to LR, so (A1)–(A4) are existential ('LR admits a pair with ...'); the theorem shows the pair is unique whenever LR ≢ 1, but an axiom system with every quantifier on LR values alone would need a density/continuity argument on N that I have only sketched (if N has empty interior and (A4-c) holds, (a,b) on N is the continuous extension from 𝒳 \\ N, but comonotone additivity across N then needs comonotone approximations I have not verified). (2) The theorem is 'easy modulo WWW Thm 1 and Schmeidler': its contribution is the exact bookkeeping of what is on LR (Möbius cash profile, law invariance, premium-weighted comonotone averaging) versus on the pair (monotonicity of the endpoints, b > 0, values on N); no new representation theorem is proved. (3) In my own strand the primary sources (Wang–Young–Panjer 1997, Wang 1996, Furman–Zitikis 2008, Gerber 1974, Bellini–Rosazza Gianin 2008) could not be opened (WebSearch disabled, publisher hosts blocked) and are quoted from memory; the theorem however only relies on WWW 2020 Thm 1/Lemmas 1,2,4/Thm 3 (read in full by three other scholars from the author PDF) and on Schmeidler 1986 (read verbatim as CMMM 2015 Thm 2), so the insurance references serve interpretation, not the proof. (4) The signed tier's continuity axiom is a sup-norm (vector-lattice) axiom; I could not find or prove a Greco/Wang–Young–Panjer-type truncation-continuity version on the nonnegative cone for the non-monotone members — it is not needed here because continuity is only imposed on the two endpoints and transported through the cash-additive extension, but a theorem with Greco-type continuity on a and b in tier (A) remains open. (5) The mediant inequality (F1) is shown to imply (A3) only on comonotone pairs strictly on the same side of break-even (Step 8); whether F1 + (A1) + (A2) + (A4) suffice globally is open (I conjecture yes in the one-sided regimes h ≤ g or h ≥ g). (6) Supplement (F) on convex sublevel sets is proved for the extended LR on L^∞; on 𝒳 only the 'if' direction is immediate (the 'only if' needs the WWW test variables shifted into L^∞_+, plausible but unchecked), and the quasi-convexity statement at β = 1 is delicate (Liu et al. Example 2.5). (7) The result is confined to bounded losses; the L^p extension via Wang–Wang–Wei ASTIN Thm 1 requires 'continuity at infinity' of a and b, an axiom I have not translated into an LR-level statement. (8) b > 0 excludes VaR-type premium principles; a version allowing LR = +∞ is routine but not written out. (9) The elicitability/identifiability side (F6) is not addressed here beyond noting, with the elicitability strand, that V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) is a functional-level, not pointwise, identification unless h and g are linear.

=== PROPOSAL 2: Zero-pole (Möbius) characterization of the loss ratio as a ratio of two normalized signed Choquet integrals, with the distortion (Yaari / Wang-Young-Panjer) pair as the monotone sub-case (Choquet/Schmeidler scholar (comonotonic additivity, Choquet and signed Choquet integrals, Greco/Denneberg/Yaari representations)) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L∞ = L∞(Ω,F,P) with the sup norm; X := L∞_+ minus {0} is Assa's domain of bounded nonnegative losses. H := {h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0}; H_1 := {h in H : h(1)=1}. For h in H the signed Choquet integral (survival form) is I_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{-∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1))dx (Wang-Wei-Willmot [4], Def. 1 and Remark 1); for nondecreasing h with h(1)=1 and X ≥ 0 this is C_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx.

Extended loss ratio. As in Assa's Prop. 1 (which evaluates LR(X − m)), LR must be defined on cash-translates of losses; we therefore take LR : L∞ → E := R ∪ {∞, ⋆}, where the two symbols are the two natural ratio conventions t/0 = ∞ for t ≠ 0 ('risk against zero premium') and 0/0 = ⋆ ('no risk, no premium: ratio undefined'). Only LR is given; no ρ, no Π, no family is part of the data. The canonical object LR = I_h/I_g read with these conventions is a map L∞ → E; restricted to X with g nondecreasing and g > 0 on (0,1] it is real-valued and equals Assa's ρ/Π.

Derived objects (functions of LR only). For X in L∞ the cash profile is m ↦ LR(X − m), m in R. If the profile is a Möbius map (axiom A1 below), its ZERO a(X) := the unique m with LR(X − m) in {0, ⋆} and its POLE b(X) := the unique m with LR(X − m) in {∞, ⋆} are well defined for every X, including the fair set N := {X : the profile takes the value 1 for all m ≠ a(X)} = {a = b}; this is exactly what the symbol ⋆ buys (on N the profile is ≡ 1 off m = a(X) and equals ⋆ there, so the common zero/pole is still read off). Interpretation: a(X) is the risk capital of X (the cash m with LR(X − m) = 0), b(X) is its premium (the cash m at which the premium of X − m vanishes). The associated family is m_β(X) := the unique m with LR(X − m) = β, β in B := [0,∞) minus {1}, which exists iff X is not in N; with Assa's sign convention X − m one has m_β = ρ_β. The comonotone mediant/betweenness property of F1 appears as a consequence (strict, premium-weighted form) and, in its equality form, as an equivalent reformulation of axiom A4 off N.
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A1) [Möbius cash profile; unit-slope ratio axiom] For every X in L∞ there exist real numbers a_X, b_X such that LR(X − m) = (a_X − m)/(b_X − m) for all m in R, read with the conventions t/0 = ∞ (t ≠ 0) and 0/0 = ⋆. (The pair (a_X,b_X) is then automatically unique: if a_X ≠ b_X the profile takes the value 0 only at m = a_X and the value ∞ only at m = b_X and never ⋆; if a_X = b_X it equals 1 everywhere except ⋆ at m = a_X. So a, b : L∞ → R are functions of LR. Interpretation: a sure loss m is carried at face value m by both risk and premium — this is Assa's 'translation to break-even' (F1) made quantitative, and it is the cardinal form of Chew's weak-substitution/Dekel betweenness structure, see proof.) (A2) (A3) [law invariance] X and Y equal in distribution implies LR(X) = LR(Y). (Applied to translates it gives a(X)=a(Y), b(X)=b(Y).) (A3) (A4) [comonotonic additivity of risk capital and premium] For comonotonic X, Y in L∞: a(X+Y) = a(X) + a(Y) and b(X+Y) = b(X) + b(Y), where a, b are the zero and pole of the profile (A1). Equivalent formulation on the values of LR, valid off the fair set N: 'comonotone break-even additivity' — if X, Y are comonotonic and LR(X − m) = LR(Y − m') = β for some β in R, then LR(X + Y − m − m') = β (take β = 0 for a and β = 2 for b; conversely this is the equality case of the mediant property). On N the a/b form is the honest statement (see weaknesses). (A4) (A5) [continuity] a and b are uniformly continuous with respect to the sup norm on L∞. EQUIVALENT replacements, each giving the same theorem: (A5-bv) a and b are of bounded variation in the sense of Cerreia-Vioglio et al. [2],[3] (sup over chains 0 = X_0 ≤ … ≤ X_n = X of Σ|a(X_i) − a(X_{i−1})| < ∞); (A5-Lip) a, b sup-norm Lipschitz. STRONGER monotone variant (A5') [endpoint monotonicity]: X ≤ Y a.s. implies a(X) ≤ a(Y) and b(X) ≤ b(Y); (A5') implies (A5) (proof, step 4) and selects the distortion class in part (II). (A5) NOT an axiom (it is a theorem here): scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X), λ > 0, follows from (A1)+(A4)+(A5) (rational homogeneity of a, b from comonotonic additivity, real homogeneity from continuity). Likewise cash-additivity of a, b, the normalization LR(c) = 1 for constants c ≠ 0, LR(0) = ⋆, and the comonotone mediant property are consequences, not hypotheses.
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: None formally: every hypothesis of the theorem is stated on LR as a map L∞ → R ∪ {∞,⋆}, because the zero a and the pole b of the cash profile are functions of LR. Substantively, (A1) encodes 'LR is the ratio of two cash-additive functionals normalized at ρ(1) = Π(1) = 1' and (A5') encodes 'numerator and denominator are monotone'; by F2 the latter cannot be expressed as a pointwise order property of the VALUES of LR, so in the monotone part (II) the monotonicity is a property of the two endpoints ρ = ρ_0 = a and Π = lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = b of the associated family, never of LR or of an intermediate ρ_β. | If LR is given ONLY on Assa's domain X = L∞_+ minus {0} (nonnegative cash shifts X + c, c ≥ 0, only), then the premium b(X) = Π(X) on the fair set N ∩ X = {ρ(X) = Π(X)} is not a function of LR (the profile c ↦ LR(X + c) is identically 1 there) and must be supplied as pair-level data; everything else in the theorem is then intrinsic, and by F3 the pair (h,g) is still identified from LR on X unless LR ≡ 1 on X.
CONCLUSION: THEOREM. Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless and LR : L∞ → R ∪ {∞,⋆}.

(I) [signed Choquet pair] LR satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4), (A5) IF AND ONLY IF there exist h, g in H_1 (bounded variation, h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1) such that
   LR(X) = I_h(X) / I_g(X) for all X in L∞ (conventions t/0 = ∞, 0/0 = ⋆),
i.e. LR = ρ/Π with ρ := I_h, Π := I_g a pair of normalized signed Choquet integrals (law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, sup-norm Lipschitz, NOT necessarily monotone). In that case a = I_h, b = I_g, N = {I_h = I_g}, and the associated family m_β = ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1 − β) = I_{(h − βg)/(1−β)} (β ≠ 1) is a pencil of signed Choquet integrals with distortions on the affine line g + (h − g)/(1 − β); ρ_0 = ρ, ρ_β → Π as β → ∞, and LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0 for every X outside the ⋆-locus {I_h = I_g = 0} (Assa's association, F6, F7).

(II) [distortion pair — Assa's setting] LR satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4), (A5') IF AND ONLY IF the representation in (I) holds with h, g NONDECREASING distortion functions [0,1] → [0,1]; then ρ = C_h is a distortion risk measure and Π = C_g a Wang-Young-Panjer/Yaari premium. Moreover Π > 0 on X (Assa's standing assumption) ⟺ g(p) > 0 for all p in (0,1], and then LR is real-valued on X and equals C_h(X)/C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx.

UNIQUENESS. In (I) and (II) the pair (h,g) in H_1 × H_1 is unique: h(t) = a(1_A), g(t) = b(1_A) for any A with P(A) = t. Without the normalization h(1) = g(1) = 1 the pair is unique only up to a common nonzero scalar (I_{ch}/I_{cg} = I_h/I_g), and (A1) is precisely what fixes the scalar. On the extended domain the F3 exception 'LR ≡ 1' disappears (the ⋆-locus {I_h = I_g = 0} determines h); on X alone, F3 holds as stated: LR|_X determines (h,g) unless LR ≡ 1 on X.

SUPPLEMENTS (each an iff within the class of (I), all proved from [4]). (a) Ordering: LR ≤ 1 on X ⟺ h ≤ g on [0,1]; LR ≡ 1 on X ⟺ h = g ([4] Lemma 1). (b) Premium-weighted mediant: for comonotonic X, Y with Π(X), Π(Y) > 0, LR(X+Y) = w LR(X) + (1 − w) LR(Y), w = Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)); in particular F1's mediant inequality, with strict betweenness. (c) Monotonicity of members of the family: ρ_β is monotone ⟺ h − βg is monotone (β < 1) resp. βg − h monotone (β > 1) ([4] Lemma 2(i)); LR is never pointwise monotone on X unless h = g (F2). (d) Convexity of level sets on X: for β > 1, {LR ≤ β} ∩ X is convex ⟺ h − βg is concave; for β < 1, {LR ≥ β} ∩ X is convex ⟺ h − βg is convex. Consequently, in the 'conic regime' h ≥ g (LR ≥ 1 on X): LR is quasi-convex on X ⟺ g is convex and h − g is concave (sufficient: h concave and g convex); in the 'actuarial regime' h ≤ g (LR ≤ 1 on X): LR is quasi-concave on X ⟺ h is convex and g − h is concave (for ρ = E: ⟺ g concave). This corrects F4's second clause: ρ = E with g concave gives a quasi-CONCAVE loss ratio. (e) Coherence of the endpoints: ρ (resp. Π) is subadditive/convex ⟺ h (resp. g) concave ([4] Thm 3, [5] Cor. 1), giving the Kusuoka-type pair ρ = ∫ES_α dμ_ρ, Π = ∫ES_α dμ_Π ([5] Thm 5) as a sub-class. (f) Variants: dropping (A3) and replacing (A5) by 'bounded variation + outer continuity' on B(Σ) gives LR = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for two signed set functions of bounded variation ([2] Cor. 18, [11] Thm 4.2, [3] Thm 6 on L∞ as a Riesz space); replacing L∞ by a law-invariant convex cone containing L∞ and adding 'continuity at infinity' gives the same theorem for unbounded losses ([5] Thm 1).
PROOF SKETCH: NOTATION. a, b : L∞ → R denote the zero and pole of the cash profile (A1). All citations [k] refer to the Choquet/Schmeidler scholar's digest list.

PART (⇐) of (I). Let h, g in H_1 and set a := I_h, b := I_g, LR := a/b with the ratio conventions.
Step 1 (A1). By [4] Lemma 2(ii), I_h(X − m) = I_h(X) − m h(1) = a(X) − m and I_g(X − m) = b(X) − m, hence LR(X − m) = (a(X) − m)/(b(X) − m) for all m, with the right conventions when the denominator (and possibly the numerator) vanishes. The zero/pole of this profile are a(X), b(X) by the uniqueness remark in (A1).
Step 2 (A3). I_h and I_g are law-invariant by construction (they depend on X only through x ↦ P(X > x)).
Step 3 (A4). Signed Choquet integrals are comonotonic-additive: [4] Theorem 1 ('if' half), equivalently the classical fact that the Choquet integral w.r.t. any set function is comonotonic additive ([1] Schmeidler 1986, [11] Murofushi-Sugeno-Machida Thm 4.2, [2] Theorem 13 (2)⇒(1)), applied to the signed set function (h∘P).
Step 4 (A5). [4] Lemma 4: |I_h(X) − I_h(Y)| ≤ TV(h) ‖X − Y‖_∞ (same for g; cf. [5] Remark 2), so a, b are Lipschitz, hence uniformly continuous and of bounded variation ([2] Lemma 14(iii) / [3] Lemma 18 give the converse implications among the three (A5) variants).
Step 5 (⇐ of (II)). If h, g are nondecreasing, [4] Lemma 2(i) (also [5] Prop. 2(ii)) gives a = I_h and b = I_g monotone, i.e. (A5'). For X in X and g nondecreasing with g > 0 on (0,1], P(X > x) > 0 on [0, ess sup X) so I_g(X) > 0; conversely g(p) = 0 for some p in (0,1] gives I_g(1_A) = 0 with P(A) = p (atomless). Hence LR is real on X and equals C_h/C_g.

PART (⇒) of (I). Assume (A1), (A3), (A4), (A5).
Step 0 (definition). By (A1) and its uniqueness remark, a(X) and b(X) are well defined for every X in L∞ (on N both equal the unique m with LR(X − m) = ⋆).
Step 1 (cash additivity). The profile of X + c is m ↦ LR(X − (m − c)); its zero is a(X) + c and its pole b(X) + c. Hence a(X + c) = a(X) + c and b(X + c) = b(X) + c for all c in R.
Step 2 (normalization). 0 is comonotonic with itself, so (A4) gives a(0) = 2a(0), i.e. a(0) = 0, and b(0) = 0. With Step 1, a(c) = b(c) = c for constants; in particular a(1) = b(1) = 1, LR(c) = c/c = 1 for c ≠ 0 and LR(0) = ⋆. (So 'translation to break-even' and the normalization are theorems.)
Step 3 (law invariance). If X =_d Y then X − m =_d Y − m for every m, so by (A3) the profiles coincide and a(X) = a(Y), b(X) = b(Y).
Step 4 (comonotonic additivity, continuity, homogeneity). (A4) is comonotonic additivity of a and b. (A5) is their uniform continuity. Under (A5') instead: X ≤ Y + ‖X − Y‖_∞ gives a(X) ≤ a(Y + ‖X−Y‖_∞) = a(Y) + ‖X − Y‖_∞ by Step 1, so a, b are 1-Lipschitz (this is [3] Prop. 16(iii) / Denneberg's Lipschitz lemma [10]); hence (A5') ⇒ (A5). Positive homogeneity: X is comonotonic with itself, so a(nX) = n a(X) and a(X/n) = a(X)/n, giving a(qX) = q a(X) for rational q > 0, and continuity extends this to all λ > 0. Consequently LR(λX) = a(λX)/b(λX) = LR(X): scale invariance is derived, not assumed.
Step 5 (representation). a, b : L∞ → R are law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly sup-norm continuous on an atomless space. By [4] Theorem 1 (the signed, non-monotone Schmeidler theorem; alternatively [3] Theorem 19 / [2] Theorem 13 for the bounded-variation form, plus [7] Prop. 2(i) to pass from a signed set function to h∘P under law invariance) there exist h, g in H with a = I_h, b = I_g. By Step 2 and [4] Lemma 2(ii), h(1) = I_h(1) = a(1) = 1 and g(1) = 1, so h, g in H_1. Uniqueness: for P(A) = t, I_h(1_A) = h(t), so h(t) = a(1_A) is determined by LR; this is [4] Lemma 1.
Step 6 (conclusion). LR(X) = LR(X − 0) = (a(X) − 0)/(b(X) − 0) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) with the conventions. This proves (I).
Step 7 (⇒ of (II)). Under (A5'), a = I_h and b = I_g are monotone, so h and g are nondecreasing by [4] Lemma 2(i); with h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1 they are distortion functions [0,1] → [0,1]. The equivalence 'Π > 0 on X ⟺ g > 0 on (0,1]' was shown in Step 5 of (⇐).
Step 8 (the associated family and the non-monotonicity of ρ_β, F2/F7). For X not in N and β ≠ 1, LR(X − m) = β ⟺ (a(X) − m) = β(b(X) − m) ⟺ m = (a(X) − βb(X))/(1 − β) =: m_β(X). By linearity of h ↦ I_h, m_β = I_{(h − βg)/(1−β)}: a signed Choquet integral with distortion of bounded variation and value 1 at 1 (cash-additive, positively homogeneous, law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, Lipschitz with constant ≤ (TV(h) + βTV(g))/|1 − β|). Thus the whole family is covered by [4] Theorem 1 without any monotonicity: by [4] Lemma 2(i), ρ_β is monotone iff h − βg is monotone (β < 1), which fails for β close to 1 whenever h ≠ g; this is the precise content of F2 and is why only the ENDPOINTS ρ_0 = a and lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = b carry a monotonicity axiom. The association LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0 holds for every X outside the ⋆-locus. The family is a pencil (affine in β after multiplication by 1 − β): this is exactly what (A1) adds to a Dekel-type abstract association, in the same way that Chew's weak substitution turns Dekel's implicit utility into weighted utility; the betweenness/mediant property alone would only give LR = φ(ρ/Π) with φ unknown and (ρ,Π) only projectively determined per comonotone cone (counterexample LR = (I_h/I_g)^3 below).

PROOF OF THE SUPPLEMENTS. (a) [4] Lemma 1 with h(1) = g(1): h ≤ g ⟺ I_h ≤ I_g on L∞; on X with I_g > 0 this is LR ≤ 1 (and conversely the restriction to X suffices since Bernoulli losses 1_A give h(t)/g(t)). (b) a(X+Y) = a(X)+a(Y), b(X+Y) = b(X)+b(Y) for comonotonic X, Y, divide. (c) [4] Lemma 2(i) applied to (h − βg)/(1 − β). (d) Fix β > 1 and k := h − βg, so I_k(X + M) = I_k(X) − (β − 1)M ([4] Lemma 2(ii)) and {LR ≤ β} ∩ X = {I_k ≤ 0} ∩ X. Suppose this set is convex. Take X, Y ≥ 0 with I_k(X), I_k(Y) ≤ c; if c ≤ 0 they lie in the set, otherwise X + M, Y + M do for M := c/(β−1); convexity gives I_k((X+Y)/2 + M) ≤ 0, i.e. I_k((X+Y)/2) ≤ c. So I_k is quasi-convex on L∞_+ (X = 0 is harmless by homogeneity); by cash-additivity, shifting an arbitrary pair in L∞ by a common constant shows I_k is quasi-convex on all of L∞, hence convex and k concave by [4] Theorem 3 (v)⇒(i) (alternatively [6] Prop. 2.7, since I_k(1) − I_k(0) = 1 − β ≠ 0). Conversely k concave ⇒ I_k convex ⇒ {I_k ≤ 0} convex. For β < 1 apply the same argument to −I_k = I_{βg − h} and the superlevel set {LR ≥ β} ∩ X = {I_k ≥ 0} ∩ X, now using I_k(X + M) = I_k(X) + (1 − β)M. The regime statements: if h ≥ g, sublevel sets with β < 1 are empty and {LR ≤ 1} ∩ X = {I_{h−g} = 0} ∩ X = {I_{h−g} ≤ 0} ∩ X (since I_{h−g} ≥ 0 on X by (a)), convex when h − g is concave; and h − βg = (h − g) − (β − 1)g is concave for all β > 1 iff h − g concave and g convex (β ↓ 1 and β → ∞). The actuarial regime is symmetric with h − βg = (1 − β)h + β(h − g). (e) [4] Theorem 3 (i)⟺(iii)⟺(iv) for I_h and I_g, and [5] Theorem 5 / Corollary 1 for the ES-mixture form. (f) [2] Corollary 18 and [3] Theorem 6 replace Step 5 when law invariance is dropped; [5] Theorem 1 replaces it on larger domains.
MINIMALITY: Each axiom is shown independent of the others by an example that satisfies the remaining ones but is not a ratio of normalized signed Choquet integrals (uniqueness of the zero/pole functionals is used throughout: in ANY representation LR = I_{h'}/I_{g'} the profile's zero and pole are I_{h'}/h'(1) and I_{g'}/g'(1), which are comonotonic-additive, law-invariant and Lipschitz).

(A1) is needed and is the 'cardinal' axiom. Take h ≠ g in H_1 and LR := (I_h/I_g)^3 (with 0, ∞, ⋆ fixed). Its zero and pole are still I_h, I_g, so (A3), (A4), (A5) hold, LR is scale- and law-invariant and satisfies the comonotone mediant/betweenness property (an increasing transform preserves betweenness). But LR is not a ratio of signed Choquet integrals: a representation I_{h'}/I_{g'} would have the same zero and pole, forcing (a − m)^3/(b − m)^3 = (a − m)/(b − m) for all m, impossible once a ≠ b for some X. This is exactly the Chew/Fishburn projective indeterminacy: betweenness gives LR = φ(ρ/Π) only. A second example shows the unit-slope normalization inside (A1) is needed: LR := I_h/I_{g'} with g'(1) = 2 has profile (a − m)/(2b' − 2m)... more precisely (a − m)/(I_{g'}(X) − 2m), a Möbius map that is not of the form (a − m)/(b − m); it satisfies everything else but has Π(c) = 2c for sure losses, outside the class H_1 × H_1.

(A3) is needed. Let Z be a non-constant density (Z ≥ 0, E[Z] = 1) and LR(X) := E[ZX]/E[X]. The zero a(X) = E[ZX] and pole b(X) = E[X] are linear, hence comonotonic-additive, cash-additive, 1-Lipschitz in L∞, so (A1), (A4), (A5) hold; but a(1_B) = E[Z 1_B] depends on B and not only on P(B), so a is not of the form I_h and LR is not a ratio of signed Choquet integrals. Without (A3) the correct conclusion is [2] Cor. 18 / [11]: LR = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for signed set functions of bounded variation.

(A4) is needed. LR(X) := E[X]/(E[X] + λ SD(X)), λ > 0. The zero E[X] and the pole E[X] + λ SD(X) are cash-additive (unit slope), law-invariant, positively homogeneous, and SD is 1-Lipschitz in the sup norm, so (A1), (A3), (A5) hold; but SD is not comonotonic additive (U uniform: SD(U + U²) < SD(U) + SD(U²) since U and U² are not affinely related), and the pole functional of any signed-Choquet representation would have to be comonotonic additive. Hence (A4) cannot be dropped; by (b) it can be replaced, off N, by the equality case of the mediant property plus the two-level break-even additivity.

(A5) is needed, with an honest caveat. On the domain of simple random variables, comonotonic additivity and law invariance force a(X) = Σ c_i h(P(A_i)) along the layer decomposition X = Σ c_i 1_{A_i} (nested A_i), for an ARBITRARY function h with h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1, e.g. h(t) = t·1_{t rational}: such a is comonotonic-additive, law-invariant, cash-additive, positively homogeneous but discontinuous, and LR := a/E is not a ratio of signed Choquet integrals on simple losses. I cannot exhibit an explicit extension of such an a to all of L∞ (it is a Hamel-type question), but [4] Remark 3 shows that already weakening (A5) to mere norm-continuity leaves the class: the representation then holds with an h NOT of bounded variation, i.e. not a signed Choquet integral. So (A5) (or bounded variation, [2],[3]) is the exact substitute for monotonicity that the non-monotone family demands.

(A5') is needed for part (II) and cannot be replaced by any order property of LR. Take h with a decreasing piece (e.g. h(t) = t + ε·sin(2πt), small ε, h in H_1 but not monotone) and g(t) = t: LR = I_h/E satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4), (A5) but ρ = I_h is not monotone, so (II) fails. By F2 (re-proved here: 1_B ≤ 1_B + 1_D ≤ 2·1_B for D ⊂ B, scale invariance and pointwise monotonicity of LR force h/g constant, hence h = g), no pointwise monotonicity of LR on X can be imposed; the monotonicity has to be placed on the endpoints a = ρ_0 and b = ρ_∞, and only there (Step 8 shows intermediate members are non-monotone as soon as h ≠ g).

Atomlessness is needed: [4] Theorem 1 and the identification h(t) = a(1_A), P(A) = t, both use it; on a space with atoms a law-invariant comonotonic-additive functional need not be of the form I_h (and the class of attainable t is not [0,1]).

Scale invariance, cash-additivity, the normalization LR(c) = 1, the mediant property and continuity of LR along translates are NOT needed as axioms: all are consequences (proof, Steps 1, 2, 4 and supplement (b)).
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: 1. Extended domain and the symbol ⋆. The theorem is stated for LR on all of L∞ with the two ratio conventions ∞ and ⋆. The symbol ∞ is unavoidable (zero premium); the symbol ⋆ is a device that makes the zero and the pole definable pointwise from LR on the fair set N = {ρ = Π}. Without it, b on N must either be supplied as pair-level data (as I state) or recovered by a density/continuity argument (N has empty interior, a and b uniformly continuous off N, comonotonic additivity and law invariance passed to the closure) which I have NOT completed rigorously: the approximation of comonotone pairs and of equidistributed pairs by pairs off N is the technical gap. On Assa's domain X with only nonnegative cash shifts, Π on N ∩ X is genuinely invisible to LR.

2. Strength of (A1). The Möbius axiom is close to saying 'LR is a ratio of two cash-additive functionals'. It is nevertheless a one-parameter, checkable property of LR with an actuarial meaning (sure losses carried at face value by both sides), it is exactly what Assa's Prop. 1 (cash-additivity of every ρ_β) asserts read backwards, and the cube example shows that the weaker betweenness/mediant property is provably insufficient (Chew/Dekel/Fishburn ordinal indeterminacy). A theorem with betweenness only would characterize LR = φ(ρ/Π) and, per comonotone cone, (ρ,Π) up to the linear-fractional group; I did not pursue the Sincov-type reconstruction of the decision-theory strand, which needs its own non-degeneracy axioms.

3. (A5) counterexample on all of L∞ not exhibited (only on simple losses and via [4] Remark 3); the necessity claim for (A5) is therefore 'cannot be weakened to norm-continuity' rather than a full independence proof.

4. Supplement (d): the convexity iff is proved for {LR ≤ β} ∩ X with β > 1 and {LR ≥ β} ∩ X with β < 1; convexity of {LR ≤ β} ∩ X for β < 1 and of {LR ≥ β} ∩ X for β > 1 is only shown to follow from concavity/convexity of h − βg, not conversely (the cash-shift argument moves in the wrong direction on the cone X). Hence the global quasi-convexity iff is clean only in the regimes h ≥ g or h ≤ g.

5. Domain L∞ only; the L^p / Orlicz extension via [5] Theorem 1 (continuity at infinity + uniform sup-continuity) is stated without proof, and Shapiro's warning that comonotonic ⟺ single generator fails at p = ∞ is irrelevant here but the integrability of signed Choquet integrals on L^p ([5] Prop. 1, [4]/[6] H_q classes) would have to be added.

6. Source confidence. [4] (Wang-Wei-Willmot 2020: Thm 1, Lemmas 1, 2, 4, Thm 3), [5], [6], [2], [3] were read in full; [1] Schmeidler only as restated in [3]; [7] read; [9] Wang-Young-Panjer, [10] Greco/Denneberg and [11] Murofushi-Sugeno-Machida are quoted from memory (medium confidence), but the proof uses them only as alternatives to [4]. The statement '(A5-bv) ⟺ (A5)' relies on [2] Lemma 14(iii)/[3] Lemma 18 (bounded variation ⇒ Lipschitz) and on the Jordan decomposition in [4] (Lipschitz signed Choquet ⇒ difference of two monotone ones ⇒ bounded variation); I did not re-verify the exact equivalence on L∞ beyond these citations.

7. Elicitability/identification: F6 is downgraded, consistent with the elicitability strand — V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) is a functional-level zero-crossing, not a Nolde-Ziegel identification function unless h, g are linear; nothing in the present theorem addresses this.

=== PROPOSAL 3: Möbius–Choquet pencil theorem: a loss ratio is a (signed) Choquet ratio iff its cash profile is a Möbius map, it is law-invariant, premium-weighted comonotone-mediant, and its two break-even cash amounts are uniformly continuous (resp. monotone) (Signed Choquet / Ruodu Wang strand (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 MOR; Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 ASTIN; Wang–Wei 2020 MF; Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang 2020; Han–Lin–Wang 2025; Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei 2022)) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with ‖·‖_∞; 𝒳 := L^∞_+ \ {0} (bounded, non-negative, non-zero losses). Two random variables are comonotonic if (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 for a.a. (ω,ω'). H := {h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0}; for h ∈ H the signed Choquet integral (survival form, Wang–Wei–Willmot Def. 1 / Remark 1) is I_h(X) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x)) dx, X ∈ L^∞. Values: the real projective line P¹ = R ∪ {∞} together with an extra symbol ∗ ('0/0'); the projective quotient r/s is r/s ∈ R if s ≠ 0, ∞ if s = 0 ≠ r, ∗ if r = s = 0. DATA: an extended loss ratio is a map LR: L^∞ → P¹ ∪ {∗}. Its restriction to 𝒳 is Assa's loss ratio; LR(X − m) for X ∈ 𝒳, m ∈ R is the loss ratio of the cash-shifted loss, exactly the object in Assa's Prop. 1 (every X ∈ L^∞ is Y − m with Y ∈ 𝒳, so 'LR on all cash shifts of non-negative losses' = 'LR on L^∞'). D := {X : LR(X) ∈ R} (finite loss ratio). Cash profile of X: the map c ↦ LR(X + c), c ∈ R. Derived break-even functionals (defined from LR alone under (A1) below): p(X) := the unique c with LR(X+c) ∈ {∞, ∗} ('premium-exhaustion cash'), z(X) := the unique c with LR(X+c) = 0 if the cash profile is non-constant, z(X) := p(X) if the profile is identically 1 off its ∗-point; Π := −p, ρ := −z. Assa's canonical family: ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β), β ∈ [0,∞) \ {1}; the degenerate set N := {z = p} = {ρ = Π} is where Assa's association LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 is silent (the profile is ≡ 1 there).
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A1) [Möbius cash profile]  For every X ∈ L^∞ there are reals z(X), p(X) such that LR(X + c) = (c − z(X)) / (c − p(X)) for ALL c ∈ R, read in P¹ ∪ {∗} with the conventions r/0 = ∞ (r ≠ 0) and 0/0 = ∗. (Equivalently: the cash profile is a non-constant Möbius map c ↦ (c−z)/(c−p) — unique zero z, unique pole p — or is ≡ 1 except at a single ∗-point p = z. Interpretation: −p(X) is the cash that exhausts the premium, −z(X) the cash that exhausts the risk. (A1) is equivalent to 'LR = ρ/Π with ρ := −z and Π := −p cash-additive'; it is Assa's Prop. 1 read backwards and makes the pole part of the data.) (A2) (A2) [Law invariance]  LR(X) = LR(Y) (in P¹ ∪ {∗}) whenever X and Y have the same distribution. (A3) (A3) [Premium-weighted comonotone mediant]  For comonotonic X, Y ∈ D with p(X) + p(Y) ≠ 0:  LR(X + Y) = [ p(X)·LR(X) + p(Y)·LR(Y) ] / [ p(X) + p(Y) ]  (equivalently, with Π = −p: LR(X+Y) = (Π(X)LR(X) + Π(Y)LR(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)), the Π-weighted average). On 𝒳 ∩ {Π > 0} this is the exact, cardinal form of F1's mediant property: min(LR(X),LR(Y)) ≤ LR(X+Y) ≤ max(...) is its corollary when both weights are positive. Since cash shifts preserve comonotonicity, (A3) automatically holds for all shifted pairs (X+a, Y+b). (A4) (A4) [Uniform continuity of the break-even cash amounts]  X ↦ z(X) and X ↦ p(X) are uniformly continuous with respect to ‖·‖_∞ on L^∞. (Equivalently, by Lemma 4 of WWW after the fact: Lipschitz.) (A5) DERIVED, NOT ASSUMED: scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X), λ>0 (follows from (A1),(A3),(A4) or (A1),(A3),(A4')); LR(c) = 1 for c ≠ 0, LR(0) = ∗; positive homogeneity and cash-additivity of ρ, Π; the two-sided mediant inequality on comonotone pairs with positive premiums; the association LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 for X ∉ N; every ρ_β is a signed Choquet integral. No pointwise monotonicity of LR or of any ρ_β is used anywhere.
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: (A4') [Endpoint monotonicity — Part B only, replaces (A4)]  X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ z(X) ≥ z(Y) and p(X) ≥ p(Y), i.e. the risk ρ = −z and the premium Π = −p are monotone. Although z and p are functions of LR, this is in substance a hypothesis on the PAIR (ρ,Π) = (ρ_0, lim_{β→∞} ρ_β): it concerns the two endpoints of the pencil {ρ_β} and cannot be replaced by any monotonicity statement about LR itself (F2: LR = I_h/I_g is pointwise monotone on 𝒳 only if h = g). In Part A no monotonicity is imposed on anything. | (Domain datum) The theorem uses LR on all cash shifts of non-negative losses (= L^∞), including the location of the pole. This is data about LR (it is what Assa's Prop. 1 already uses), but it is what identifies the pair: on 𝒳 alone LR is invariant under (ρ,Π) ↦ (ψρ, ψΠ) for every positive functional ψ, so no axiom system on LR|_𝒳 can single out Choquet pairs. Nothing is assumed on the family {ρ_β} beyond what (A1)–(A4) imply; equivalently, (A3)+(A4) can be restated as 'ρ_0 and ρ_{β_1} (any β_1 ∉ {0,1}) are comonotonic-additive and uniformly continuous', which by affinity of the pencil is the same for two, for all, or for the endpoints (ρ,Π). | (Optional, Part C) subadditivity of z, p (⇔ ρ, Π subadditive) — again an endpoint/pair hypothesis — selects the coherent / Kusuoka sub-class.
CONCLUSION: PART A (signed-Choquet class). For LR: L^∞ → P¹ ∪ {∗} the following are equivalent:
 (I)  LR satisfies (A1), (A2), (A3), (A4).
 (II) There exist h, g ∈ H with h(1) = g(1) = 1 such that LR(X) = I_h(X) / I_g(X) (projective quotient) for every X ∈ L^∞; in particular LR(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx for X ∈ 𝒳 with I_g(X) ≠ 0.
Then: (a) the pair (h,g) is UNIQUE (h(t) = ρ(1_A), g(t) = Π(1_A) for P(A) = t; WWW Lemma 1) — unconditionally, even when LR ≡ 1 on 𝒳, because the pole datum identifies g (this sharpens F3, whose 'unless LR ≡ 1' caveat is an artefact of ignoring cash shifts); (b) z = −I_h, p = −I_g, N = {I_{h−g} = 0}; (c) Assa's family is ρ_β = I_{k_β}, k_β := (h − βg)/(1−β) = g + (h−g)/(1−β), a pencil of signed Choquet integrals whose distortions lie on the affine line through g with direction h − g; each ρ_β is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, cash-additive (k_β(1) = 1), positively homogeneous and ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz with constant TV(k_β) ≤ (TV(h) + β TV(g))/|1−β|, and is monotone iff h − βg is monotone (β<1) resp. βg − h is monotone (β>1) — the exact quantification of F2; (d) for β ≠ 1 and X ∉ N the equation LR(X − m) = β has the unique solution m = ρ_β(X) (Assa's Prop. 1, sign convention X − m), so LR and {ρ_β} are in association, LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0; on 𝒳 ∩ D one also has the level-search form LR(X) = inf{β ≥ 0 : I_{h−βg}(X) ≤ 0} with the DECREASING family V_β = I_{h−βg} (a Han–Lin–Wang DQ / Cherny–Madan-type index with non-monotone cones); (e) LR is scale invariant and LR = 1 + I_{h−g}/I_g, i.e. 'one plus a location-invariant signed Choquet variability functional over a Choquet premium' (Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei structure, a generalized Gini coefficient).
PART B (distortion class). The following are equivalent:
 (I')  LR satisfies (A1), (A2), (A3), (A4').
 (II') (II) holds with h and g NON-DECREASING, i.e. h, g: [0,1] → [0,1] are distortion functions with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, and ρ = C_h, Π = C_g are Yaari/Wang–Young–Panjer distortion functionals.
Then (A4) holds automatically (ρ_β is Lipschitz with constant (1+β)/|1−β|), LR ≥ 0 on 𝒳, and D ⊇ 𝒳 (finite loss ratio for every non-negative non-zero loss, Assa's standing assumption Π > 0) iff g > 0 on (0,1].
PART C (iff supplements inside class (II')). (c1) ρ (resp. Π) subadditive ⇔ h (resp. g) concave ⇔ ρ (resp. Π) coherent ⇔ ρ = ∫ ES_s dμ, Π = ∫ ES_s dν with unique probability measures μ, ν on (0,1] (Kusuoka-type pair); then ρ_β = (1−β)^{-1}(∫ES_s dμ − β∫ES_s dν) is a signed Kusuoka mixture. (c2) LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⇔ h ≤ g on [0,1]; LR pointwise monotone on 𝒳 ⇔ h = g ⇔ LR ≡ 1 on 𝒳 (F2). (c3) Shape: for every β ≠ 1, {X ∈ L^∞ : (1−β)ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} is convex ⇔ ρ_β is quasi-convex (β<1) / quasi-concave (β>1) ⇔ h − βg is concave; for β > 1, {LR ≤ β} ∩ 𝒳 is convex ⇔ h − βg concave, hence LR is quasi-convex on {LR > 1} ∩ 𝒳 ⇔ h − g concave and g convex; for β < 1, {LR ≥ β} ∩ 𝒳 is convex ⇔ h − βg convex, hence LR is quasi-concave on {LR < 1} ∩ 𝒳 ⇔ h convex and h − g convex (actuarial picture ρ = E, g concave: LR = E/C_g is quasi-CONCAVE, correcting F4); on the cash-extended domain, all sets {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0}, β ≥ 0, are convex ⇔ h concave and g convex (conic picture), all sets {I_{h−βg} ≥ 0} convex ⇔ h convex and g concave. (c4) Identification: V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) is a functional-level (zero-set) identification only; LR has convex level sets on discrete laws for all β if {h,g} ⊂ H1* (ess sup/ess inf mixtures) or {h,g} ⊂ H3* with a common quantile level α (e.g. LR = VaR^{c1}_{1−α}/VaR^{c2}_{1−α}); outside such pairs ρ_β leaves Wang–Wei's class H* for some β in the range and LR is neither identifiable nor elicitable in the pointwise (Nolde–Ziegel/Fissler–Ziegel) sense.
VARIANTS (same proof, different representation theorem): drop (A2) and replace (A4) by 'z, p of bounded variation and outer continuous on B(Σ)': LR = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for two signed set functions of bounded variation (CMMM 2012 Cor. 18 / Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida Thm 4.2); on L^p or a law-invariant convex cone ⊃ L^∞, add 'continuity at infinity' of z, p and use Wang–Wang–Wei (ASTIN) Thm 1.
PROOF SKETCH: Notation: strand references are to the digest; 'SC[k]' = Signed-Choquet strand item k, 'CS[k]' = Choquet/Schmeidler strand item k, 'KU[k]' = Kusuoka/coherent strand item k.

STEP 0 (z, p are well defined from LR). Under (A1) the cash profile c ↦ LR(X+c) is either a non-constant Möbius map, which has exactly one zero and exactly one pole in P¹, or ≡ 1 off exactly one ∗-point; in both cases (z(X), p(X)) is uniquely determined by LR. Hence z, p, ρ := −z, Π := −p, D = {p ≠ 0} and N = {z = p} are functions of LR.

STEP 1 ((A1) ⇒ cash-additivity and LR = ρ/Π). Evaluating (A1) at c = 0 gives LR(X) = z(X)/p(X) = ρ(X)/Π(X) (projective quotient). Comparing the profile of X + c, namely c' ↦ LR(X + c + c') = (c + c' − z(X))/(c + c' − p(X)), with its own (A1)-representation c' ↦ (c' − z(X+c))/(c' − p(X+c)) and using Step 0 gives z(X+c) = z(X) − c, p(X+c) = p(X) − c, i.e. ρ(X+c) = ρ(X) + c and Π(X+c) = Π(X) + c for all X ∈ L^∞, c ∈ R.

STEP 2 ((A2) ⇒ law invariance of ρ, Π). X =_d Y ⇒ X + c =_d Y + c ⇒ the two cash profiles coincide ⇒ (z,p)(X) = (z,p)(Y).

STEP 3 ((A3) ⇒ comonotonic additivity of ρ and Π). Fix comonotonic X, Y and write r := ρ(X)+ρ(Y), q := Π(X)+Π(Y). For every s ∈ R with s ≠ −q choose a ≠ p(X), b := s − a ≠ p(Y) (all but two choices of a work). The pair (X+a, Y+b) is comonotonic, lies in D, and by Step 1 has weights Π(X)+a, Π(Y)+b with sum q + s ≠ 0; (A3) applied to it gives LR(X+Y+s) = [(Π(X)+a)(ρ(X)+a)/(Π(X)+a) + (Π(Y)+b)(ρ(Y)+b)/(Π(Y)+b)]/(q+s) = (s + r)/(s + q) ∈ R. By (A1) also LR(X+Y+s) = (s − z(X+Y))/(s − p(X+Y)). Case r ≠ q: the profile of X+Y is a non-constant Möbius map agreeing with (s+r)/(s+q) on a cofinite set, so its zero and pole are −r and −q: z(X+Y) = −r, p(X+Y) = −q. Case r = q: LR(X+Y+s) = 1 for all s ≠ −q, so by (A1) z(X+Y) = p(X+Y) and the ∗-point must be −q. In both cases ρ(X+Y) = ρ(X)+ρ(Y), Π(X+Y) = Π(X)+Π(Y). (This covers X or Y in N, where Assa's association is silent — the reason for using the weighted identity rather than the level-crossing axiom.)

STEP 4 (continuity). Part A: (A4) says exactly that ρ and Π are uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous. Part B: (A4') plus Step 1 give ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y + ‖X−Y‖_∞) = ρ(Y) + ‖X−Y‖_∞, so ρ, Π are 1-Lipschitz; (A4) follows.

STEP 5 (positive homogeneity, hence scale invariance, is derived). Comonotonic additivity gives ρ(nX) = nρ(X) and ρ(X/n) = ρ(X)/n, hence ρ(qX) = qρ(X) for rational q > 0; continuity (Step 4) extends this to all λ > 0. Same for Π. Hence LR(λX) = LR(X) and LR(c) = ρ(c)/Π(c) = c/c = 1 (c ≠ 0), LR(0) = ∗.

STEP 6 (representation). ρ and Π are law-invariant (Step 2), comonotonic-additive (Step 3) and uniformly norm-continuous (Step 4) functionals L^∞ → R on an atomless space, so by SC[1] = Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020) THEOREM 1 there are h, g ∈ H with ρ = I_h, Π = I_g. By SC[1] Lemma 2(ii), I_h(c) = c·h(1); since ρ(c) = ρ(0) + c = c (Step 1 and I_h(0) = 0) we get h(1) = 1, likewise g(1) = 1. Uniqueness: SC[1] Lemma 1 (h_1(1) = h_2(1) and I_{h_1} = I_{h_2} ⇒ h_1 = h_2), or directly h(t) = ρ(1_A), P(A) = t. Then LR(X) = ρ(X)/Π(X) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) for all X ∈ L^∞ (Step 1), which is (II). Part B: (A4') says I_h, I_g are increasing, so h, g are non-decreasing by SC[1] Lemma 2(i); with h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1 they are distortion functions, giving (II').

STEP 7 (family and association). h ↦ I_h is linear and injective, so ρ_β = (I_h − βI_g)/(1−β) = I_{k_β} with k_β = (h−βg)/(1−β); k_β(1) = 1 gives cash-additivity (Lemma 2(ii)), Lemma 2(iii) positive homogeneity, Lemma 4 the Lipschitz bound TV(k_β) ≤ (TV h + β TV g)/|1−β|, Lemma 2(i) the monotonicity criterion in (c). For X ∉ N and β ≠ 1: LR(X − m) = (ρ(X) − m)/(Π(X) − m) = β ⇔ m(1−β) = ρ(X) − βΠ(X) ⇔ m = ρ_β(X), proving (d); LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 is the case m = 0. For X ∈ 𝒳 ∩ D, I_g(X) > 0 in Part B, so β ↦ I_{h−βg}(X) = I_h(X) − βI_g(X) is strictly decreasing and LR(X) = inf{β : I_{h−βg}(X) ≤ 0} (SC[6] Han–Lin–Wang Def. 1 / Remark 3 template with non-monotone members).

CONVERSE (II) ⇒ (I) (and (II') ⇒ (I')). Let ρ = I_h, Π = I_g with h(1) = g(1) = 1. (A1): by SC[1] Lemma 2(ii), LR(X+c) = (I_h(X)+c)/(I_g(X)+c) = (c − z)/(c − p) with z = −I_h(X), p = −I_g(X), including the projective conventions (∞ when I_g(X)+c = 0 ≠ I_h(X)+c, ∗ when both vanish). (A2): I_h, I_g are law-invariant by definition. (A3): for comonotonic X, Y ∈ D with I_g(X)+I_g(Y) ≠ 0, comonotonic additivity (SC[1] Thm 1, 'if' direction) gives [Π(X)LR(X) + Π(Y)LR(Y)]/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) = (I_h(X)+I_h(Y))/(I_g(X)+I_g(Y)) = I_h(X+Y)/I_g(X+Y) = LR(X+Y). (A4): SC[1] Lemma 4, |I_h(X) − I_h(Y)| ≤ TV(h)‖X−Y‖_∞. (A4'): SC[1] Lemma 2(i), I_h increasing iff h increasing. Part B domain claim: for non-decreasing g with g(1) = 1, I_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx > 0 for all X ∈ 𝒳 iff g > 0 on (0,1] (⇐: P(X>x) > 0 on [0, ess sup X), a set of positive length; ⇒: X = 1_B, P(B) = t gives I_g(1_B) = g(t)).

PART C. (c1): SC[1] Theorem 3 (i)⇔(iii) (h concave ⇔ I_h subadditive) together with Part B monotonicity gives coherence; the ES-mixture form with unique μ is KU[2] = Jouini–Schachermayer–Touzi Thm 2.3 / Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 (KU[5]) and uniqueness KU[11] Shapiro Thm 3.1(ii). (c2): LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⇔ I_{h−g} ≤ 0 on 𝒳 ⇔ (cash-invariance of I_{h−g}, (h−g)(1) = 0, and every X ∈ L^∞ is a cash shift of some element of 𝒳) I_{h−g} ≤ 0 on L^∞ ⇔ I_h ≤ I_g on L^∞ ⇔ h ≤ g by SC[1] Lemma 1 / SC[3] Prop. 2(i); the monotonicity statement is F2 (nested layers 1_B + 1_D, D ⊂ B, give h/g non-increasing on (0,1], hence ≡ h(1)/g(1) = 1). (c3): for β ≠ 1, ρ_β(X + t) = ρ_β(X) + t, so every sublevel set of ρ_β is a translate of {ρ_β ≤ 0}; hence {ρ_β ≤ 0} convex ⇔ ρ_β quasi-convex ⇔ (SC[1] Theorem 3 (v)⇔(i)) k_β concave ⇔ h − βg concave (β<1) / convex (β>1); the same for superlevel sets with the signs reversed, and {(1−β)ρ_β ≤ 0} = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} convex ⇔ h − βg concave in both regimes (SC[7] Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang Prop. 2.7 is the general principle: quasi-convex = convex when the cash slope is ≠ 0; its Example 2.5 shows the breakdown at β = 1, i.e. for D = ρ − Π). On 𝒳: {LR ≤ β} ∩ 𝒳 = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} ∩ 𝒳 for every β (I_g > 0 there). Sufficiency is immediate. Necessity for β > 1: if X_1, X_2 ∈ L^∞ satisfy I_{h−βg}(X_i) ≤ 0, shifting by c > 0 large puts X_i + c ∈ 𝒳 and lowers I_{h−βg} by c(β−1) ≥ 0, so convexity on 𝒳 yields I_{h−βg}((X_1+X_2)/2) ≤ 0, i.e. {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} is convex on L^∞ and h − βg is concave; letting β ↓ 1 and β → ∞ in 'h − βg = (h−g) − (β−1)g concave' gives h − g concave and g convex, and conversely. Necessity for {LR ≥ β} ∩ 𝒳, β < 1, is the mirror argument (upward shifts raise I_{h−βg}); β ↑ 1 and β = 0 give h convex and h − g convex. (c4): LR(F) = β ⇔ I_{k_β}(F) = 0; mixtures commute with cash shifts and k_β(1) = 1, so convexity of the zero set of I_{k_β} is equivalent to CxLS of I_{k_β}, decided by SC[4] Wang–Wei Theorem 3.2 (I_k has CxLS on a convex class containing three-point laws iff k ∈ H* = H1* ∪ H2* ∪ H3*) and Corollary 3.3 (CxLS ⇒ monotone); if {h,g} ⊂ H1* or {h,g} ⊂ H3* with common α, every k_β stays in the same class, giving sufficiency; identifiability/elicitability ⇒ CxLS is SC[4] Prop. 6.4. (Necessity of the pair description is a special case of Wang–Wei's open problem on two-dimensional signed Choquet integrals with CxLS and is only conjectured.)

VARIANTS: replace SC[1] Thm 1 in Step 6 by CS[2] CMMM Cor. 18 (comonotonic additive + bounded variation + outer continuous on B(Σ) ⇔ Choquet w.r.t. a signed set function of bounded variation) to drop law invariance, or by SC[3] Wang–Wang–Wei Thm 1 (law-invariant + comonotonic-additive + continuous at infinity + uniformly sup-continuous on a law-invariant convex cone ⊃ L^∞) to pass to unbounded losses.
MINIMALITY: (A1) [Möbius profile] is the structural axiom: it is what defines z and p, and it is EQUIVALENT to 'LR = ρ/Π with ρ, Π cash-additive' (Step 1 and the converse). It cannot be weakened to the ordinal/betweenness information of F1: LR' := (I_h/I_g)^3 (cubing in P¹, ∗ ↦ ∗) is law-invariant, scale-invariant, continuous, has the same poles and zeros, and satisfies the two-sided mediant INEQUALITY on comonotone pairs with positive premiums (a monotone transform preserves betweenness), but its cash profile ((c−z)/(c−p))^3 is not a Möbius map and the weighted identity (A3) fails; LR' is not a Choquet ratio. This also shows that the unweighted mediant property of F1 (Chew/Dekel betweenness) only determines the pair projectively on each comonotone cone, and that the cardinal, premium-weighted form is essential. Note moreover that the two-sided mediant inequality is FALSE for the true distortion ratio once a cash shift pushes a premium below zero (e.g. ρ(X)=Π(X)=1, ρ(Y)=2, Π(Y)=1, a=−2.5: LR(X+a)=1, LR(Y)=2, LR(X+Y+a)=−1), so only the weighted identity is globally valid.
(A2) [law invariance]: ρ = E_Q, Π = E_P with Q ≠ P equivalent to P satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4), (A4') but LR = E_Q[X]/E[X] is not law-invariant and is not of the form I_h/I_g (which is law-invariant). Without (A2) the correct conclusion is the CMMM/Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida ratio of two Choquet integrals w.r.t. signed set functions of bounded variation.
(A3) [weighted mediant]: ρ = E, Π = I_{g_1} ∨ I_{g_2} for two crossing concave distortions g_1 ≠ g_2 (or Π = E + λ·SD): Π is law-invariant, monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous and ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz, so (A1), (A2), (A4), (A4') hold, but Π is not comonotonic-additive (strictly subadditive on comonotone pairs whose argmax differ), so (A3) fails; by uniqueness of the pair Π would have to equal some I_g, impossible. Thus the Kusuoka/convex world is genuinely larger and (A3) is exactly what cuts it down to single distortions.
(A4) [uniform continuity, Part A]: with the axiom of choice take an additive, discontinuous φ: R → R and set ρ := E + φ∘GMD (GMD = Gini mean difference, a location-invariant comonotonic-additive signed Choquet integral), Π := E. Then (A1) (GMD is location invariant, so ρ is cash-additive), (A2), (A3) hold, but ρ is not continuous, not positively homogeneous, and not a signed Choquet integral; LR = 1 + φ(GMD(X))/E[X] is not even scale invariant. Mere norm-continuity instead of uniform continuity is not enough for h ∈ H either (SC[1] Remark 3: the representation persists with h of unbounded variation). In Part B the Hamel example is killed by (A4'), which is why (A4) can be dropped there.
(A4') [endpoint monotonicity, Part B]: g(t) = t + ½ sin(2πt) (positive on (0,1], g(1) = 1, of bounded variation, not monotone) with h(t) = t gives a pair satisfying (A1)–(A4) with Π = I_g non-monotone; so (A4') is needed for the distortion conclusion, and by F2 it cannot be replaced by any monotonicity statement about LR. Conversely (A4') cannot be weakened to monotonicity of a single interior member ρ_β: ρ_β monotone only says h − βg is monotone, which neither implies nor is implied by monotonicity of h and g.
Atomlessness: on a space with atoms the range of t ↦ P(X > x) misses part of [0,1], h and g are determined only on that range (uniqueness fails) and WWW Theorem 1 is unavailable; law invariance then does not yield v = h∘P.
Cash-extended domain: on 𝒳 alone, LR is invariant under (ρ,Π) ↦ (ψρ, ψΠ) for any positive functional ψ (e.g. ψ(X) = 1 + Var(X)/(1+‖X‖_∞^2)), so no theorem can recover a Choquet pair from LR|_𝒳; Assa's cash shifts LR(X − m) are the minimal additional datum, and the pole of the profile is what identifies Π on the degenerate set N.
Independence of the derived properties: scale invariance, LR(c) = 1 and the association with {ρ_β} are consequences (Steps 5, 7) and would be redundant as axioms.
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) The theorem is 'on LR alone' only in the extended sense that LR must be known on all cash shifts of non-negative losses, with the location of its pole as part of the data; (A4) and (A4') are phrased through the derived break-even functionals z, p rather than through inequalities on LR-values, and (A4') is in substance a hypothesis on the pair (ρ,Π). I see no way around this: on 𝒳 the pair is only determined up to a positive gauge, and by F2 monotonicity is invisible in LR. (2) (A4) is a technical axiom with a non-constructive (Hamel-basis) counterexample; a more intrinsic substitute would be 'z, p of bounded variation' (CMMM) or a Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei-type 'relevance/boundedness on the unit ball' condition from which continuity is derived, which I have not carried out. (3) In (c3), necessity of 'h − βg concave' from convexity of {LR ≤ β} ∩ 𝒳 is proved only for β > 1 (and of 'h − βg convex' from {LR ≥ β} ∩ 𝒳 only for β < 1); for the complementary level sets on 𝒳 I only have sufficiency, since the WWW Theorem 3 test variables may need to be negative; on the cash-extended domain the statements are full iffs. Consequently 'LR quasi-convex on 𝒳' is sandwiched between 'h concave and g convex' (sufficient) and 'h − g concave and g convex' (necessary). (4) (c4) necessity (the only CxLS pairs are H1*- or common-α H3*-pairs) is conjectural (Wang–Wei's open problem); note that the elicitability strand's claim 'CxLS ⇒ LR ≡ 1' is too strong (VaR^{c1}_{1−α}/VaR^{c2}_{1−α} is a non-constant counterexample), and F6 should be read as functional-level identification only. (5) Continuity of h, g (Assa's continuity along bounded a.s. convergence in F1) is not addressed; I expect 'ρ, Π continuous along bounded a.s. convergence ⇔ h, g continuous' for monotone h, g but have not verified it against a source. (6) WWW 2020, Wang–Wang–Wei 2020, Wang–Wei 2020, Liu et al. 2020 and Han–Lin–Wang were read in full by this strand (high confidence); the CMMM/Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida variant and the Kusuoka/JST/Föllmer–Schied citations are taken from the other strands' digests (medium confidence for exact numbering). (7) The theorem characterizes the signed/distortion pencil, not more general 'Kusuoka-type pairs' (suprema of distortions): for those (A3) must be dropped and replaced by convexity + L^p-continuity of two members of the family (Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang Thm 2.2/2.4), which yields only a sup/inf representation of (ρ,Π), not a single pair, and I have not stated that as an iff.

=== PROPOSAL 4: Möbius–Choquet characterization of the extended loss ratio: (D,LR) has a Möbius cash profile, is law-invariant, has comonotone-additive zero and pole, and monotone endpoints  <=>  LR = C_h/C_g on D = {C_g ≠ 0} for a unique pair of distortion functions (h,g); with coherent/Kusuoka, convexity, continuity and signed-Choquet add-ons (Law-invariant coherent/convex scholar (Kusuoka / JST / Föllmer–Schied / Shapiro / signed-Choquet strand)) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with ‖·‖_∞ (classes mod P-null sets); X+c denotes the position X shifted by the cash amount c ∈ ℝ (Assa's LR(X−m) is the case c = −m). 𝒳₊ := {X ∈ L^∞ : X ≥ 0 a.s., P(X>0) > 0} = the insurance losses proper. A distortion function is a nondecreasing h:[0,1]→[0,1] with h(0)=0, h(1)=1 (NO continuity assumed; VaR-type jumps allowed). For h of bounded variation with h(0)=0 the (signed) Choquet integral in survival form is I_h(X) := ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1))dx (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 Def. 1, Remark 1: P(X≥x) may replace P(X>x)); for a distortion h write C_h := I_h; on 𝒳₊ this is ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx. ES_α (α ∈ (0,1]) is the upper-tail expected shortfall of the loss, ES_1 = E, ES_0 := ess sup.  OBJECT: an extended loss ratio is a pair (D, LR) with D ⊂ L^∞ (the positions on which the loss ratio is defined, i.e. with non-zero premium) and LR: D → ℝ. The loss ratio proper is LR restricted to 𝒳₊ ∩ D. DERIVED DATA (used to phrase two axioms): for X ∈ L^∞, the cash line ℓ_X := {X+c : c ∈ ℝ}; the pole of X is the cash level at which the line leaves D; the zero of X is the cash level at which LR vanishes. The 'β-capital' m_β(X) is the solution m of LR(X−m) = β. Conventions: LR need not be monotone in X (impossible, F2) and is not assumed scale-invariant (this is derived).
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (M) Möbius cash structure (ratio structure + break-even normalization). For every X ∈ L^∞: (M1) the cash line ℓ_X meets L^∞ \ D in exactly one point, X + c_X; write Π(X) := −c_X ('pole' = premium: X − Π(X) is the position whose premium is exhausted); (M2) there is a real number ρ(X) such that LR(X+c) = (ρ(X) + c)/(Π(X) + c) for all c ≠ −Π(X). [Consequences built into (M): c ↦ LR(X+c) is a normalized Möbius map, LR(X+c) → 1 as c → ±∞ (translation to break-even, F1), LR(X+c) = 0 iff c = −ρ(X) ('zero'). Uniqueness of the data: Π(X) is the hole; if the profile is not identically 1 then ρ(X) is its zero, and if the profile is identically 1 then necessarily ρ(X) = Π(X) ('fair position'). Thus (ρ,Π): L^∞ → ℝ are functions of (D,LR).] (A2) (L) Law invariance of (D,LR): if X and Y have the same distribution under P then X ∈ D ⟺ Y ∈ D, and LR(X) = LR(Y) when X ∈ D. (A3) (C) Comonotone additivity of zero and pole: for comonotone X, Y ∈ L^∞, ρ(X+Y) = ρ(X) + ρ(Y) and Π(X+Y) = Π(X) + Π(Y). [Purely in terms of (D,LR), given (M), this is equivalent to: (i) pole additivity: X + Y + c ∉ D ⟺ c = −Π(X) − Π(Y); (ii) premium-weighted mediant identity: for comonotone X, Y with X, Y, X+Y ∈ D, LR(X+Y) = (Π(X)·LR(X) + Π(Y)·LR(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) — the equality version of the comonotone mediant property F1 with the weights revealed by the poles; equivalently the Sincov/Cauchy odds axioms of the decision-theory strand.] (A4) (E) Endpoint monotonicity: X ≤ Y a.s. ⟹ ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y) and Π(X) ≤ Π(Y). [In LR language: the two extreme β-capitals m_0(X) = ρ(X) (cash whose withdrawal drives the loss ratio to 0) and m_∞(X) := lim_{β→∞} m_β(X) = Π(X) (cash whose withdrawal exhausts the premium, the hole) are nondecreasing in the loss. This is monotonicity of the two ENDPOINTS ρ_0 = ρ, ρ_∞ = Π of the associated family only, never of LR or of an intermediate ρ_β.] (A5) NOT assumed (derived): scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X), λD = D (λ>0); normalization LR(c) = 1 for constants c ≠ 0 and 0 ∉ D; Lipschitz continuity of ρ, Π and continuity of LR on D; the affine-in-β structure of the associated family and the association LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0.
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: The domain D ⊂ L^∞ is part of the data: the premium Π is read off (D,LR) as the hole of each cash line. This is the only genuinely pair-level input. It is unavoidable if the fair set {ρ = Π} is to be handled without gaps: on 𝒳₊ alone, LR carries no information about (ρ,Π) at positions with LR(X+c) ≡ 1 (F3 identifies (h,g) from LR|𝒳₊ when LR ≢ 1, but an axiom system living on 𝒳₊ only cannot speak about comonotone additivity through fair positions). Interpretation: the loss ratio is undefined exactly where the premium is zero, as in Assa's extended loss ratio evaluated at X − m. | (C) and (E) are stated on the derived functionals (ρ,Π) = (zero, pole). Formally they are conditions on (D,LR); conceptually they are conditions on the pair: (C) is the premium-weighted mediant identity (needs the weights Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)), i.e. Π), and (E) is monotonicity of the two endpoint members ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_∞ = Π of the associated family. (E) cannot be replaced by ANY monotonicity property of LR itself (F2: a pointwise monotone LR = C_h/C_g forces h = g).
