(E) may be replaced by uniform ‖·‖_∞-continuity of (ρ,Π) at the price of enlarging the class to signed Choquet pairs (add-on 6). | Nothing is assumed on the intermediate members ρ_β, β ∉ {0,∞}: their non-monotonicity (F2) is a consequence, not a hypothesis, and they are automatically signed Choquet integrals (add-on (d)).
CONCLUSION: THEOREM. Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless and (D,LR) an extended loss ratio. The following are equivalent:
(I) (D,LR) satisfies (M), (L), (C), (E).
(II) There exist distortion functions h, g: [0,1] → [0,1] (nondecreasing, h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, not necessarily continuous) such that D = {X ∈ L^∞ : C_g(X) ≠ 0} and LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) for every X ∈ D.
Under (I)/(II): (a) UNIQUENESS: the pair (h,g) is uniquely determined by (D,LR), namely g(t) = Π(1_A) = −(the hole of ℓ_{1_A}) and h(t) = ρ(1_A) = (the zero of ℓ_{1_A}, = g(t) if 1_A is fair), for any A with P(A) = t; uniqueness holds even when LR ≡ 1 on D (then h = g is still identified through D). (b) DERIVED PROPERTIES: ρ = C_h and Π = C_g are law-invariant, monotone, cash-additive, comonotone-additive, positively homogeneous, 1-Lipschitz; LR(λX) = LR(X), λD = D for λ > 0; LR(c) = 1 for constants c ≠ 0, 0 ∉ D; LR is continuous on D. (c) DOMAIN: 𝒳₊ ⊂ D ⟺ g(t) > 0 for all t ∈ (0,1]; on 𝒳₊ ∩ D one has C_g > 0 and LR ≥ 0 (LR > 0 iff h > 0 on (0,1]). (d) ASSOCIATION (Assa's canonical family is a theorem): for β ≠ 1 put ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)}. Then for X ∈ D, β ≠ 1: LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0; for non-fair X the β-capital m_β(X) (unique m with LR(X−m) = β) equals ρ_β(X); for fair X, LR(X−m) = 1 for all m ≠ Π(X) and ρ_β(X) = ρ(X) = Π(X) for all β; ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_β → Π as |β| → ∞. Each ρ_β is a signed Choquet integral (F7): law-invariant, comonotone-additive, cash-additive with constant 1, positively homogeneous, ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz with constant ≤ (1+|β|)/|1−β|, and ρ_β is monotone ⟺ (h−βg)/(1−β) is nondecreasing (fails for most β unless h = g). 
ADD-ONS, each an 'iff' inside (II): (1) [coherent numerator / Kusuoka] ρ is subadditive ⟺ ρ is coherent ⟺ h is concave ⟺ ρ(X) = ∫_{[0,1]} ES_α(X) μ(dα) for a probability measure μ on [0,1] (ES_0 = ess sup), μ unique. (2) [premium] Π subadditive (coherent premium) ⟺ g concave ⟺ Π = ∫ ES_α ν(dα); Π superadditive ('bid price') ⟺ g convex ⟺ Π(X) = −∫ ES_α(−X) ν(dα). In the concave/convex case ρ_β = (1−β)^{-1}[∫ES_α(X)μ(dα) + β∫ES_α(−X)ν(dα)] is a two-sided (signed) Kusuoka mixture (Wang–Wang–Wei Thm 5 form). (3) [convexity of the family / shape of level sets] For β ≠ 1: ρ_β convex ⟺ ρ_β quasi-convex ⟺ ρ_β subadditive ⟺ (h−βg)/(1−β) concave. Hence: h concave and g convex ⟹ LR is quasi-convex on the convex set 𝒳₊ ∩ D and LR ≥ 1 there ('conic picture'); h convex and g concave ⟹ 𝒳₊ ⊂ D, LR is quasi-concave on 𝒳₊ and LR ≤ 1 ('actuarial picture', e.g. ρ = E, Π = C_g with g concave — corrected direction of F4). Rigidity: ρ_β is convex for EVERY β ≠ 1 ⟺ h = g ⟺ LR ≡ 1 on D. (4) [continuity] h and g continuous ⟺ ρ and Π are continuous along uniformly bounded a.s.-convergent sequences (Lebesgue property) ⟺ LR has this continuity on D. (5) [F2] LR is monotone on 𝒳₊ ⟺ h = g ⟺ LR ≡ 1 on D. (6) [signed variant] Replace (E) by (E′): ρ and Π are uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous. Then (M),(L),(C),(E′) ⟺ there are h, g of bounded variation with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, D = {I_g ≠ 0}, LR = I_h/I_g on D; (h,g) unique; and (E) ⟺ (E′) + [h,g nondecreasing]. (7) [non-law-invariant variant] (M),(C),(E) alone ⟺ LR = ∫X dv_1 / ∫X dv_2 on D = {∫X dv_2 ≠ 0} for unique capacities v_1, v_2 on F with v_i(Ω) = 1 (Choquet integrals in the Schmeidler sense); (L) is exactly what turns v_i into h∘P, g∘P.
PROOF SKETCH: Notation for citations: WWW20 = Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020, MOR) [Choquet-scholar [4]; coherent-scholar [8]; signed-Choquet-scholar [1],[2]]; WWW-ASTIN = Wang–Wang–Wei (2020) [Choquet [5]; coherent [9]]; Schm86 = Schmeidler (1986) [Choquet [1]; coherent [7]]; GWXY = Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i) [Choquet [7]]; FS/JST = Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 / Jouini–Schachermayer–Touzi Thm 2.3 [coherent [2],[5]]; Kus = Kusuoka Thm 7 [coherent [1],[2]]; Shap = Shapiro Thm 3.1 [coherent [11]]; LCLW = Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang [coherent [10]].

STEP 0 (uniqueness of the Möbius data; used in both directions). Two normalized Möbius maps c ↦ (c+a)/(c+b), c ↦ (c+a′)/(c+b′) on ℝ minus their poles coincide (with the same pole) iff (a,b) = (a′,b′) when a ≠ b: equality for all c gives a+b′ = a′+b and ab′ = a′b; with d := a−b = a′−b′ ≠ 0 this yields d b′ = d b, so b = b′, a = a′. If a = b the map is ≡ 1 and the convention ρ(X) = Π(X) applies. Hence (ρ,Π) are well-defined functions of (D,LR).

(II) ⟹ (I). Let h, g be distortions, D := {C_g ≠ 0}, LR := C_h/C_g on D. Cash additivity I_k(X+c) = I_k(X) + c·k(1) (WWW20 Lemma 2(ii)) with h(1) = g(1) = 1 gives C_g(X+c) = C_g(X)+c, which vanishes at exactly one c = −C_g(X): (M1) holds with Π = C_g; and LR(X+c) = (C_h(X)+c)/(C_g(X)+c): (M2) holds with ρ = C_h (and fair positions are those with C_h(X) = C_g(X), consistent with the convention). (L): law invariance of Choquet integrals. (C): comonotone additivity of C_h, C_g (Schm86; WWW20 Thm 1 direction '⟸'; Denneberg). (E): C_h, C_g monotone since h,g nondecreasing (WWW20 Lemma 2(i); or directly from the integrand). 

(I) ⟹ (II). (1) Cash additivity: the line of X+c₀ is the line of X reparametrized; its hole is at c = −Π(X) − c₀ and its profile is c ↦ (ρ(X)+c₀+c)/(Π(X)+c₀+c); by Step 0, ρ(X+c₀) = ρ(X)+c₀, Π(X+c₀) = Π(X)+c₀. (2) Law invariance of ρ, Π: X =_d Y ⟹ X+c =_d Y+c for all c, so by (L) the holes and profiles coincide; Step 0. (3) ρ(0) = Π(0) = 0 by (C) (0 is comonotone with itself: ρ(0) = 2ρ(0)); hence ρ(c) = Π(c) = c, 0 ∉ D, LR(c) = 1 for c ≠ 0. (4) Lipschitz: (E)+(1) give the standard estimate X ≤ Y + ‖X−Y‖_∞ ⟹ ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y) + ‖X−Y‖_∞, i.e. ρ, Π are 1-Lipschitz; in particular uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous. (5) Positive homogeneity (hence scale invariance of LR): (C) with X comonotone to itself gives ρ(nX) = nρ(X) and ρ(X) = nρ(X/n), so ρ(qX) = qρ(X) for rational q > 0; by (4) and q_n → λ, ρ(λX) = λρ(X); same for Π. Therefore λD = D and LR(λX) = ρ(λX)/Π(λX) = LR(X). (6) Representation: ρ: L^∞ → ℝ is law-invariant (2), comonotone-additive (C) and uniformly norm-continuous (4), so by WWW20 Thm 1 ρ = I_{h} for a unique h ∈ H (bounded variation, h(0) = 0; uniqueness: WWW20 Lemma 1, or directly h(t) = I_h(1_A) = ρ(1_A), P(A) = t, all t ∈ [0,1] being attained because the space is atomless). By (E) and WWW20 Lemma 2(i), h is nondecreasing; by (3) and Lemma 2(ii), h(1) = ρ(1) = 1; thus h is a distortion function. Likewise Π = C_g with g a distortion. [Alternative route without any continuity: Schm86 (monotone + comonotone additive on bounded measurable functions ⟹ Choquet integral w.r.t. the capacity v(A) = ρ(1_A)); by (2) and atomlessness v(A) = h(P(A)) with h nondecreasing, h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1 (this is GWXY Prop. 2(i) / Kus Thm 7 / Yaari); the integrals with P(X≥x) and P(X>x) agree because the two survival functions differ at countably many x.] (7) Conclusion: X ∈ D ⟺ the hole of ℓ_X is not at c = 0 ⟺ Π(X) ≠ 0 ⟺ C_g(X) ≠ 0, so D = {C_g ≠ 0}; for X ∈ D, taking c = 0 in (M2), LR(X) = ρ(X)/Π(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X). 

(a) Uniqueness: if LR = C_h/C_g = C_{h′}/C_{g′} on D = {C_g ≠ 0} = {C_{g′} ≠ 0}, then both pairs are Möbius data for (D,LR) (direction (II)⟹(I)), so by Step 0 C_h = C_{h′} and C_g = C_{g′} on L^∞, and h(t) = C_h(1_A) gives h = h′, g = g′. (b) is (1)–(5). (c) For X ∈ 𝒳₊, P(X>x) > 0 on some [0,δ) (right-continuity of the survival function at 0), so C_g(X) > 0 if g > 0 on (0,1]; conversely if g(t) = 0 for some t ∈ (0,1] then C_g(1_A) = 0 for P(A) = t, i.e. 1_A ∈ 𝒳₊ \\ D. (d) For X ∈ D with Π(X) ≠ 0 and β ≠ 1: LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ(X) = βΠ(X) ⟺ (1−β)ρ_β(X) = 0. The equation LR(X−m) = β reads (ρ(X)−m)/(Π(X)−m) = β, i.e. m(β−1) = βΠ(X) − ρ(X), i.e. m = (ρ(X) − βΠ(X))/(1−β) = ρ_β(X), unique when ρ(X) ≠ Π(X) (fair X give the constant profile). ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} by linearity of k ↦ I_k (F7); its properties are WWW20 Lemma 2 and Lemma 4 with TV((h−βg)/(1−β)) ≤ (TV h + |β| TV g)/|1−β| = (1+|β|)/|1−β|; monotonicity criterion is Lemma 2(i). 

ADD-ONS. (1) h concave ⟺ C_h subadditive ⟺ C_h convex (WWW20 Thm 3 (i)⟺(iii)⟺(iv); WWW-ASTIN Cor. 1), and a monotone cash-additive positively homogeneous subadditive functional is coherent; coherent + law-invariant + comonotone-additive ⟺ ∫ES_α dμ(α) (FS Thm 4.93 / JST Thm 2.3 / Kus Thm 7, Fatou property automatic by JST Thm 2.1); μ is unique (Shap Thm 3.1(ii) on L^p, p<∞; on L^∞ uniqueness follows from uniqueness of h since the concave distortion determines μ through ψ′(t) = ∫_{(t,1]} s^{-1} μ(ds)). (2) Same for g; for g convex use I_g(X) = −I_{ĝ}(−X), ĝ(t) = 1 − g(1−t) concave (WWW20 Lemma 2(iv)), then apply (1) to ĝ. (3) WWW20 Thm 3 applied to k_β = (h−βg)/(1−β) (k_β(1) = 1 so all sublevel sets of ρ_β are translates of {ρ_β ≤ 0}, which is why convexity of the single level set and quasi-convexity coincide, cf. LCLW Prop. 2.7 with c = 1). On 𝒳₊ ∩ D one has C_g > 0 and {LR ≤ β} = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} ∩ 𝒳₊ ∩ D; if h is concave and g convex then h − βg is concave for every β ≥ 0, I_{h−βg} is convex, 𝒳₊ ∩ D = 𝒳₊ ∩ {C_g > 0} is convex (superlevel set of the concave C_g intersected with a convex cone), so every sublevel set is convex (β < 0 gives the empty set since LR ≥ 0): LR is quasi-convex; LR ≥ 1 because h ≥ id ≥ g pointwise gives C_h ≥ C_g (WWW20 Lemma 1). Symmetrically h convex, g concave: g ≥ id > 0 on (0,1] so 𝒳₊ ⊂ D, {LR ≥ β} = {I_{h−βg} ≥ 0} ∩ 𝒳₊ is a superlevel set of the concave I_{h−βg}, hence convex: LR is quasi-concave and ≤ 1. Rigidity: ρ_β convex for all β ∈ [0,1) ⟺ h and h−g concave (β = 0 and β → 1); for all β > 1 ⟺ βg − h concave ∀β>1 ⟺ g and g−h concave (β → ∞ and β → 1); so h−g is concave and convex, i.e. affine with (h−g)(0) = (h−g)(1) = 0, i.e. h = g. (4) If h is continuous and X_n → X a.s. boundedly, then P(X_n > x) → P(X > x) for all x outside the countable set of atoms of the law of X, and dominated convergence on a bounded x-interval gives C_h(X_n) → C_h(X); conversely A_n ↓ A and A_n ↑ A with P(A_n) → t from the right/left (available on an atomless space) give right- and left-continuity of h at t. (5) F2: if LR is monotone on 𝒳₊, then for D ⊂ B, P(D) = q ≤ p = P(B), 1_B ≤ 1_B + 1_D ≤ 2·1_B and scale invariance give h(p)/g(p) ≤ (h(p)+h(q))/(g(p)+g(q)) ≤ h(p)/g(p), hence h(q)/g(q) = h(p)/g(p) for all q ≤ p (where g > 0), so h/g is constant = h(1)/g(1) = 1, i.e. h = g; conversely h = g gives LR ≡ 1, which is monotone. (6) Identical proof with WWW20 Thm 1 (needs exactly (L), (C), (E′)) and without Lemma 2(i); uniqueness by WWW20 Lemma 1. (7) Schm86 on representatives in B(Ω,F): ρ(X) = ∫ X dv_1 with v_1(A) = ρ(1_A), monotone with v_1(Ω) = ρ(1) = 1; the P-null sets are v_1-null because ρ is defined on classes; same for Π.
MINIMALITY: Scale invariance is NOT an axiom: it follows from (C)+(E) (rational homogeneity from comonotone additivity, then Lipschitz continuity), in accordance with Assa's Prop. 1 'ρ_β positively homogeneous ⟺ LR scale invariant'. The remaining four axioms are independent, as the following examples show (all on an atomless space).
(M) is needed, in both of its contents. (i) Ratio/cash structure: LR′ := φ∘(C_h/C_g) with φ a non-Möbius bijection (e.g. φ(t) = t³ or φ(t) = e^{t−1}) satisfies (L), scale invariance, continuity and the two-sided mediant INEQUALITY of F1 (monotone φ preserves 'between'), but it is not a ratio of cash-additive functionals: any such ratio has a Möbius cash profile, and φ∘Möbius is Möbius only if φ is itself a Möbius transformation φ(t) = (αt+β)/(γt+δ) — in which case LR′ = (αC_h+βC_g)/(γC_h+δC_g) is again in the class (with distortions αh+βg, γh+δg). So the ordinal content of LR (its level sets) never determines the pair; the cardinal content carried by the cash profile is exactly what (M) fixes. (ii) Normalization: the expected-value-principle loss ratio LR(X) = C_h(X)/((1+θ)E[X]), θ > 0, satisfies (L),(C),(E)-type properties for the pair (C_h, (1+θ)E) but its cash profile tends to 1/(1+θ), not to 1; it violates (M2) and is not a ratio of distortion functions (g(1) = 1+θ). (M) can be relaxed harmlessly to LR(X+c) = (c+ρ(X))/(κ(c+Π(X)/κ)) with a fixed κ > 0, yielding g(1) = κ; the theorem as stated chooses κ = 1 (break-even normalization LR(X+c) → 1, F1). (iii) 'Exactly one hole per cash line' is needed: Π(X) = |E[X]| has one hole but the profile (c+ρ(X))/|c+E[X]| is not Möbius; here no cash-additive premium exists.
(L) is needed: ρ(X) = ∫X d(h∘Q), Π(X) = E_Q[X] with Q ≈ P, Q ≠ P, satisfy (M),(C),(E) but ρ(1_A) depends on Q(A), not on P(A), so LR is not a ratio of P-distortions. Without (L) the theorem degrades to add-on (7) (capacities), which is sharp.
(C) is needed, in both parts. (i) Zero: ρ := max(ES_α, ½(E + ess sup)) (a maximum of two law-invariant coherent comonotone-additive functionals, neither dominating the other, hence not comonotone-additive: take A with P(A) ≥ α and D ⊂ A small), Π := E. Then (M),(L),(E) hold (ρ is cash-additive, monotone, law-invariant), but LR = ρ/E is not a ratio of distortions: by Step 0 of the proof the Möbius data are unique, so a representation C_h/C_g would force ρ = C_h, contradicting non-additivity. (ii) Pole: ρ := C_h, Π := E + sd (standard-deviation premium) has a Möbius profile with pole −(E[X]+sd(X)) but the pole is not comonotone-additive; LR is not in the class. (iii) The INEQUALITY form of the mediant property (F1) cannot replace the identity form in (C): example (M)(i) above (φ∘(C_h/C_g)) satisfies the inequality and fails to be a ratio of comonotone-additive functionals; the weights Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) revealed by the identity are the cardinal information (Sincov/Cauchy equations in the decision-theory strand).
(E) is needed and cannot be replaced by any property of LR. (i) ρ := I_h with h(t) = t + ½ sin(2πt) (bounded variation, h(0)=0, h(1)=1, h′ = 1 + π cos(2πt) < 0 near t = ½), Π := E: (M),(L),(C) hold, LR = I_h/E is a ratio of signed Choquet integrals with a unique non-monotone h, so it is not in the distortion class; this is exactly the enlargement described in add-on (6). (ii) Dropping (E) without substituting continuity destroys even the signed representation: with f: ℝ → ℝ additive but discontinuous (Hamel basis), ρ := E + f∘(ES_α − E) is cash-additive (ES_α − E is cash-invariant), law-invariant, comonotone-additive (ES_α − E is, and f is additive), so (M),(L),(C) hold with Π = E, but ρ is not norm-continuous while every signed Choquet integral is Lipschitz (WWW20 Lemma 4): no representation of any kind. With mere (non-uniform) norm continuity one obtains a distortion not of bounded variation (WWW20 Remark 3), outside the class. (iii) F2 (add-on 5) shows that monotonicity of LR itself on 𝒳₊ forces LR ≡ 1, so (E) cannot be traded for an axiom on LR; it must be placed on the endpoints m_0 = ρ and m_∞ = Π.
Atomless space is needed: with atoms, law invariance does not produce h on all of [0,1] and the identification in (a) fails (F3 uses all probability levels and nested events).
The domain D as data is needed for a gap-free statement: on the fair set {C_h = C_g} the restriction LR|𝒳₊ is identically 1 along cash shifts and carries no information on (ρ,Π); without D one can still identify (h,g) from LR|𝒳₊ when LR ≢ 1 (F3), but an axiom system on 𝒳₊ alone cannot express (C) through fair positions (see weaknesses).
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) Axioms (C) and (E) are phrased on the derived pair (ρ,Π) = (zero, pole). They are functions of (D,LR), so formally the theorem is 'on LR alone', but conceptually (E) is a hypothesis on the two endpoint members of the associated family and (C) is the premium-weighted mediant identity, i.e. it uses the premium weights. I do not believe this can be improved: F2 rules out monotonicity on LR, and the φ∘LR example shows that no family of axioms on the VALUES of LR without a cardinal (cash-profile) anchor can single out the ratio structure. (2) The domain D (positions with non-zero premium) is primitive data; equivalently the premium is observed through the hole of each cash line. A version with LR defined only on 𝒳₊ (and nonnegative cash additions) needs an extra regularity axiom on the fair set N = {X : LR(X+c) ≡ 1} (e.g. N closed with empty interior and LR continuous) and a proof that comonotone additivity extends through N; I could not close that extension argument in general (the obstruction is that N ∩ K, K a comonotone cone, need not be approximable by comonotone triples outside N without already knowing linearity on K), so I state the theorem with D. (3) The converse in add-on (3) on 𝒳₊ (quasi-convexity of LR on 𝒳₊ ⟹ h concave and g convex) is not proved: WWW20 Thm 3 (v)⟹(i) is on L^∞ and its test variables may be signed; on L^∞ the equivalence for each ρ_β is exact. (4) The convex-but-not-comonotone-additive ('Kusuoka-supremum') class is not covered: replacing (C) by subadditivity + positive homogeneity of ρ_β leads, via LCLW Thm 2.2/2.4 and JST Thm 2.2, to ρ_β = sup of concave signed Choquet integrals, but the pair is then recovered only as (sup of C_h over a set, inf of C_g over a set) and is not unique; no clean iff with uniqueness is available in that class. (5) The theorem is on L^∞; for L^p losses one must add continuity at infinity (WWW-ASTIN Thm 1) and the Shapiro warning that comonotonicity ⟺ single Kusuoka generator fails at p = ∞ is irrelevant here but the L^p version was not written out. (6) Source reliability: WWW20, WWW-ASTIN, LCLW, Shapiro, JST were read in the primary PDFs by the panel; Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 numbering, Kusuoka Thm 7 and Schmeidler's exact wording are from memory/secondary citations (medium confidence) — the proof only uses them through WWW20 Thm 1/Lemma 2 and JST Thm 2.3, which were read. (7) Elicitability/identifiability of LR is not addressed: by the elicitability strand (Wang–Wei Thm 3.2 + Cor 3.3) a distortion loss ratio has convex level sets only if h = g, so no identification-function axiom can be added; (d) is a zero-set ('functional') identification only. (8) The convention that fair positions carry ρ(X) = Π(X) is forced by (M) but means that on the fair set the premium is identified by D alone; a reader who insists on LR being the only observable will regard this as a pair-level input.

=== PROPOSAL 5: Möbius-profile characterization of the loss ratio as a ratio of signed Choquet integrals, with signed-kernel acceptability representation of its level cones (Acceptability-index / quasiconvex-duality scholar (Cherny–Madan, Drapeau–Kupper, CMMM, Frittelli–Maggis, BCDK; bipolar/level-set methods), ten-scholar panel on the loss ratio) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with ‖·‖_∞; 𝒳 := L^∞_+∖{0} (Assa's nonnegative bounded losses). H := {k:[0,1]→ℝ of bounded variation, k(0)=0}; for k∈H the signed Choquet integral (survival form) is I_k(X) = ∫_0^∞ k(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (k(P(X>x))−k(1))dx (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 [WWW], Def. 1; C_k := I_k when k is a distortion). An EXTENDED LOSS RATIO is a partial map LR: D → ℝ, D ⊂ L^∞ its domain (the set where the premium does not vanish; LR is 'undefined' off D). Assa's loss ratio is the restriction of LR to 𝒳 ∩ D, and Assa's cash shifts LR(X−m) are exactly what forces LR to be defined on translates of 𝒳, i.e. on signed positions. Comonotone: (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 a.s.⊗a.s. Derived objects (defined from LR alone under axiom A0 below): the POLE p(X) and the ZERO z(X) of the cash profile c ↦ LR(X+c); we write b(X) := −p(X) ('premium capital') and a(X) := −z(X) ('risk capital'), and V_β := a − βb, β ≥ 0. Assa's canonical family is ρ_β = V_β/(1−β) (β≠1), and the β-capital m_β(X) solving LR(X−m)=β is m_β = ρ_β(X) (sign convention X−m).
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A0) MÖBIUS CASH PROFILE: for every X ∈ L^∞ the set {c ∈ ℝ : X+c ∉ D} is a singleton {p(X)}, and there is z(X) ∈ ℝ with LR(X+c) = (c − z(X))/(c − p(X)) for all c ≠ p(X). [Intrinsic reformulation (Assa Prop. 1 read backwards): LR admits a family {ρ_β}_{β≠1} of CASH-ADDITIVE functionals in Assa's association LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0 (X∈D), with (1−β)ρ_β(X) affine in β; such a family is unique, see proof step 0. The degenerate set N={z=p} (profile ≡ 1 off the pole) needs no separate treatment: the pole still identifies b, and a=b on N.] (A2) (A2) LAW INVARIANCE OF LR AND OF ITS DOMAIN: if X =_d Y then X∈D ⟺ Y∈D, and LR(X)=LR(Y) for X∈D. (Equivalently: a and b are law-invariant.) (A3) (A3) COMONOTONE BREAK-EVEN ADDITIVITY AT TWO LEVELS: there is β_1 ∈ (0,∞)∖{1} such that for β ∈ {0, β_1} and all comonotone X,Y ∈ L^∞: (i) the level set {LR = β} is closed under comonotone sums, i.e. LR(X')=LR(Y')=β ⟹ LR(X'+Y')=β whenever X',Y' are comonotone and in D; (ii) poles add: p(X+Y) = p(X)+p(Y). (Equivalently: V_0 = a and V_{β_1} = a − β_1 b are comonotone additive, hence a and b are.) (A4) (A4) UNIFORM CONTINUITY OF THE CAPITALS: X ↦ a(X) and X ↦ b(X) are uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞ on L^∞. (Equivalently, by (A0): the zero and the pole of the cash profile of LR move uniformly continuously with X; under (A3) this is the same as Lipschitz continuity, WWW Remark 3.) (A5) DERIVED, NOT ASSUMED: (A1) scale invariance LR(λX)=LR(X), λ>0 (and λD = D), is a CONSEQUENCE of (A0),(A2)–(A4) (positive homogeneity of signed Choquet integrals, WWW Lemma 2(iii)); it may be listed as an axiom for readability but is redundant.
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: NONE are needed for the representation class 'signed Choquet pair' (Theorem A): every hypothesis is a property of the values of LR, of its domain D, or of the zero/pole of its cash profile, which are functions of LR. | ADD-ONS that cannot be expressed through the values of LR (by F2) and are stated on the two ENDPOINT capitals a = ρ_0 and b = lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = Π: (M) a monotone ⟺ h nondecreasing; b monotone ⟺ g nondecreasing (then (h,g) is an ordinary distortion pair, Assa's setting, and b>0 on 𝒳 ⟺ g>0 on (0,1]); (S) a subadditive ⟺ h concave; b subadditive ⟺ g concave (coherent pair, Kusuoka/JST/Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93: a=∫ES_α dμ_h, b=∫ES_α dμ_g); (C) the convexity hypothesis 'h − βg concave for β in an interval J' used in Theorem B is a hypothesis on the pair, equivalent by WWW Thm 3 to quasi-convexity of ρ_β for β∈J, and it is NOT implied by any property of LR values alone.
CONCLUSION: THEOREM A (representation and uniqueness). For a partial map LR: D → ℝ, D ⊂ L^∞, the following are equivalent: (I) there exist h, g ∈ H with h(1)=g(1)=1 such that D = {X ∈ L^∞ : I_g(X) ≠ 0} and LR(X) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) for all X ∈ D; (II) LR satisfies (A0),(A2),(A3),(A4). In that case: (a) the pair (h,g) is UNIQUE (always — including LR ≡ 1 on D, because the pole identifies g; this removes the exception in F3); (b) a = I_h = ρ, b = I_g = Π, and the unique cash-additive family in association with LR is Assa's canonical family ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (F7), with ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_β → Π as β→∞; (c) LR is scale invariant, Lipschitz on {|I_g| ≥ δ} for each δ>0, and the mediant identity LR(X+Y) = [I_g(X)LR(X) + I_g(Y)LR(Y)]/(I_g(X)+I_g(Y)) holds for comonotone X,Y ∈ D with X+Y ∈ D. Subclasses, each an iff on the endpoints: (M) h,g nondecreasing ⟺ a,b monotone (distortion pair; then I_g > 0 on 𝒳 iff g>0 on (0,1], and restricting to 𝒳 recovers Assa's LR = C_h/C_g on nonnegative losses); (S) h (resp. g) concave ⟺ a (resp. b) subadditive; LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⟺ h ≤ g on [0,1] (WWW Lemma 1); LR pointwise monotone on 𝒳 ⟺ h = g ⟺ LR ≡ 1 (F2). THEOREM B (level cones and signed-kernel acceptability representation — this strand's contribution). Assume (I) and write V_β := I_{h−βg} = (1−β)ρ_β. (B1) For every β ≠ 1: the cone A_β := {V_β ≤ 0} is convex ⟺ V_β is quasi-convex ⟺ V_β is subadditive ⟺ h − βg is concave. Consequently, on the cone 𝒳_g := {X : I_g(X) > 0} (⊇ 𝒳 when g>0 on (0,1]) one has {LR ≤ β} ∩ 𝒳_g = A_β ∩ 𝒳_g, and LR is quasi-convex on 𝒳_g if h − βg is concave for all β ≥ 0, i.e. iff h is concave and g is convex (conic picture of F4, now an iff within the pair class for the cones A_β on L^∞); symmetrically LR is quasi-concave on 𝒳_g iff h is convex and g is concave (this CORRECTS F4's 'actuarial picture': ρ=E, g concave gives a quasi-CONCAVE loss ratio). (B2) If h − βg is concave for all β in an interval J ⊂ [0,∞)∖{1}, then for β ∈ J there is a unique σ(L^1,L^∞)-compact convex set D_β ⊂ L^1 of SIGNED kernels, D_β = core(h) − β·anticore(g) ⊂ {Z : E[Z] = 1−β}, where core(h) = {Z∈L^1 : E[Z1_A] ≤ h(P(A)) ∀A, E[Z]=1} and anticore(g) = {W∈L^1 : E[W1_A] ≥ g(P(A)) ∀A, E[W]=1}, such that V_β(X) = max_{Z∈D_β} E[ZX] and LR(X) = inf{β ∈ J : max_{Z∈D_β} E[ZX] ≤ 0} for all X ∈ 𝒳_g with LR(X) ∈ J (inf ∅ := sup J), i.e. LR is a Cherny–Madan/Drapeau–Kupper acceptability index whose acceptance cones A_β (nested and increasing in β on 𝒳_g) are generated by nested families of signed, mass-(1−β) kernels; the kernels are nonnegative for all β∈J iff h − βg is nondecreasing, which fails for large β unless h=g (F2 in dual form). Normalization of the dual variables (the step that fails without monotonicity in Drapeau–Kupper Thm 2.8 / BCDK Rem. 2.13) is supplied by the constant 1: for β>1, V_β(1) = 1−β < 0 and V_β is Lipschitz, so 1 is a norm-interior point of A_β and E[Z] = 1−β ≠ 0 on D_β.
PROOF SKETCH: Numbers refer to the digest entries of the Choquet/Schmeidler scholar [C-n], the signed-Choquet strand [S-n], the Kusuoka strand [K-n], and this strand [Q-n].
STEP 0 (uniqueness of a cash-additive associated family; justifies the bracketed reformulation of (A0)). Let {ρ_β},{ρ'_β} be cash-additive (ρ_β(X+c)=ρ_β(X)+c) and both in association with LR. Then LR(X − ρ_β(X)) = β, hence ρ'_β(X − ρ_β(X)) = 0, hence ρ'_β(X) = ρ_β(X). Given (A0), the family ρ_β := (a−βb)/(1−β) is cash-additive and in association: LR(X)=β ⟺ a(X)/b(X)=β (X∈D, b(X)≠0) ⟺ a(X)−βb(X)=0. Conversely an affine-in-β cash-additive family gives LR(X+c) = (a(X)+c)/(b(X)+c) with the pole at c=−b(X), i.e. (A0).
STEP 1 ((II)⟹(I)). (1a) a and b are well defined and cash-additive: the profile of X+c' is c ↦ LR(X+c'+c) = (c+c'−z(X))/(c+c'−p(X)); by uniqueness of zero and pole of a non-constant Möbius map (and of the pole on N), z(X+c') = z(X) − c', p(X+c') = p(X) − c', i.e. a(X+c')=a(X)+c', b(X+c')=b(X)+c'. (1b) Comonotone additivity of V_β, β∈{0,β_1}: for comonotone X,Y put X' = X+c, Y' = Y+c' with c := −V_β(X)/(1−β), c' := −V_β(Y)/(1−β) (so V_β(X')=V_β(Y')=0, i.e. LR(X')=LR(Y')=β when X',Y'∈D; if X' ∉ D use the pole clause (A3)(ii) instead). X',Y' are comonotone; by (A3)(i) LR(X'+Y')=β, so V_β(X+Y) + (c+c')(1−β) = 0, i.e. V_β(X+Y) = V_β(X)+V_β(Y). With β=0 this is additivity of a; with β=β_1 ≠ 0 it then gives additivity of b. (1c) a, b are law-invariant (A2) and uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous (A4). By [S-1]/[C-4] WWW Theorem 1 (law-invariant + comonotonic-additive + uniformly norm-continuous ⟺ signed Choquet integral; NO monotonicity), a = I_h, b = I_g for some h,g ∈ H. By WWW Lemma 2(ii), I_h(X+c) = I_h(X) + c·h(1); cash-additivity from (1a) forces h(1)=g(1)=1. (1d) D = {b ≠ 0} = {I_g ≠ 0} because X ∈ D ⟺ 0 ≠ p(X) ⟺ b(X) ≠ 0, and LR(X) = LR(X+0) = (0−z(X))/(0−p(X)) = a(X)/b(X) = I_h(X)/I_g(X). (1e) Uniqueness: if also LR = I_{h'}/I_{g'} on D' = {I_{g'}≠0}, then the pole of X+c is −I_{g'}(X), so I_{g'} = I_g on L^∞, and I_{h'}(X) = LR(X)I_{g'}(X) = I_h(X) on D, extended to L^∞ by cash shift; WWW Lemma 1 (h(1)=h'(1)) gives h=h', g=g'. (1f) Derived (A1): I_h, I_g are positively homogeneous (WWW Lemma 2(iii)), so LR(λX)=LR(X) and λD=D. Add-ons (M),(S): WWW Lemma 2(i) and Theorem 3 (iii)⟺(i); LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⟺ I_h ≤ I_g on 𝒳 ⟺ (cash shift) on L^∞ ⟺ h ≤ g by WWW Lemma 1; F2 as in the digest (nested layers 1_B+1_D).
STEP 2 ((I)⟹(II)). Let a=I_h, b=I_g. (A0): by WWW Lemma 2(ii), LR(X+c) = (I_h(X)+c)/(I_g(X)+c), a Möbius map of c with unique pole −I_g(X) (and ≡ 1 iff I_h(X)=I_g(X)). (A2): I_h, I_g are law-invariant by definition. (A3): I_{h−βg} is comonotonic additive for every β (it is a signed Choquet integral, [S-1]), hence {V_β=0} is closed under comonotone sums and poles add. (A4): WWW Lemma 4, |I_k(X)−I_k(Y)| ≤ TV(k)‖X−Y‖_∞. The mediant identity in (c) is comonotonic additivity of I_h and I_g divided by I_g(X+Y) = I_g(X)+I_g(Y).
STEP 3 (Theorem B1). V_β(X+c) = V_β(X) + c(1−β) with 1−β ≠ 0, so every sublevel set {V_β ≤ t} equals A_β − t/(1−β): A_β convex ⟺ all sublevel sets convex ⟺ V_β quasi-convex ⟺ (WWW Theorem 3 (v)⟺(i)⟺(iii)) h − βg concave ⟺ V_β subadditive. On 𝒳_g, V_β(X) = I_g(X)(LR(X)−β) with I_g(X)>0, so {LR ≤ β}∩𝒳_g = A_β∩𝒳_g, which is convex when A_β is; h−βg concave ∀β≥0 ⟺ h concave (β=0) and g convex (β→∞, after dividing by β) — and conversely h concave, g convex ⟹ h−βg concave ∀β≥0. The quasi-concave statement uses {LR ≥ β}∩𝒳_g = {V_β ≥ 0}∩𝒳_g and −V_β = I_{βg−h}. (Only '⟸' is claimed for quasi-convexity of LR restricted to 𝒳 itself; see weaknesses.)
STEP 4 (Theorem B2). For β∈J, V_β is sublinear (positively homogeneous + subadditive, Step 3), law-invariant and ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz; by Jouini–Schachermayer–Touzi Thm 2.2 [K-3] (concave + law-invariant + norm-usc ⟹ σ(L^∞,L^1)-usc, NO monotonicity, applied to −V_β) V_β is weak*-lsc, so by Fenchel–Moreau (equivalently the bipolar theorem for the weak*-closed convex cone A_β [Q-2 Volle; Q-4 Drapeau–Kupper Thm 2.7 with trivial preorder K={0}]) V_β(X) = sup_{Z∈D_β} E[ZX] with D_β = ∂V_β(0) ⊂ L^1 weak*-closed convex, L^1-bounded by TV(h−βg) (Lipschitz), hence weak*-compact and the sup is a max; E[Z] = 1−β on D_β because V_β(±1) = ±(1−β). Explicit form: h concave with h(1)=1 gives I_h(X) = max_{Z∈core(h)} E[ZX] (Schmeidler/Denneberg core representation of a supermodular capacity h∘P; no monotonicity of h needed), and g convex gives I_g(X) = min_{W∈anticore(g)} E[WX] (apply the same to −g); comonotonic additivity gives V_β = I_h − βI_g = max_{Z,W} E[(Z−βW)X], whose support set is the weak*-compact convex set core(h) − β·anticore(g); uniqueness of the support set of a weak*-lsc sublinear functional gives D_β = core(h) − β·anticore(g). Level-set form: on 𝒳_g, β ↦ V_β(X) is strictly decreasing and vanishes exactly at β = LR(X), so LR(X) = inf{β∈J : V_β(X) ≤ 0}; A_β ∩ 𝒳_g is nondecreasing in β. Positivity of kernels: D_β ⊂ L^1_+ ⟺ V_β monotone ⟺ h−βg nondecreasing (WWW Lemma 2(i)). Interiority of 1 for β>1 as stated in the conclusion.
MINIMALITY: (A0) cannot be dropped: LR̃ := ψ(I_h/I_g) with ψ(t)=t² (or any non-Möbius ψ) is law-invariant, scale-invariant, has the same level sets as I_h/I_g (so (A3)(i) holds, and the pole clause holds), and is continuous, but its cash profile c ↦ ((I_h+c)/(I_g+c))² is a degree-2 rational function, not Möbius, so it is not a ratio of two signed Choquet integrals with h(1)=g(1)=1 (otherwise its profile would be Möbius). More generally any Drapeau–Kupper/Cherny–Madan index inf{β : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} with a nested but non-pencil family (e.g. ρ_β = I_{h−βg−β²k}) satisfies all other axioms and is not a ratio; (A0) is exactly the 'pencil/affine-in-β' structure, which is the one thing the acceptability-index duality cannot deliver on its own. (A2) cannot be dropped: a(X) = ∫X dν_1, b(X) = ∫X dν_2 for non-law-invariant capacities (e.g. ν_2 = Q, an equivalent probability with dQ/dP non-constant, giving LR = E_P[X]/E_Q[X]) satisfies (A0),(A3),(A4) and is not of the form I_h/I_g (the representing capacities of a Choquet integral are unique, Schmeidler/CMMM Cor. 18 [C-2]); without (A2) the correct conclusion is the CMMM class 'ratio of two Choquet integrals w.r.t. set functions of bounded variation'. (A3) cannot be dropped: a(X) = E[X] + σ(X) (standard-deviation premium), b(X) = E[X]: a is cash-additive, positively homogeneous, law-invariant and ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz (|σ(X)−σ(Y)| ≤ ‖X−Y‖_∞), so (A0),(A2),(A4) hold, but a − βb = (1−β)E + σ is not comonotonic additive for any β (σ(1_B+1_D) < σ(1_B)+σ(1_D) for D ⊊ B), so no level set is closed under comonotone sums and LR = 1 + σ/E is not a Choquet ratio (WWW Lemma 1 uniqueness). Two levels in (A3) are needed: one level β gives only additivity of a − βb, which does not separate a and b (take a = I_h + βσ, b = I_g + σ). (A4) cannot be dropped: by WWW Remark 3 [S-1], a law-invariant comonotonic-additive functional that is norm-continuous but not uniformly so is represented by a distortion h of UNBOUNDED variation (e.g. h(t) = t + ε t sin(1/t), renormalized), lying outside H and without a Lipschitz constant; dropping all continuity admits pathological additive functionals (Hamel-type) with no integral representation. Atomlessness cannot be dropped: on a space with atoms law-invariant capacities need not be of the form h∘P and WWW Theorem 1 fails. What I CANNOT show: that (A4) can be replaced by a weaker, Greco/Wang–Young–Panjer-type truncation continuity stated on 𝒳 only (no non-monotone Greco theorem is known, [C-10]); that uniform continuity can be derived from a 'relevance/boundedness' condition as in Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei Thm 3 (plausible by the same route but not checked); and that the convexity hypothesis of Theorem B2 can be weakened to quasi-convexity of LR on 𝒳 alone (the passage from convexity of {LR ≤ β}∩𝒳 to convexity of A_β on L^∞ is open: for β<1 the cash shift that moves a signed X into 𝒳 may leave A_β).
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) Honesty about 'LR alone': (A0) is stated on LR but is strong — it is equivalent to LR being a ratio of two cash-additive functionals, and it requires LR to be defined on signed positions (translates of 𝒳 plus a pole), i.e. the extended loss ratio of Assa's Prop. 1 rather than the map on 𝒳 only. A theorem on 𝒳 alone would need the Sincov/mediant-odds route (decision-theory scholar) or the weighted-mediant axiom; both still smuggle in the pencil structure. (2) Monotonicity of ρ and Π (Assa's distortion setting) is invisible in LR (F2) and appears only as add-on (M) on the endpoints; the theorem's base class is the SIGNED Choquet pair, in which the numerator and even the premium may be non-monotone. (3) Theorem B is proved on L^∞ or on the cone 𝒳_g; the '⟹' half of quasi-convexity of LR restricted to 𝒳 is not established. (4) Theorem B2 assumes h−βg concave on an interval of β (a pair hypothesis); outside it the level cones are non-convex and only the Penot–Volle/Drapeau–Kupper representation with the trivial preorder (sup over the full dual of a risk function, Q-2/Q-4) survives, giving no explicit kernel sets. (5) The core/anticore description with non-monotone h is used for concave h with h(1)=1 (supermodular signed capacity); I rely on the standard core theorem (Schmeidler/Denneberg) extended to signed capacities via CMMM Thm 13 — the signed-capacity version should be double-checked, though the abstract weak*-compact set D_β exists regardless. (6) Identifiability: V(β,X)=ρ_β(X) is only a zero-set ('functional') identification; by Wang–Wei Thm 3.2/Cor 3.3 and Steinwart et al., LR within this class has CxLS/is elicitable only if h=g, so no identification/elicitability axiom can enter the characterization (F6 downgraded). (7) Sources: WWW 2020 (Thm 1, Lemmas 1,2,4, Thm 3, Remark 3), JST 2006 Thm 2.2 and the digest's CMMM/Drapeau–Kupper/Volle statements were read online by panel members; Schmeidler/Denneberg core facts are classical but quoted from memory. WebSearch was disabled for this organization and most publisher sites were blocked, so no additional iff theorem on ratios of Choquet integrals could be located beyond the digest; to my knowledge none exists, and Theorem A is new in the sense that it combines Assa's association with WWW Thm 1 via the Möbius profile.

=== PROPOSAL 6: Pencil theorem for the loss ratio: the zero–pole (Möbius) characterization of ratios of distortion Choquet integrals, with monotonicity imposed only at the two endpoints of the associated family (Return / star-shaped / scale-free risk measures scholar (Bellini–Laeven–Rosazza Gianin return risk measures; Castagnoli–Cattelan–Maccheroni–Tebaldi–Wang; Laeven–Rosazza Gianin–Zullino; Jia–Xia–Zhao; Herdegen–Khan; Han–Lin–Wang DQ; Cherny–Madan). Search note for this run: WebSearch is still disabled for the organization (HTTP 403 "feature not enabled", re-tested today); no new primary source could be opened, so every citation below refers to the panel digest by strand and theorem number, with the confidence level recorded there.) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with ‖·‖_∞; L^∞_+\{0} the nonnegative bounded losses of Assa (2024). Because Assa's Prop. 1 already evaluates LR at translates X − m, the object axiomatized is the EXTENDED loss ratio LR : L^∞ → R̂_⋆ := R ∪ {∞} ∪ {⋆}, where R̂ = R ∪ {∞} is the projective line (value ∞ = "premium exhausted, risk not") and ⋆ is the symbol "undefined, 0/0". Reading convention: LR(X) = [ρ(X) : Π(X)] is the projective ratio of a risk functional and a premium functional, so LR(X)=∞ iff Π(X)=0≠ρ(X) and LR(X)=⋆ iff ρ(X)=Π(X)=0; the ordinary loss ratio is the restriction to L^∞_+\{0}, where it is real-valued iff Π>0 there. Cash profile of X: φ_X(c) := LR(X+c), c ∈ R. Break-even set: N := {X : φ_X(c) = 1 for all but one c}. Choquet convention (survival form, cash-additive extension to L^∞): C_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{-∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1))dx for h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0; a distortion function is a nondecreasing h with h(0)=0, h(1)=1. Associated (canonical) family: ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β), β ≠ 1; its endpoints are ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_∞ := lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = Π. Comonotone X,Y: (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 a.s.⊗a.s.; on an atomless space every comonotone pair can be written (f(U),k(U)) with U uniform and f,k nondecreasing.
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A1) MÖBIUS CASH PROFILE (= 'LR is a projective ratio of two cash-additive functionals'; equivalently, in Assa's language: LR is in association with a family {ρ_β}_{β≠1} of cash-additive functionals, ρ_β(X+c)=ρ_β(X)+c, such that β ↦ (1−β)ρ_β(X) is affine and LR(X−m)=β has exactly one solution m=ρ_β(X) for X ∉ N). Precisely: for every X ∈ L^∞ exactly one of the following holds. (i) [off break-even] there are reals ρ(X) ≠ Π(X) with LR(X+c) = (ρ(X)+c)/(Π(X)+c) ∈ R̂ for all c ∈ R (value ∞ at the pole c = −Π(X), value 0 at the zero c = −ρ(X)); (ii) [break-even, X ∈ N] there is a real π(X) with LR(X+c) = 1 for all c ≠ −π(X) and LR(X − π(X)) = ⋆; in this case set ρ(X) := Π(X) := π(X). (The zero and the pole/⋆-point of a non-constant Möbius map are unique, so ρ and Π are functions of LR: −ρ(X) is the cash that zeroes the loss ratio of X, −Π(X) the cash that exhausts the premium.) (A2) (A3) COMONOTONE BREAK-EVEN ADDITIVITY: the two capital functionals read off LR in (A1) are additive on comonotone pairs: ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) and Π(X+Y)=Π(X)+Π(Y) whenever X,Y are comonotone. LR-level content (Lemma M in the proof): on pairs off N it is exactly the equality case of the comonotone mediant property — 'if X and Y are comonotone and each is loaded with cash to the same target loss ratio β ≠ 1, then the pooled position with the pooled cash has loss ratio β' — and on the positive-premium cone it is the premium-weighted mediant identity LR(X+Y) = (Π(X)LR(X)+Π(Y)LR(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)); on N it says that the premium-exhausting cash (the ⋆-point) is additive and that break-even is comonotonically absorbing (X ∈ N, Y comonotone ⇒ X+Y ∈ N iff Y ∈ N). (A3) (A4) LAW INVARIANCE of the extended loss ratio: X =_d Y ⇒ LR(X) = LR(Y) (on all of L^∞; equivalently ρ, Π law-invariant, since X+c =_d Y+c). (A4) (A5) ENDPOINT MONOTONICITY, stated through the cash profile: if X ≤ Y a.s. then the loss-ratio-zeroing cash and the premium-exhausting cash of X are at least those of Y, i.e. ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y) and Π(X) ≤ Π(Y). This is monotonicity of the two ENDPOINTS ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_∞ = Π of the associated family only; no monotonicity of LR (impossible, F2) and none of the intermediate ρ_β (false, F2) is assumed. (A5) (A2) SCALE INVARIANCE LR(λX)=LR(X), λ>0, is listed for comparison with Assa's Prop. 1 but is NOT an axiom: it is a consequence of (A1)+(A3)+(A5) (monotone comonotone-additive functionals on bounded functions are positively homogeneous: Denneberg / Schmeidler, Choquet strand Thm [1],[10]), and of (A1)+(A3)+(A5′) in the signed variant (CMMM Lemma 14(iii), Choquet strand Thm [2]; Wang–Wei–Willmot Lemma 2(iii), signed strand Thm [1]).
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: (A5) is formally stated on LR (through the zero and the pole/⋆-point of its cash profile) but is MATERIALLY a hypothesis on the pair: it is monotonicity of the two endpoints ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_∞ = Π of the associated family, and by F2 it cannot be replaced by any monotonicity property of LR itself. Signed variant (A5′): ρ and Π uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous (equivalently: of bounded variation / Lipschitz, given (A3): CMMM 2012 Lemma 14, 2015 Lemma 18) — again a property of the endpoints. | (A1)(ii), the ⋆-convention on the break-even set N: it prescribes that for a break-even risk the loss ratio becomes undefined (0/0) at exactly one cash level, which is how the premium Π is read off LR on N. This is a modelling convention about what the 'extended loss ratio' records on N; if one insists on LR ≡ 1 on N (no ⋆), then Π on N is NOT determined by LR, and one must either (a) add the data Π|_N, or (b) replace it by continuity of LR into R̂ plus the absorbing clause of (A3) and accept uniqueness of (h,g) only when h ≠ g (F3). Nothing else is assumed on the pair or on the family; the signed-Choquet structure of all intermediate ρ_β (F7) is a consequence.
CONCLUSION: THEOREM. Let LR : L^∞ → R̂_⋆. The following are equivalent. (I) LR satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4), (A5). (II) There exist distortion functions h, g : [0,1] → [0,1] (nondecreasing, h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1) such that LR(X) = [C_h(X) : C_g(X)] for all X ∈ L^∞, i.e. LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) whenever C_g(X) ≠ 0, LR(X)=∞ when C_g(X)=0≠C_h(X), LR(X)=⋆ when C_h(X)=C_g(X)=0. UNIQUENESS: in (II) the pair (h,g) is unique, h(p) = ρ(1_A) and g(p) = Π(1_A) for P(A)=p, where ρ, Π are the zero- and pole-functionals of LR defined in (A1); in particular LR on L^∞_+\{0} together with its cash profiles determines (h,g) even when h = g (LR ≡ 1 off the ⋆-set), and LR on L^∞_+\{0} alone (ratio values only) determines (h,g) iff h ≠ g (F3). CLASS: exactly the ratios of two Yaari/Denneberg/Wang–Young–Panjer distortion functionals ('distortion pairs'); in Assa's terms LR is in association with the canonical pencil ρ_β = C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} of SIGNED Choquet integrals (F7), whose two monotone endpoints are ρ_0 = C_h and ρ_∞ = C_g, and ρ_β is monotone iff h − βg is nondecreasing (Wang–Wei–Willmot Lemma 2(i)). ADD-ONS, each an iff inside (II): (a) [signed variant] replace (A5) by (A5′) ⇔ h, g of bounded variation with h(1)=g(1)=1 (signed Choquet pair; Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 Thm 1), and then (A5) ⇔ h, g nondecreasing; (b) Π > 0 on L^∞_+\{0} (LR real-valued on nonnegative losses) ⇔ g > 0 on (0,1]; (c) LR ≥ 1 on L^∞_+ ⇔ h ≥ g, LR ≤ 1 ⇔ h ≤ g (WWW Lemma 1 / ASTIN Prop. 2(i)); (d) ρ (resp. Π) subadditive ⇔ h (resp. g) concave (Wang–Dhaene 1998; Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93; WWW Thm 3), giving the 'coherent pair' and 'Kusuoka pair' subclasses ρ = ∫ES_α dμ_ρ, Π = ∫ES_α dμ_Π with unique μ's (Shapiro Thm 3.1); (e) for β ≠ 1, {X ∈ L^∞ : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} is convex ⇔ h − βg concave (WWW Thm 3 (iv)⇔(v) plus cash-additivity of ρ_β with slope 1), hence on {Π>0}: LR quasi-convex ⇔ h concave and g convex ('conic picture'), LR quasi-concave ⇔ h convex and g concave (this corrects F4's 'actuarial picture': ρ = E, g concave gives a quasi-CONCAVE loss ratio); (f) LR pointwise monotone on L^∞_+ ⇔ h = g ⇔ LR ≡ 1 off ⋆ (F2); (g) [return-risk-measure reading] on L^∞_+, ρ and Π are return risk measures (monotone, positively homogeneous, normalized; Bellini–Laeven–Rosazza Gianin 2018, star-shaped strand Thm [7]), and log LR(e^X) = ρ̂(X) − Π̂(X) is the difference of the two monetary risk measures ρ̂ = log ρ∘exp, Π̂ = log Π∘exp — a cash-invariant 'generalized coefficient of variation' (LR − 1 = C_{h−g}/C_g, a signed-Choquet variability functional over a premium, Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei Thm 3 template), which is the structural reason LR can never be monotone and why its natural axioms are comonotone-additive rather than acceptance-cone based.
PROOF SKETCH: NOTATION. Theorem numbers refer to the digest: [C-k] Choquet/Schmeidler strand, [S-k] signed-Choquet/R. Wang strand, [K-k] Kusuoka strand, [R-k] return/star-shaped strand, [A-k] author/critic strand.

(I) ⇒ (II).
Step 0 (ρ, Π are well defined on all of L^∞ and are functions of LR). For X ∉ N, c ↦ (ρ+c)/(Π+c) with ρ ≠ Π is a bijection of R̂, so its zero −ρ(X) and pole −Π(X) are unique; for X ∈ N the ⋆-point −π(X) is unique by (A1)(ii). Hence ρ, Π : L^∞ → R are determined by LR, and LR(X) = φ_X(0) = [ρ(X) : Π(X)] for every X (for X ∈ N: 1 if π(X) ≠ 0, ⋆ if π(X) = 0).
Step 1 (cash additivity). φ_{X+m}(c) = φ_X(m+c); comparing zeros, poles and ⋆-points gives ρ(X+m) = ρ(X)+m, Π(X+m) = Π(X)+m, and N + R = N.
Step 2 (comonotone additivity, normalization). By (A3) ρ and Π are additive on comonotone pairs; with Y = X = 0 this gives ρ(0) = Π(0) = 0, hence by Step 1 ρ(1) = Π(1) = 1, so 1 ∈ N and all constants are in N (constant losses are break-even). This replaces the normalization axiom.
Step 3 (monotonicity, Lipschitz, positive homogeneity). By (A5) ρ, Π are monotone on L^∞; monotone + cash-additive ⇒ 1-Lipschitz for ‖·‖_∞ (X ≤ Y + ‖X−Y‖_∞). Monotone + comonotone additive on bounded functions ⇒ positively homogeneous (Denneberg Ch. 13 / Schmeidler, [C-1],[C-10]; or directly: additivity on the comonotone pair (X,X) gives ρ(nX)=nρ(X), hence ρ(qX)=qρ(X) for rationals q>0, and monotonicity extends to all λ>0). This yields (A2) as a consequence: LR(λX) = [λρ(X):λΠ(X)] = LR(X).
Step 4 (Schmeidler). ρ is monotone and comonotone additive on L^∞ (lift to bounded measurable functions, constant on a.s.-classes). By Schmeidler (1986) [C-1, verbatim in CMMM 2015 Thm 2; K-7 for the norm-continuous version], ρ(X) = ∫ X dν with the unique capacity ν(A) = ρ(1_A), ν(Ω) = ρ(1) = 1, and ∫X dν = ∫_0^∞ ν(X ≥ t)dt + ∫_{-∞}^0 (ν(X ≥ t) − 1)dt; no continuity hypothesis is needed. Likewise Π = ∫ · dν′ with ν′(A) = Π(1_A).
Step 5 (law invariance ⇒ distortion). By (A4) ρ(1_A) depends only on P(A); on an atomless space every p ∈ [0,1] is attained, so h(p) := ρ(1_A), P(A)=p, is a well-defined nondecreasing function with h(0)=0, h(1)=1 and ν = h∘P (Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i) [C-7]; Kusuoka Thm 7 / Yaari [K-2],[C-8]). Since P(X ≥ t) = P(X > t) for all but countably many t, ρ = C_h. Same for Π = C_g.
Step 6 (conclusion). LR(X) = [ρ(X) : Π(X)] = [C_h(X) : C_g(X)] for all X (Step 0). Uniqueness: h(p) = ρ(1_A) and ρ is the zero-functional of LR.
Step 7 (association). With ρ_β := (C_h − βC_g)/(1−β) = C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (linearity of h ↦ C_h; F7), for X with LR(X) ≠ ⋆ and β ≠ 1: LR(X) = β ⇔ C_h(X) = βC_g(X) ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0; and LR(X−m) = β ⇔ m = ρ_β(X), which is Assa's Prop. 1 with the sign fixed as in [A]. Each ρ_β is law-invariant, comonotone additive, cash-additive with slope 1 and TV((h−βg)/(1−β))-Lipschitz (WWW Lemma 4 [S-1]; Wang–Wang–Wei Remark 2 [C-5]); ρ_β is monotone iff h − βg is nondecreasing (WWW Lemma 2(i)), which fails for large β unless h = g — the precise content of F2; ρ_0 = C_h and ρ_β → C_g as β → ∞.

(II) ⇒ (I). Let ρ := C_h, Π := C_g, LR := [ρ : Π]. (A1): ρ, Π are cash-additive with slope h(1) = g(1) = 1 (WWW Lemma 2(ii) [S-1]; F1), so for ρ(X) ≠ Π(X) the profile is (ρ(X)+c)/(Π(X)+c), never ⋆ (numerator and denominator cannot vanish together); for ρ(X) = Π(X) =: π the profile is [π+c : π+c] = 1 for c ≠ −π and ⋆ at c = −π: exactly one ⋆-point. (A3): Choquet integrals are comonotone additive (Schmeidler/Denneberg; WWW Thm 1 'if' part). (A4): C_h depends on X through its survival function. (A5): C_h, C_g are monotone because h, g are nondecreasing (WWW Lemma 2(i); ASTIN Prop. 2(ii) [C-5]). Positive homogeneity (WWW Lemma 2(iii)) gives (A2).

LEMMA M (role of the mediant/betweenness property). Assume (A1). (i) For comonotone X, Y ∉ N on the same side of break-even (sign(Π(X)−ρ(X)) = sign(Π(Y)−ρ(Y))), the two-sided comonotone mediant inequality min{LR(X′),LR(Y′)} ≤ LR(X′+Y′) ≤ max{LR(X′),LR(Y′)} on the positive-premium cone {Π>0} (F1) implies ρ(X+Y) = ρ(X)+ρ(Y) and Π(X+Y) = Π(X)+Π(Y). Proof: for β ≠ 1 with sign(1−β) = sign(Π(X)−ρ(X)) put X′ = X + c_β(X), c_β(X) = (βΠ(X)−ρ(X))/(1−β) = −ρ_β(X); then LR(X′) = β and Π(X′) = (Π(X)−ρ(X))/(1−β) > 0, similarly Y′; the mediant inequality forces LR(X′+Y′) = β, so X+Y ∉ N (otherwise LR(X+Y+c) ∈ {1,⋆} ≠ β) and by uniqueness c_β(X+Y) = c_β(X)+c_β(Y); two admissible values of β give additivity of ρ and Π. (ii) Conversely, under (A3), for comonotone X, Y with Π(X), Π(Y) > 0: LR(X+Y) = wLR(X) + (1−w)LR(Y), w = Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) (premium-weighted mediant; Furman–Zitikis/Wang layer form), which implies the inequality. (iii) Hence, whenever h − g has constant sign (LR ≥ 1 or LR ≤ 1 on L^∞_+, add-on (c)), (A3) off N is EQUIVALENT, given (A1), to the comonotone mediant inequality of F1; the Dekel/Chew reading of the decision-theory strand (betweenness ⇔ association with linear-on-comonotone-cones functionals; weak substitution ⇔ affine pencil) is the ordinal version of this lemma, and (A1) is the cardinal (Aczél/Sincov) ingredient that removes the projective ambiguity.

LEMMA N (what the ⋆-convention does). Without (A1)(ii) — i.e. if LR records only '1' on N — the map LR* := 1 on finitely-valued X, := C_h/C_g otherwise (h > g on (0,1)) satisfies every axiom restricted to L^∞ \ N (Möbius profile, comonotone additivity on pairs off N — the comonotone sum of two infinitely-valued nondecreasing functions of U is infinitely valued —, law invariance, endpoint monotonicity) but is not a Choquet ratio: LR*(1_D) = 1 ≠ h(p)/g(p). The ⋆-convention (or, alternatively, continuity of LR into R̂ together with the absorbing clause of (A3)) is what excludes it; with the alternative route one extends ρ, Π from the dense set L^∞\N by 1-Lipschitz continuity along X + s1_{D′}, D′ = {U > 1−p*}, 1_{D′} ∉ N, which leaves N for all but one s.
MINIMALITY: Each remaining axiom is independent of the others (all examples are on an atomless space, with ρ, Π read off the cash profile as in (A1)):
• (A1) Möbius profile: LR := ψ∘(C_h/C_g) with ψ(t) = t³ (a homeomorphism of R̂ fixing 0, 1, ∞) keeps the same zeros and poles, hence satisfies (A3), (A4), (A5) and scale invariance, but its cash profile (h(p)+c)³/(g(p)+c)³ on X = 1_D is not of the form (a+c)/(b+c); equivalently it is in association with the NON-affine family ρ − β^{1/3}Π. So (A1) is exactly what forces the pencil (affine-in-β) structure; without it the index/family duality only yields a nested family of cones (Cherny–Madan/Drapeau–Kupper/Han–Lin–Wang type) with no single pair (ρ,Π).
• (A3) comonotone additivity: Π := max(C_g, C_{g′}) with g(p) = min(2p,1), g′(p) = √p, ρ := C_h. Π is monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, law-invariant (a supremum of two distortion premia, Song–Yan class [K-14]) but not comonotone additive: for D ⊂ B, P(D)=1/16, P(B)=1/2, Π(1_D)=1/4, Π(1_B)=1, Π(1_B+1_D)=max(9/8, √½+¼)=9/8 ≠ 5/4. LR = C_h/Π satisfies (A1), (A4), (A5) but is not a ratio of Choquet integrals (its pole functional is not comonotone additive).
• (A4) law invariance: LR := E_Q[X]/E[X] with Q ~ P, Q ≠ P. Both functionals are linear, monotone, cash-additive; (A1), (A3), (A5) hold, but LR is not law-invariant under P, and the representation is ∫X dν_1/∫X dν_2 with two capacities (CMMM 2012 Cor. 18 / Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida [C-2],[C-11]) that are not of the form h∘P. Law invariance is what turns capacities into distortions (Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i)).
• (A5) endpoint monotonicity: ρ := I_h with h(p) = p + ¼ sin(2πp) (bounded variation, h(0)=0, h(1)=1, h′ takes negative values), Π := C_g. ρ is law-invariant, comonotone additive, cash-additive, Lipschitz but not monotone (WWW Lemma 2(i)); LR = I_h/C_g satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4) and (A5′) but not (A5), and is a signed-Choquet ratio (add-on (a)) that is not a distortion ratio. This also shows that (A5) is needed only at the endpoints: the intermediate ρ_β of a genuine distortion pair are themselves non-monotone signed Choquet integrals.
• (A5′) in the signed variant: by WWW Remark 3 [S-1], norm continuity without uniformity still gives a representation of the same form but with h not of bounded variation, so uniform continuity is exactly what is needed for the BV conclusion; I cannot exhibit an explicit, constructive law-invariant comonotone-additive functional that is discontinuous (such examples would need a non-measurable/Hamel-type construction), so necessity of (A5′) is established only at the BV boundary.
• (A1)(ii) ⋆-convention (control of the break-even set): Lemma N above (LR*) shows that axioms restricted to L^∞\N are insufficient; and if ⋆ is discarded, uniqueness fails exactly when h = g (every pair (g,g) gives LR ≡ 1), which is F3's 'unless LR ≡ 1'.
• Atomless space: needed to attain every p ∈ [0,1] by an event, so that h is defined and unique on all of [0,1] (with atoms the representation holds with h defined only on the range of P, and WWW Thm 1 for the signed variant is stated on atomless spaces).
• Scale invariance (A2) is NOT needed as an axiom (derived; Step 3), and pointwise monotonicity of LR is never used: assuming it forces h = g (F2, nested-layer argument 1_B ≤ 1_B+1_D ≤ 2·1_B).
• Continuity of LR is not needed in the monotone version (Schmeidler needs none; monotone + cash-additive ⇒ 1-Lipschitz), in contrast with the signed version.
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: 1. (A1) is strong: it is the ratio structure itself (projective Möbius cash profile = 'LR is a quotient of two cash-additive functionals'), and the theorem's genuine content is (a) that the pair is recoverable from LR (zero, pole, ⋆-point), (b) that monotonicity enters only at the two endpoints of the pencil, and (c) Schmeidler + law invariance. A reader who regards (A1) as 'assuming the conclusion' should note that it is equivalent to Assa's association with a cash-additive, affine-in-β family, i.e. to the conjunction of Assa's Prop. 1 hypotheses, and that the ψ∘ratio counterexample shows no weaker betweenness-type axiom (Dekel/Chew ordinal version) can replace it without a cardinal (Sincov-type) supplement.
2. The ⋆-convention on the break-even set is a modelling choice. Without it, one must assume continuity of LR into R̂ plus the comonotone-absorbing clause for N, and uniqueness of (h,g) holds only for h ≠ g (F3); I verified the extension argument (1-Lipschitz extension along X + s1_{D′}) but it is more delicate than the main line and I have presented it only as Lemma N.
3. The mediant/betweenness inequality of F1 is shown (Lemma M) to be equivalent to (A3) off N only for comonotone pairs on the same side of break-even; when h − g changes sign I do not know whether the inequality alone implies full comonotone additivity, so for sign-changing pairs (A3) is formally stronger than F1's mediant property.
4. The theorem is on L^∞; for L^p or Orlicz losses one must add Wang–Wang–Wei's continuity at infinity [C-5] to the endpoints, and Shapiro's warning [K-11] that comonotonicity ⇔ single generator fails at p = ∞ does not affect the L^∞ statement but matters for Kusuoka-pair add-ons on L^p.
5. All quasi-convexity add-ons are inherited from WWW Thm 3 on L^∞ and are stated on {Π>0}; the restriction to L^∞_+ requires nonnegative test variables and I have not re-proved the 'only if' directions there (same caveat as the Kusuoka strand).
6. No new primary source could be consulted in this run (WebSearch disabled, 403); the literature claims rest on the digest, where Schmeidler 1986, Wang–Young–Panjer 1997, Yaari 1987, Bellini–Laeven–Rosazza Gianin 2018 are quoted from memory (medium confidence) while Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020, Wang–Wang–Wei 2020, CMMM 2012/2015, Han–Lin–Wang and Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei were read in full (high confidence). I found, like the other strands, no published iff characterization of a ratio of two Choquet integrals or of two coherent risk measures; the closest results are the diversification-quotient Theorem 1 (level search over a monotone family, monotone axioms — inapplicable because of F2) and the generalized-PELVE existence/uniqueness results.

=== PROPOSAL 7: Zero–pole (Möbius cash-profile) characterization of the loss ratio: LR = C_h/C_g with (h,g) a pair of distinct normalized distortion functions, g > 0 on (0,1] (Performance-ratio axiomatics scholar (gain-loss / Omega / Sharpe / RAROC / conic-finance / acceptability-index strand)) ===
SETTING: Probability space (Ω,F,P) ATOMLESS. L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with the sup-norm ‖·‖_∞ (a.s. equivalence classes). Domain of the loss ratio: 𝒳 := L^∞_+ \ {0} = {X ∈ L^∞ : X ≥ 0 a.s., P(X>0) > 0}, which is closed under positive scaling λX (λ>0), upward cash shifts X + c (c ≥ 0) and sums; it is NOT closed under downward shifts, so only upward shifts are used (this keeps everything inside Assa's domain; the extended-domain version LR(X−m) is discussed under 'mediant'). LR: 𝒳 → ℝ is a real-valued map (finiteness is part of the hypothesis; it forces the premium to be strictly positive on 𝒳). Conventions: survival-form Choquet integral C_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx for X ≥ 0, extended to L^∞ by C_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1))dx; a (normalized) distortion function is h:[0,1]→[0,1] nondecreasing with h(0)=0, h(1)=1 (no continuity assumed; h may jump, e.g. VaR-type). Two random variables are comonotone if (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 a.s.⊗a.s. The CASH PROFILE of X ∈ 𝒳 is the map c ↦ LR(X + c), c ∈ [0,∞). The FAIR SET is N := {X ∈ 𝒳 : LR(X) = 1}. For a non-constant Möbius map c ↦ (a+c)/(b+c) (a ≠ b, b > 0) the numbers a and b are uniquely determined by its values on [0,∞) (elementary: (a+c)(b'+c) = (a'+c)(b+c) ∀c ≥ 0 forces a+b' = a'+b and ab' = a'b, hence (a−a')(b'−a') = 0, and a' = b' is excluded because the profile is not ≡ 1); −a is the ZERO and −b the POLE of the analytic continuation of the profile to c ∈ ℝ. Throughout, the pair (ρ,Π) is NOT part of the data: it is reconstructed from LR as (zero, pole) of the cash profile.
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A0) NON-DEGENERACY: the fair set N = {X ∈ 𝒳 : LR(X) = 1} has empty interior in (𝒳, ‖·‖_∞), i.e. {LR ≠ 1} is dense in 𝒳. [In the representation this is exactly h ≠ g, i.e. LR ≢ 1; it is needed only for uniqueness of (h,g) and for the continuity-extension device in the proof.] (A2) (A1) MÖBIUS CASH PROFILE (unit-slope ratio axiom): for every X ∈ 𝒳 with LR(X) ≠ 1 there are real numbers a_X ≠ b_X such that LR(X + c) = (a_X + c)/(b_X + c) for all c ≥ 0; and LR(X + c) = 1 for all c ≥ 0 whenever LR(X) = 1. [Economic reading: adding one unit of sure loss to X raises both the risk capital and the premium by one unit; this is Assa's cash-additivity of the associated family (Prop. 1) read as a property of LR. Since LR is real-valued, b_X > 0 automatically (c = 0 is not a pole). We write ρ(X) := a_X (the zero), Π(X) := b_X (the pole) on 𝒳 \ N.] (A3) (A2) LAW INVARIANCE: LR(X) = LR(Y) whenever X and Y have the same distribution. (A4) (A3) MONOTONICITY OF THE ZERO AND OF THE POLE (endpoint monotonicity): for X, Y ∈ 𝒳 \ N with X ≤ Y a.s.: ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y) and Π(X) ≤ Π(Y). [This is the ONLY place where monotonicity enters; it is a condition on the two numbers that LR's cash profile determines, never on the values of LR themselves (pointwise monotonicity of LR is impossible, F2). Under (A0),(A1),(A3), ρ and Π are 1-Lipschitz on 𝒳 \ N and extend uniquely by continuity to 𝒳; the extended maps are used in (A4).] (A5) (A4) PREMIUM-WEIGHTED COMONOTONE MEDIANT (comonotone additivity of the pole and of the zero): for all comonotone X, Y ∈ 𝒳 (extended Π): Π(X+Y) = Π(X) + Π(Y) and LR(X+Y) = [Π(X)·LR(X) + Π(Y)·LR(Y)] / [Π(X) + Π(Y)]. [Equivalently: ρ = LR·Π and Π are both additive on comonotone pairs. It implies the two-sided unweighted mediant property of F1, min{LR(X),LR(Y)} ≤ LR(X+Y) ≤ max{LR(X),LR(Y)}, and is implied by the 'comonotone level-set closure' LR(X+c) = LR(Y+c') = β ≠ 1 ⇒ LR(X+Y+c+c') = β for all comonotone pairs whose values lie on the same side of 1 (see proof, Lemma M).] (A6) (A5) CONTINUITY: LR is continuous on (𝒳, ‖·‖_∞). [Only needed at points of N; off N it is a consequence of (A0),(A1),(A3).] (A7) DERIVED, NOT ASSUMED: scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X), λ > 0, follows from (A1),(A3),(A4) (Cauchy's equation on a cone plus Lipschitz continuity); so does the 'translation to break-even' property c ↦ LR(X+c) monotone with limit 1, and the unweighted comonotone mediant inequality of F1.
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: None in the literal sense: (ρ,Π) and the associated family {ρ_β} are reconstructed from LR, so every hypothesis is a hypothesis on LR. HONEST ACCOUNTING of what is 'on the pair in disguise': (i) (A1) with UNIT slopes is a normalization (h(1) = g(1) = 1, i.e. cash-additivity of both ρ and Π); without it LR is invariant under (ρ,Π) ↦ (φρ,φΠ) and the pair is not identifiable from LR (replace unit slopes by a common slope α>0 and one gets h(1)=g(1)=α). (ii) (A3) is a monotonicity requirement on the two endpoint functionals ρ = ρ_0 and Π = lim_{β→∞} ρ_β of the canonical family, i.e. on the pair; it cannot be expressed through inequalities between values of LR (F2). (iii) (A4) is stated for the extended Π on all of 𝒳 (including the fair set N), because comonotone additivity on 𝒳 \ N does not obviously propagate to N by density along comonotone directions. (iv) Values of (ρ,Π) on N are fixed by continuity, which needs (A0) and (A5). Everything else (comonotone additivity, law invariance, continuity, positivity of Π, the signed-Choquet form of the intermediate ρ_β, scale invariance) is either an axiom on LR or derived.
CONCLUSION: THEOREM. Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless and LR: 𝒳 = L^∞_+ \ {0} → ℝ. The following are equivalent.
(I) LR satisfies (A0)–(A5).
(II) There exist nondecreasing functions h, g: [0,1] → [0,1] with h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1, h ≠ g, and g(p) > 0 for every p ∈ (0,1], such that
      LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx   for all X ∈ 𝒳.
Exact class of pairs: (ρ,Π) = (C_h, C_g) with h, g normalized distortion functions (Yaari/Wang–Young–Panjer distortion risk measure and distortion premium, not necessarily continuous or concave), h ≠ g, and the premium strictly positive on 𝒳 (g > 0 on (0,1]; this excludes VaR-type premiums g = 1_{(α,1]} but allows VaR-type risk measures h).
UNIQUENESS: the pair (h,g) is uniquely determined by LR; in fact ρ(X) = C_h(X) and Π(X) = C_g(X) are the zero and the pole of the cash profile of X for every X ∉ N, and h(p) = ρ(1_B), g(p) = Π(1_B) for P(B) = p. Uniqueness holds even within the wider class of signed normalized pairs (h,g ∈ BV, h(1) = g(1) = 1, C_g > 0 on 𝒳). If (A0) is dropped, LR ≡ 1 = C_h/C_h is the only additional solution and uniqueness fails exactly there (consistent with F3).
ASSOCIATED FAMILY (association in Assa's sense is a CONSEQUENCE): for β ∈ [0,∞) \ {1} put ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) = C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)}, a signed Choquet integral with distortion (h − βg)/(1−β) (F7). Then LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0; ρ_β is cash-additive with unit slope, positively homogeneous, law-invariant, comonotone-additive and Lipschitz with constant ≤ (1+β)/|1−β|; ρ_0 = ρ, ρ_β → Π as β → ∞; −ρ_β(X) is the unique cash amount m with LR(X + m) = β whenever X + m ∈ 𝒳; {ρ_β} is the UNIQUE cash-additive family in association with LR; ρ_β is monotone iff h − βg is nondecreasing (WWW Lemma 2(i)) — in general it is not (F2) and no axiom is imposed on it.
ADD-ONS (each an iff on the endpoints, none on LR values): h, g continuous ⇔ LR continuous along bounded a.s. convergence; h concave ⇔ ρ subadditive (coherent); g concave ⇔ Π subadditive; LR ≤ 1 on 𝒳 ⇔ h ≤ g; for β ≠ 1, {LR ≤ β} ∩ 𝒳 is convex iff h − βg is concave (WWW Thm 3 + cash-additivity), so LR is quasi-convex on 𝒳 iff h concave and g convex (conic picture) and quasi-concave iff h convex and g concave (actuarial picture, correcting F4's second clause).
SIGNED VARIANT: replace (A3) by (A3') 'ρ and Π are uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous on 𝒳 \ N'. Then (I') ⇔ (II') with h, g of bounded variation, h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, h ≠ g, C_g > 0 on 𝒳 (ratio of two signed Choquet integrals), via Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020) Thm 1 instead of Schmeidler; uniqueness as above; (A3) is then equivalent to h, g nondecreasing.
PROOF SKETCH: NOTATION. Cite: [Ch-k] = Choquet/Schmeidler scholar item k; [SC-k] = Signed Choquet/Ruodu Wang strand item k; [Ins-k] = Insurance-pricing item k; [DT-k] = Decision-theory item k; [PR-k] = Performance-ratio item k; [Au-k] = Assa/critic item k.

PART A: (I) ⇒ (II).
Step 1 (zero and pole; cash additivity). For X ∉ N, (A1) gives unique (ρ(X), Π(X)) = (a_X, b_X), with Π(X) > 0 because LR(X) = a_X/b_X is finite. For c ≥ 0, the profile of X + c is c' ↦ (a_X + c + c')/(b_X + c + c'), so by uniqueness of Möbius parameters ρ(X + c) = ρ(X) + c and Π(X + c) = Π(X) + c; also X + c ∉ N. (This is the cash-shift recovery of [PR-2] BCZ Thm 4.6 / [PR-1] Cherny–Madan eq. (4.3) specialised to a Möbius index; it is Assa's Prop. 1 read backwards.)
Step 2 (law invariance). If X =_d Y then X + c =_d Y + c, so the profiles coincide by (A2) and ρ, Π are law-invariant on 𝒳 \ N (and N is law-invariant).
Step 3 (Lipschitz, extension, LR = ρ/Π on 𝒳). For X, Y ∈ 𝒳 \ N put d = ‖X−Y‖_∞; X ≤ Y + d and Y + d ∈ 𝒳 \ N, so (A3) and Step 1 give ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y) + d; by symmetry |ρ(X) − ρ(Y)| ≤ d, likewise for Π. By (A0), 𝒳 \ N is dense in 𝒳, so ρ, Π extend uniquely to 1-Lipschitz maps on 𝒳; the extensions are cash-additive (N^c is shift-closed), law-invariant, and monotone on 𝒳 (for X ≤ Y pick X_n → X, Y_n → Y in 𝒳 \ N and replace Y_n by Y_n + ‖X_n − X‖ + ‖Y_n − Y‖ ≥ X_n, still off N). Π is continuous and > 0 off N; on N, Π > 0 as well: if Π(X) = 0 for X ∈ N then Π(X + 1) = 1 while... more directly Π(λX) = λΠ(X) (Step 5) and monotonicity give Π(X) ≥ Π(λX) → 0, and Π(X) ≥ Π(ε1) = ε for X ≥ ε; in general, Π(X) > 0 follows from Π(X) ≥ Π(X ∧ ε) and Π(1_B)-type lower bounds obtained after Step 6 (g > 0 on (0,1]); we postpone positivity on N to Step 6. The function ρ/Π is continuous wherever Π > 0 and equals LR on the dense set 𝒳 \ N; by (A5) LR is continuous; hence LR = ρ/Π on 𝒳 and ρ = LR·Π, in particular ρ = Π on N.
Step 4 (comonotone additivity). By (A4) and ρ = LR·Π: for comonotone X, Y ∈ 𝒳, Π(X+Y) = Π(X) + Π(Y) and ρ(X+Y) = LR(X+Y)Π(X+Y) = Π(X)LR(X) + Π(Y)LR(Y) = ρ(X) + ρ(Y).
Step 5 (positive homogeneity, normalization, scale invariance). X is comonotone with itself, so ρ(nX) = nρ(X), ρ((m/n)X) = (m/n)ρ(X); Lipschitz continuity gives ρ(λX) = λρ(X) for all λ > 0 ([DT-8] Aczél, Cauchy's equation on a cone, item (ii)); same for Π. Consequently LR(λX) = LR(X) (scale invariance is derived). For constants: cash-additivity gives ρ(1 + c) = ρ(1) + c, homogeneity gives ρ(1 + c) = (1 + c)ρ(1); hence ρ(1) = 1 and likewise Π(1) = 1. Monotonicity + homogeneity give ρ ≥ 0.
Step 6 (distortion representation). For p ∈ (0,1] choose B with P(B) = p (atomless) and set h(p) := ρ(1_B), g(p) := Π(1_B); well defined by law invariance, nondecreasing by monotonicity (atomless: p ≤ q ⇒ ∃ B ⊂ B' with the right masses), h(1) = g(1) = 1, and put h(0) = g(0) = 0. Every simple X ∈ 𝒳 is a nested-layer sum X = Σ_{i=1}^n c_i 1_{B_i}, c_i > 0, B_1 ⊃ ... ⊃ B_n (c_i = x_(i) − x_(i−1), B_i = {X ≥ x_(i)}), whose summands are pairwise comonotone; Steps 4–5 give ρ(X) = Σ c_i h(P(B_i)) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx = C_h(X). Simple functions are ‖·‖_∞-dense in 𝒳 and both ρ and C_h are Lipschitz (C_h with constant h(1) = 1, [Ch-1] consequences / [Ch-10] Denneberg), so ρ = C_h on 𝒳; likewise Π = C_g. [This is the direct, self-contained instance of Schmeidler's theorem [Ch-1] (monotone + comonotone additive ⇒ Choquet w.r.t. ν(A) = ρ(1_A)) combined with law invariance on an atomless space ⇒ ν = h∘P ([Ch-7] Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i); [Ins-1] Wang–Young–Panjer Thm, whose Greco-type continuity conditions hold here because ρ is Lipschitz and X bounded; [Ch-10] Greco/Denneberg Thm 13.2).] Positivity: for X ∈ 𝒳, P(X > x) ↑ P(X>0) > 0 as x ↓ 0, so if g > 0 on (0,1] then C_g(X) > 0; conversely Π(1_B) = g(P(B)) must be > 0 since LR is finite at X = 1_B ∈ 𝒳. So g > 0 on (0,1] and Π = C_g > 0 on all of 𝒳 (settling the postponed point of Step 3). Finally h ≠ g: otherwise ρ = Π and N = 𝒳, contradicting (A0).
Step 7 (conclusion, uniqueness, association). LR = ρ/Π = C_h/C_g on 𝒳 (Step 3). Uniqueness: if LR = C_{h'}/C_{g'} with h', g' normalized (possibly signed BV) and C_{g'} > 0 on 𝒳, then by cash-additivity of C_{h'}, C_{g'} (h'(1) = g'(1) = 1) the cash profile of X ∉ N is c ↦ (C_{h'}(X)+c)/(C_{g'}(X)+c), so by uniqueness of Möbius parameters C_{h'} = ρ and C_{g'} = Π on 𝒳 \ N, hence on 𝒳 by continuity, hence h'(p) = C_{h'}(1_B) = h(p), g' = g. Association: ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) = C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} by linearity of h ↦ C_h ([SC-1] WWW Def. 1; F7); for X ∈ 𝒳 and β ≠ 1, ρ_β(X) = 0 ⇔ C_h(X) = βC_g(X) ⇔ LR(X) = β since C_g(X) > 0. Uniqueness of the cash-additive associated family: if {ρ'_β} is cash-additive (unit slope) and in association with LR, then for X with X + m ∈ 𝒳, LR(X + m) = β ⇔ ρ'_β(X) + m = 0, i.e. ρ'_β(X) = −m = ρ_β(X) ([SC] key insight; [PR-2] Thm 4.6 recovery formula). Lipschitz constant of ρ_β: TV((h−βg)/(1−β)) ≤ (1+β)/|1−β| ([SC-1] Lemma 4). Monotonicity of ρ_β ⇔ h − βg nondecreasing ([SC-1] Lemma 2(i)) — not assumed anywhere.

PART B: (II) ⇒ (I). Let h, g be as in (II), ρ = C_h, Π = C_g.
(A1): C_h(X + c) = C_h(X) + c and C_g(X + c) = C_g(X) + c for c ≥ 0 (h(1) = g(1) = 1, [Ch-1] consequences / [SC-1] Lemma 2(ii)), C_g > 0 on 𝒳 (g > 0 on (0,1], Step 6 argument), so LR(X + c) = (C_h(X)+c)/(C_g(X)+c), a Möbius map with a_X = C_h(X) ≠ C_g(X) = b_X exactly when LR(X) ≠ 1, and ≡ 1 when LR(X) = 1.
(A2): C_h, C_g are law-invariant. (A3): h, g nondecreasing ⇒ C_h, C_g monotone ([SC-1] Lemma 2(i); [Ch-1]). (A4): C_h, C_g are comonotone-additive ([Ch-1]; [SC-1] Thm 1 direction ⇐), hence Π(X+Y) = Π(X)+Π(Y) and ρ(X+Y) = ρ(X)+ρ(Y) for comonotone X, Y, which rewritten with ρ = LR·Π is the premium-weighted mediant. (A5): C_h, C_g are 1-Lipschitz and C_g > 0, so LR is continuous on 𝒳.
(A0): suppose a ball B(X, r) ⊂ N, r > 0. For Y ∈ B(X, r/2) and 0 < δ < r/2, Y and Y + δ·1_{Y>q} are comonotone and both in N, so C_{h−g}(Y) = 0 = C_{h−g}(Y + δ1_{Y>q}) = C_{h−g}(Y) + δ(h−g)(P(Y>q)) by comonotone additivity, i.e. (h−g)(P(Y>q)) = 0 for every q ∈ ℝ. Now take Y = X + ε1_A with ε < r/2 and A ⊂ {q − ε < X ≤ q}; since the space is atomless, P(Y > q) = P(X > q) + P(A) sweeps the whole interval [S_X(q), S_X(q−ε)] as A varies; the union over q of these intervals is (0,1] (for p ∈ (0,1) let q* = sup{q : S_X(q) ≥ p}; right-continuity gives S_X(q*) ≤ p ≤ S_X(q*−ε)). Hence h − g = 0 on (0,1], i.e. h = g, contradicting h ≠ g. So N has empty interior.
Thus (I) holds. ∎

LEMMA M (role of the mediant / betweenness; what the unweighted mediant does and does not give). (i) [Ch-1]/[SC-1] Lemma 2 show that for a Choquet pair LR(X+Y) = (Π(X)LR(X)+Π(Y)LR(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) for comonotone X,Y, which implies the unweighted two-sided mediant of F1. (ii) Conversely, under (A0),(A1),(A3),(A5), the comonotone LEVEL-SET CLOSURE 'LR(X+c) = LR(Y+c') = β ≠ 1 ⇒ LR(X+Y+c+c') = β' implies additivity of ρ and Π on every comonotone pair X, Y ∉ N whose values lie on the same side of 1 and with X+Y ∉ N: as c, c' ≥ 0 vary, β ranges over a non-degenerate interval, and the three Möbius identities ρ(X)+c = β(Π(X)+c), ρ(Y)+c' = β(Π(Y)+c'), ρ(X+Y)+c+c' = β(Π(X+Y)+c+c') give ρ(X+Y) − βΠ(X+Y) = (ρ(X) − βΠ(X)) + (ρ(Y) − βΠ(Y)) for two distinct β, hence ρ(X+Y) = ρ(X)+ρ(Y), Π(X+Y) = Π(X)+Π(Y) (this is the 'cash-shift to a common level' argument of [PR-1]/[PR-2] and the ρ_β-additivity of the key insight). (iii) On the nonnegative domain with upward shifts only, pairs on opposite sides of 1 cannot be aligned, which is why (A4) is stated in weighted form; on Assa's extended domain (LR(X − m) allowed, i.e. LR on L^∞ with the pole set {Π = 0}) level-set closure for ALL comonotone pairs is equivalent to (A4). (iv) The unweighted mediant INEQUALITY alone is strictly weaker: it is Chew–Dekel betweenness on each comonotone cone ([DT-1],[DT-2],[DT-3]) and yields only an ordinal weighted-utility representation LR = ψ(ρ/Π) with (ρ,Π) projectively non-unique per cone; the cardinal normalization that fixes ψ = id and glues the cones is exactly the Möbius axiom (A1) (equivalently Sincov-consistent odds, [DT-8]). Counterexample ψ∘(C_h/C_g) below.

QUASI-CONVEXITY ADD-ON (proof): for β ≠ 1, ρ_β is cash-additive with unit slope, so every sublevel set {ρ_β ≤ t} is a translate of {ρ_β ≤ 0} = {LR ≤ β} (on L^∞; on 𝒳 after intersecting with the cone); by [SC-2] WWW Thm 3 (iv)⇔(v)⇔(i), ρ_β is quasi-convex iff convex iff h − βg concave; letting β run over [0,∞) gives h concave and g convex (and conversely), i.e. LR quasi-convex ⇔ (h concave, g convex); symmetrically LR quasi-concave ⇔ (h convex, g concave). [Au] key insight confirms the actuarial case ρ = E, g concave is quasi-CONCAVE.
MINIMALITY: Each axiom is shown necessary by a loss ratio satisfying all the others but not the conclusion (all examples on an atomless space; 'all other axioms' always includes those derivable).
(A0) non-degeneracy: LR ≡ 1 satisfies (A1)–(A5) (with the convention that the profile is ≡ 1 everywhere) and is represented by every pair h = g; the representation survives but uniqueness and the class description 'h ≠ g' fail. (A0) is precisely the dividing line LR ≢ 1 and cannot be weakened to 'N ≠ 𝒳' without losing the extension device (one needs 𝒳 \ N dense).
(A1) Möbius profile: LR^(2) := (C_h/C_g)^2 (or ψ∘(C_h/C_g) for any strictly increasing continuous ψ with ψ(1) = 1 that is not a Möbius map) satisfies (A0), (A2), (A5), scale invariance, the unweighted two-sided mediant and comonotone level-set closure, and its 'profile' c ↦ ((a+c)/(b+c))^2 is not of unit-slope Möbius form; it is not a ratio of two normalized Choquet integrals: on two-layer losses a + c1_B a representation C_{h'}/C_{g'} would force ((a + ch(p))/(a + cg(p)))^2 = (a + ch'(p))/(a + cg'(p)) for all a, c > 0, a polynomial identity in a whose coefficients force h(p) = g(p) wherever h'(p) ≠ g'(p), hence LR^(2) ≡ 1, contradiction. Also, the unit slopes in (A1) are a genuine normalization: C_{2h}/C_{2g} = C_h/C_g on 𝒳 but has profile (a + 2c)/(b + c); dropping the slope normalization leaves the pair identifiable only up to a common factor (F3 caveat of [Au]).
(A2) law invariance: LR(X) = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for two capacities ν_1 ≠ ν_2 on F with ν_i(Ω) = 1, ν_2 > 0 on non-null sets and ν_i not of the form h∘P (e.g. ν_i(A) = h_i(Q(A)) for an equivalent Q ≠ P): satisfies (A0),(A1),(A3)–(A5) (Schmeidler [Ch-1]: Choquet integrals w.r.t. capacities are monotone, comonotone additive, cash-additive, Lipschitz) but is not C_h/C_g for any (h,g) (its value on 1_A depends on A beyond P(A)). Dropping (A2) yields instead the capacity-ratio theorem via [Ch-2] CMMM Cor. 18 / [Ch-11] Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida.
(A3) endpoint monotonicity: take h(p) = p + κ sin(2πp) with 0 < κ small enough that h is of bounded variation but not monotone (h(0)=0, h(1)=1), g(p) = p: LR = C_h/E satisfies (A0),(A1),(A2),(A4),(A5) and (A3') uniform continuity (C_h is TV(h)-Lipschitz), but ρ = C_h is not monotone, so LR is not a ratio of distortion (monotone) functionals; it IS a ratio of signed Choquet integrals — exactly the signed variant of the theorem ([SC-1] WWW Thm 1 + Lemma 2(i)). So (A3) is exactly what separates the Yaari/Wang–Young–Panjer class from the signed-Choquet class, and it is irreplaceable by any property of LR's values (F2: X ≤ Y ⇒ LR(X) ≤ LR(Y) forces h = g).
(A4) comonotone additivity: ρ := max(C_{h1}, C_{h2}) with distinct distortions h1, h2 crossing each other, Π := E: ρ is monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, law-invariant, 1-Lipschitz but not comonotone-additive (on a three-layer loss c_1 1_{B_1} + c_2 1_{B_2}, B_1 ⊃ B_2, with h1 > h2 at P(B_1) and h1 < h2 at P(B_2), max(Σ c_i h1(p_i), Σ c_i h2(p_i)) < Σ c_i max(h1,h2)(p_i)). LR = ρ/E satisfies (A0),(A1),(A2),(A3),(A5) and the unweighted mediant fails; it is not C_h/C_g: the pole functional is E (so g = id is forced) and the zero functional ρ = LR·E would have to be C_h with h = max(h1,h2) by its values on Bernoulli losses, contradicting the three-layer computation. Dropping (A4) but keeping convexity leads to the Kusuoka-type (sup of concave signed Choquet) class of [SC-7] Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang, not to a single pair.
(A5) continuity at the fair set: let M := {X ∈ 𝒳 : X takes at most two values a.s.} (a ‖·‖_∞-closed, law-invariant cone with empty interior, closed under upward shifts, containing the constants) and define LR(X) := C_h(X)/C_g(X) for X ∉ M, LR(X) := 1 for X ∈ M (h ≠ g distortions). Then N = M ∪ {C_h = C_g}, which has empty interior; the cash profiles off N are Möbius with (ρ,Π) = (C_h, C_g), which are monotone, law-invariant, comonotone additive on 𝒳 (their continuous extensions are C_h, C_g); so (A0)–(A4) hold, but LR is discontinuous at every two-valued X with C_h(X) ≠ C_g(X) and is not a ratio of Choquet integrals (on Bernoulli losses it would force h = g). Hence continuity of LR on N cannot be dropped, although off N it is automatic.
Atomlessness: on a space with atoms the map p ↦ ρ(1_B) is only defined on the range of P, the nested-layer density argument and the (A0)-perturbation argument fail, and Yaari/Schmeidler-type law-invariant representations are not available (Shapiro's warning [LI-11]); the theorem is stated for atomless spaces only.
Scale invariance is NOT an axiom: it is implied (Step 5), which shows Assa's Prop. 1 'ρ_β positively homogeneous ⇔ LR scale invariant' is a consequence of cash-additivity + comonotone additivity + continuity. Likewise 'translation to break-even' and the unweighted mediant are consequences.
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) The axiom (A1) is strong and is the real content: it says the cash profile of every loss is a unit-slope Möbius map, i.e. it already encodes 'LR is a ratio of two cash-additive functionals'. It is intrinsic to LR (testable from LR's values on X, X+c, X+c') and is exactly Assa's cash-additivity of the associated family, but a sceptic may call it a disguised pair hypothesis; the honest statement is that WITHOUT some cardinal normalization no theorem on LR alone can identify (ρ,Π), because LR is invariant under (ρ,Π) ↦ (φρ,φΠ) (betweenness/weighted-utility theorems of Chew/Dekel/Fishburn only give LR = ψ(ρ/Π) with projective freedom). (2) (A3) is a monotonicity requirement on the derived zero and pole functionals; it is on LR only through the reconstruction and cannot be phrased as an inequality between values of LR (F2). (3) The fair set N must be handled by a continuity-extension device requiring (A0) and (A5); (A4) is stated for the extended Π on N, and I have not proved that comonotone additivity off N propagates to N by itself (so the statement 'all axioms live on 𝒳 \ N' is not established). (4) On the nonnegative domain with upward shifts only, the comonotone level-set closure (the natural unweighted axiom) gives additivity only for pairs on the same side of 1; the full equivalence with the unweighted axiom needs Assa's extended domain LR(X − m), which leaves L^∞_+. (5) The theorem is on L^∞ of an atomless space; the L^p/Orlicz version would need the 'continuity at infinity' axiom of Wang–Wang–Wei (ASTIN 2020, Thm 1) and is not worked out. (6) The quasi-convexity add-on uses WWW Theorem 3 on L^∞; restricting sublevel sets to the cone L^∞_+ requires checking that the convexity test variables can be taken nonnegative (plausible by cash shifts, not written out). (7) Continuity of h, g (Assa's standing assumption in F1) requires the extra axiom of continuity along bounded a.s. convergence and is not part of the main theorem.
