(8) Literature verification: WebSearch was disabled for this organisation and most publishers were blocked during the panel; the theorems cited are those read by the ten scholars (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 MOR, Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 ASTIN, CMMM 2012/2015, BCZ 2014, Han–Lin–Wang 2025 read in full; Schmeidler 1986, Wang–Young–Panjer 1997, Greco/Denneberg partly from memory). No existing iff theorem for a ratio of two Choquet integrals (or of two coherent risk measures) was found in the QRM literature — the closest are Cherny–Madan Thm 1 (monotone families, not applicable), Han–Lin–Wang Thm 1 (diversification quotient, MCP families), Gneiting Thm 3.2 (ratio of expectations, elicitability), and Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei Thm 3 (difference of Choquet integrals) — so the theorem above is, to the panel's knowledge, new, and its novelty claim should be re-checked against the full texts of Cheridito–Kromer 'Reward–risk ratios' and Fiori–Rosazza Gianin 'Generalized PELVE' once accessible. (9) The 'counterexample' for (A1) relies on a polynomial-identity computation on two-layer losses that I have sketched, not written out in full.

=== PROPOSAL 8: Break-even-capital characterization of the loss ratio: LR is a ratio of two signed Choquet integrals iff its beta-capitals are law-invariant, comonotonically additive and uniformly continuous — with the elicitability dichotomy (pointwise identifiable iff mixture-linear pair; within the distortion class iff LR ≡ 1) (Elicitability / CxLS / identification-function scholar (Gneiting; Fissler–Ziegel; Steinwart et al.; Wang–Ziegel; Wang–Wei; Wang–Wei–Willmot; Delbaen–Bellini–Bignozzi–Ziegel; Fissler–Liu–Wang–Wei)) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with ‖·‖_∞; 𝒳 := L^∞_+ \ {0} the nonnegative bounded losses. Survival-form (signed) Choquet integral for k:[0,1]→ℝ of bounded variation with k(0)=0: I_k(X) = ∫_0^∞ k(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (k(P(X>x)) − k(1))dx; for k nondecreasing with k(1)=1 this is the distortion functional C_k of the project. An EXTENDED loss ratio is a map LR: L^∞ → ℝ ∪ {+∞} (it must be defined on cash-shifted positions X − m, m ∈ ℝ, exactly as in Assa's Prop. 1; its restriction to 𝒳 is the loss ratio proper). For β ∈ [0,∞)\{1} the β-CAPITAL of X is m_β(X) := the unique m ∈ ℝ with LR(X − m) = β, whenever such an m exists and is unique (Assa's association read through cash shifts: LR(X − m) = β ⟺ m = m_β(X); with LR = ρ/Π one gets m_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1 − β) = ρ_β, the canonical family). The DEGENERATE set is N := {X ∈ L^∞ : LR(X − m) = 1 for all m ∈ ℝ} (for LR = ρ/Π this is {ρ(X) = Π(X)}). Conventions: comonotone = (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 a.s.; law-invariant = depends on X only through its distribution; CxLS (Osband/Wang–Wei) for a law-invariant functional T on a convex set M of laws: T(F)=T(G) ⇒ T(λF+(1−λ)G)=T(F); pointwise identification function in the Fissler–Ziegel/Nolde–Ziegel sense: V(β,y) with ∫V(β,y)dF(y)=0 ⟺ β=T(F).
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A0) Non-degeneracy: the degenerate set N = {X : LR(X − m) = 1 for all m} has empty ‖·‖_∞-interior (equivalently, as the proof shows, LR ≢ 1). (A2) (A1) Möbius break-even structure (cash-shift axiom): for every X ∈ L^∞ the cash profile m ↦ LR(X − m) is a Möbius (linear-fractional) map with unit-slope numerator and denominator: there are reals a(X), b(X) with LR(X − m) = (a(X) − m)/(b(X) − m) for all m ≠ b(X), LR(X − b(X)) = +∞ if a(X) ≠ b(X), and the profile is ≡ 1 iff a(X) = b(X). [Equivalent intrinsic form: for every β ∈ [0,∞)\{1} and every X ∉ N the β-capital m_β(X) exists, is unique, and β ↦ (1 − β) m_β(X) is affine on [0,∞)\{1}. Off N the numbers a(X) = m_0(X) (the ZERO of the profile) and b(X) = lim_{β→∞} m_β(X) (the POLE) are functions of LR alone.] (A3) (A3) Law invariance: LR(X) = LR(Y) whenever X and Y have the same distribution (applied to all of L^∞, hence to the shifted positions X − m). (A4) (A4) Comonotone break-even additivity: for two distinct levels β_0, β_1 ∈ [0,∞)\{1} and all comonotone X, Y with X, Y, X+Y ∉ N: if LR(X + c) = LR(Y + c') = β_i then LR(X + Y + c + c') = β_i; equivalently m_{β_i}(X + Y) = m_{β_i}(X) + m_{β_i}(Y). [Equivalently, for all β ≠ 1; equivalently the premium-weighted comonotone mediant LR(X+Y) = (b(X)LR(X) + b(Y)LR(Y))/(b(X)+b(Y)) whenever b(X)+b(Y) ≠ 0.] (A5) (A5) Uniform continuity: X ↦ m_{β_0}(X) and X ↦ m_{β_1}(X) are uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞ on L^∞ \ N (for β_0, β_1 as in (A4)). (A6) (Derived, NOT an axiom) Scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X), λ > 0, is a CONSEQUENCE of (A1)+(A4)+(A5): comonotone additivity of m_β gives m_β(qX) = q m_β(X) for rational q > 0 (X is comonotone with itself), (A5) extends this to all λ > 0, and then LR(λX) = a(λX)/b(λX) = a(X)/b(X). Likewise 'translation to break-even' (LR(X+c) → 1 as c → ∞, monotone in c) is a consequence of (A1).
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: None are needed for the representation itself: under (A0),(A1),(A5) the numerator a = m_0 and the denominator b = m_∞ := lim_{β→∞} m_β are functions of LR, so every hypothesis above is a hypothesis on LR. The following OPTIONAL add-ons are genuinely statements on the pair (a,b) = (ρ,Π) and cannot be replaced by any property of LR (F2): (P1) monotonicity of a (resp. b): X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ a(X) ≤ a(Y) (resp. b) ⟺ h (resp. g) nondecreasing; (P2) subadditivity of a (resp. b) ⟺ h (resp. g) concave; (P3) positivity of the premium on 𝒳: b > 0 on 𝒳 ⟺ g > 0 on (0,1] (given (P1) for b). | For the elicitability supplement (part C below) the hypothesis is on the family: 'm_{β_0}, m_{β_1} are mixture-linear, i.e. m_β(F) = ∫ v_β dF' — this replaces (A4) and is a hypothesis on two members of the associated family, not on LR alone; conversely part (B) of the supplement needs the distortion-pair structure (P1) as input.
CONCLUSION: MAIN THEOREM (iff). Let LR: L^∞ → ℝ ∪ {+∞}. Then LR satisfies (A0),(A1),(A3),(A4),(A5) IF AND ONLY IF there exist functions h, g: [0,1] → ℝ of bounded variation with h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1 and h ≠ g, such that LR(X) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) for all X with I_g(X) ≠ 0, LR(X) = +∞ when I_g(X) = 0 ≠ I_h(X), and LR(X) = 1 on {I_h = I_g} (a signed Choquet pair with unit cash slope). In that case: (i) a = m_0 = I_h =: ρ and b = m_∞ = I_g =: Π, N = {I_h = I_g}; (ii) the β-capitals are exactly Assa's canonical family, m_β = (I_h − βI_g)/(1 − β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} = ρ_β, so LR and {ρ_β} are in association (LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0 for β ≠ 1, LR(X) = 1 ⟺ I_h(X) = I_g(X)) and every ρ_β is a (generally non-monotone) signed Choquet integral (F7); (iii) UNIQUENESS: the pair (h,g) is uniquely determined by LR (indeed by LR restricted to 𝒳 together with the cash profile); the excluded case h = g is exactly LR ≡ 1, where (h,g) = (h,h) is arbitrary and (A0) fails. On 𝒳 = L^∞_+ \ {0} the restriction is LR = C_h/C_g whenever C_g > 0. ADD-ONS (each an iff on the endpoints, by Wang–Wei–Willmot Lemma 2(i) and Theorem 3): a monotone ⟺ h nondecreasing (ρ a distortion risk measure); b monotone ⟺ g nondecreasing (Π a Wang–Young–Panjer premium), and then Π > 0 on 𝒳 ⟺ g > 0 on (0,1]; a (resp. b) subadditive ⟺ h (resp. g) concave; {LR ≤ β} convex ⟺ h − βg concave, hence LR quasi-convex on L^∞_+ ⟺ h concave and g convex, quasi-concave ⟺ h convex and g concave; LR pointwise monotone ⟺ h = g (excluded). ELICITABILITY SUPPLEMENT (this scholar's strand), under (A0),(A1),(A3),(A5) with a = ρ, Π = b, LR = ρ/Π: (B) [distortion pair] if h, g are nondecreasing distortion functions (equivalently (A4) + (P1)), then, on the convex set of finitely supported laws of L^∞ positions, TFAE: (B1) LR has CxLS; (B2) LR is identifiable with a strict pointwise identification function; (B3) LR is elicitable; (B4) every ρ_β (β in the range of LR, β ≠ 1) has CxLS; (B5) every such ρ_β is monotone and (h − βg)/(1 − β) ∈ H* (Wang–Wei's class: ess-sup/ess-inf mixtures, the mean, or VaR^c-type step functions); (B6) h = g, i.e. LR ≡ 1. (C) [mixture-linear pair] if (A4) is replaced by 'm_{β_0}, m_{β_1} are mixture-linear' then ρ(F) = ∫ r dF, Π(F) = ∫ s dF, LR(F) = ∫ r dF / ∫ s dF, LR is identifiable with the affine-in-forecast identification function V(β,y) = β s(y) − r(y) (= the canonical family ρ − βΠ written pointwise; Gneiting Thm 3.2 / Fissler–Ziegel Table 1) and elicitable with Gneiting's scores (22); in this class scale invariance plus common-degree homogeneity of r, s forces LR constant. Hence: Assa's association V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) is a pointwise (Nolde–Ziegel) identification function iff the pair is mixture-linear, and a nonconstant distortion loss ratio is never identifiable, elicitable or CxLS — its association is a functional-level (zero-set) identification only.
PROOF SKETCH: Numbering of cited theorems follows the Elicitability/CxLS digest (E[n]) and the Choquet/Schmeidler digest (Ch[n]).

STEP 0 (the two endpoints are functions of LR). Fix X ∉ N. By (A1) the profile m ↦ LR(X − m) is a non-constant Möbius map with unit slopes; a non-constant Möbius map has a unique zero and a unique pole, so a(X) = m_0(X) and b(X) = lim_{β→∞} m_β(X) are determined by LR, and m_β(X) = (a(X) − βb(X))/(1 − β) (solve (a − m)/(b − m) = β). On N, (A1) forces a(X) = b(X); the common value is pinned by (A0)+(A5): m_{β_0}, m_{β_1} are uniformly continuous on the dense set L^∞ \ N, hence extend uniquely to L^∞, and a = ((1−β_0)m_{β_0} − ... ) is recovered by inverting the 2×2 system a − β_i b = (1 − β_i) m_{β_i}, i = 0,1 (determinant β_1 − β_0 ≠ 0). So a and b are well-defined, uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous maps L^∞ → ℝ determined by LR.

STEP 1 (⇒, structural properties of a and b). (1a) Cash-additivity: compare the profiles of X and X + c: LR((X+c) − m) = LR(X − (m − c)) = (a(X) + c − m)/(b(X) + c − m); uniqueness of zero and pole gives a(X + c) = a(X) + c, b(X + c) = b(X) + c (off N; on N by continuity). (1b) Comonotone additivity: (A4) says m_{β_i}(X+Y) = m_{β_i}(X) + m_{β_i}(Y), i.e. a − β_i b is comonotone-additive for i = 0,1; subtracting, (β_1 − β_0) b is comonotone-additive, hence b and then a are (on L^∞ \ N, then on L^∞ by density and continuity, since comonotone pairs with X, Y, X+Y ∉ N are dense among comonotone pairs because N has empty interior and comonotonicity is preserved by adding small comonotone perturbations). (1c) Positive homogeneity: X is comonotone with itself, so a(nX) = n a(X), a(X/n) = a(X)/n, hence a(qX) = q a(X) for rational q > 0, and (A5) gives a(λX) = λa(X) for all λ > 0; same for b. (1d) Law invariance: if X =_d Y then X − m =_d Y − m, so by (A3) the profiles coincide, hence a(X) = a(Y), b(X) = b(Y) (off N; on N by continuity). (1e) Uniform ‖·‖_∞-continuity of a and b: they are linear combinations of m_{β_0}, m_{β_1}.

STEP 2 (⇒, representation). By Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020) Theorem 1 (E[5], Ch[4]) — 'a functional L^∞ → ℝ on an atomless space is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly norm-continuous IFF it is a signed Choquet integral I_k, k of bounded variation, k(0)=0' — applied to a and to b (NO monotonicity needed, which is exactly how the non-monotonicity of the intermediate ρ_β is handled), there are BV h, g with h(0)=g(0)=0, a = I_h, b = I_g. By Lemma 2(ii) of the same paper, I_k(X + c) = I_k(X) + c k(1); cash-additivity (1a) gives h(1) = g(1) = 1. Finally LR(X) = LR(X − 0) = (a(X) − 0)/(b(X) − 0) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) when b(X) ≠ 0, LR(X) = +∞ when b(X) = 0 ≠ a(X), and LR = 1 on N = {a = b} = {I_h = I_g}. By linearity of k ↦ I_k, m_β = (I_h − βI_g)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)}, which is Assa's canonical ρ_β (F7). h ≠ g because N ≠ L^∞ by (A0).

STEP 3 (⇐). Let LR = I_h/I_g as in the conclusion. I_h, I_g are law-invariant, comonotone-additive, positively homogeneous, cash-additive with slope h(1)=g(1)=1, and Lipschitz with constants TV(h), TV(g) (WWW Lemma 2 and Lemma 4; Wang–Wang–Wei ASTIN Remark 2). (A1): LR(X − m) = (I_h(X) − m)/(I_g(X) − m) by cash-additivity, so a = I_h, b = I_g and m_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)}. (A3): law invariance of I_h, I_g. (A4): m_β is comonotone-additive as a linear combination of comonotone-additive functionals; the break-even formulation follows since LR(X + c) = β ⟺ c = −m_β(X). (A5): m_β is Lipschitz with constant (TV(h) + βTV(g))/|1 − β|. (A0): N = {I_{h−g} = 0}; if N contained a ball B(X_0, ε) then for every Y with ‖Y‖_∞ ≤ 1 and small t, I_{h−g}(X_0 + tY) = 0; comonotone additivity and homogeneity are not available for X_0 + tY vs X_0 directly, so argue instead: N ⊇ B(X_0,ε) ⊇ {c + tY : |c − c_0| small, ...} is not immediate — use that I_{h−g}(c + tY) = c (h−g)(1) + t I_{h−g}(Y) = t I_{h−g}(Y) for constants c (Lemma 2(ii) with (h−g)(1) = 0); choosing X_0 = c_0 constant is allowed only if the ball is around a constant. General case: for X_0 ∈ int N and any Y, the map t ↦ I_{h−g}(X_0 + tY) vanishes near 0; by positive homogeneity I_{h−g}(λX_0 + λtY) = 0, and λX_0 ∈ N for all λ>0 since N is a cone; the function (λ,t) ↦ I_{h−g}(λX_0 + tY) is 1-Lipschitz·TV in both arguments and vanishes on an open set of (λ,t) around (1,0); for fixed Y take comonotone decomposition? Simpler and rigorous: by Wang–Wei–Willmot Lemma 1 (uniqueness), I_{h−g} ≡ 0 on L^∞ iff h = g; and a signed Choquet integral vanishing on an open set vanishes identically because for any event A with P(A) = p and any X_0 in the open set, X_0 + t1_A and X_0 are comonotone only when X_0 is constant on the relevant level sets — so instead pick X_0 constant: an open subset of N contains, by density of simple functions and N being closed, a point X_1 = Σ c_i 1_{A_i}; perturb to X_1 + t1_{A_j}: X_1 and 1_{A_j} are comonotone iff A_j is an upper level set of X_1, which holds for the top level set; hence 0 = I_{h−g}(X_1 + t1_{A_top}) = I_{h−g}(X_1) + t (h−g)(P(A_top)) gives (h−g)(p) = 0 for the probabilities p of top level sets of simple functions in the ball, which (atomless space, varying the simple function inside the ball) exhaust an interval of p; then (h − g) is a BV function vanishing on an interval, and repeating with nested layers (I_{h−g}(X_1 + t(1_{A_top} + 1_{A_top∪A_next})) etc.) one gets (h−g) = 0 on (0,1], i.e. h = g, contradicting h ≠ g. So (A0) holds. (A2) and translation-to-break-even hold by F1.

STEP 4 (uniqueness). If I_h/I_g = I_{h'}/I_{g'} with unit cash slopes and both pairs non-degenerate, Step 0 shows a = I_h = I_{h'} and b = I_g = I_{g'} (zero and pole of the profile), and WWW Lemma 1 (I_{k_1} = I_{k_2} with k_1(1) = k_2(1) ⟹ k_1 = k_2) gives h = h', g = g'. This is F3 extended from 𝒳 to the signed class: on 𝒳 alone LR determines (h,g) only up to a common scalar, and the cash profile (A1) with unit slopes fixes the scalar.

STEP 5 (supplement (C), mixture-linear pair). If m_{β_i}(F) = ∫ v_i dF for i = 0,1, then a and b, as linear combinations, are mixture-linear: a(F) = ∫ r dF, b(F) = ∫ s dF (r, s bounded measurable on the support, s > 0 where Π > 0). Then ∫(β s − r)dF = 0 ⟺ β = LR(F): this is Fissler–Ziegel Table 1 / Gneiting Thm 3.2's identification function V(β,y) = β s(y) − r(y) (E[1], E[2]); elicitability and the exact form of consistent scores follow from Gneiting Thm 3.2(a)-(c) (E[1]). Conversely, if LR has a strict identification function affine in β, V(β,y) = β s(y) − r(y), then the zero set of ∫V(β,·)dF equals {β = LR(F)} = {ρ_β(F) = 0}, and since both ∫V(β,·)dF and (1−β)ρ_β(F) = ρ(F) − βΠ(F) are affine in β with the same zero, ρ − βΠ = κ(F)(β s̄(F) − r̄(F)) with s̄ = ∫s dF; cash-additivity with unit slope pins κ, giving ρ, Π mixture-linear. Scale invariance with r, s homogeneous of the same degree d: LR(λX) = λ^d∫r/λ^d∫s — automatically invariant — but LR(1) = 1 and the cash profile (A1) force r(y) − y and s(y) − y to be 'location-equivariant' only when r(y) = y = s(y) (an expectation is the only mixture-linear cash-additive law-invariant functional: ∫r d(F*δ_c) = ∫r dF + c for all F forces r(y+c) − r(y) = c, so r = id + const, const = 0 by r(0)... ) — hence in the mixture-linear class the only cash-additive loss ratio is E[X]/E[X] ≡ 1, and nonconstant expectation-type ratios E[r(X)]/E[s(X)] violate (A1). This is the precise reason the elicitable ratios of Gneiting/Fissler–Ziegel and the actuarial loss ratio are disjoint classes.

STEP 6 (supplement (B), distortion pair ⟹ dichotomy). (B3)⇒(B1) and (B2)⇒(B1): Osband's theorem (Gneiting Thm 2.8, E[1]; Fissler–Ziegel Prop 2.14, E[2]; Wang–Wei Prop 6.4, E[4]). (B1)⟺(B4): LR(F) = β ⟺ ρ_β(F) = 0, and ρ_β(F + c) = ρ_β(F) + c (unit slope, Lemma 2(ii)); mixtures of laws commute with common translations, so CxLS of the zero level of ρ_β for all β is equivalent to CxLS of ρ_β at all levels: if ρ_β(F) = ρ_β(G) = v, shift both laws by −v to the zero level, use CxLS of {LR = β}, shift back (the domain of finitely supported laws is closed under translation and mixtures). (B4)⟺(B5): Wang–Wei Theorem 3.2 and Corollary 3.3 (E[4]) applied to the signed Choquet integral ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} — this is precisely the theorem built for non-monotone signed Choquet integrals, so F2 is not an obstacle: CxLS on discrete laws forces ρ_β monotone and (h − βg)/(1−β) ∈ H* = H*_1 ∪ H*_2 ∪ H*_3. (B5)⇒(B6): H* consists of three disjoint one- or two-parameter families of shapes (step at 0+ and 1−; linear; single step at α); the map β ↦ (h − βg)/(1−β) is a projective line in the vector space H through the two points h (β = 0) and g (β → ∞); if two distinct members of a projective line lie in the same family of H* (same α if H*_3), then h and g lie in the affine span of that family, and h(1) = g(1) = 1 with h, g nondecreasing distortions forces h = g in the H*_2 case (h = g = id), and in the H*_1 / H*_3 cases h = c_1 1_{(0,1)} + 1_{{1}}, g = c_2 1_{(0,1)} + 1_{{1}} (resp. step shapes with common α) whose ratio LR = (c_1 ess sup + (1−c_1) ess inf)/(c_2 ess sup + (1−c_2) ess inf) — here the range of LR on 𝒳 contains values β for which (h − βg)/(1−β) has a negative jump, i.e. ρ_β is non-monotone, contradicting Cor 3.3 unless c_1 = c_2; members of two DIFFERENT H*-families cannot both lie on one projective line through nondecreasing normalized distortions (the mean is not a combination of step functions and vice versa); hence h = g. (B6)⇒ all: LR ≡ 1 trivially has CxLS, is identifiable (V ≡ β − 1) and elicitable. The independent route via Embrechts–Mao–Wang–Wang Thm 6.1 (E[10]) in the conic regime (ρ_β mixture-concave by WWW Thm 3) gives the same conclusion: a positively homogeneous mixture-concave CxLS functional is entropic only if linear, i.e. ρ_β = E.
MINIMALITY: (A1) Möbius cash structure is needed: LR := ψ(C_h/C_g) with ψ strictly increasing, ψ(1) = 1, not a Möbius map (e.g. ψ(t) = t^2) satisfies (A0),(A3), scale invariance and a version of (A4),(A5) for its own level sets, but its cash profile is not linear-fractional with unit slopes, so it is not a ratio of two cash-additive functionals; equivalently its β-capitals are m_{ψ^{-1}(β)} and (1−β)m_β is not affine in β. More generally any index LR(X) = inf{β : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} built from a non-affine nested family (Cherny–Madan/Drapeau–Kupper/Han–Lin–Wang type) satisfies everything except the affinity encoded in (A1). (A3) law invariance is needed: a(X) = ∫X dν_1, b(X) = ∫X dν_2 with ν_1, ν_2 capacities not of the form k∘P (e.g. Π = E_Q, Q ≠ P, ρ = E_P) satisfy (A0),(A1),(A4),(A5) (Schmeidler/Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida: comonotone-additive + bounded variation) but LR = ∫X dν_1/∫X dν_2 is not a ratio of distortion-type signed Choquet integrals; this is exactly CMMM Cor. 18's non-law-invariant variant. (A4) comonotone additivity is needed: a(X) = max(C_{h_1}(X), C_{h_2}(X)) (a law-invariant coherent, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, Lipschitz, non-comonotone-additive functional) with b = E gives LR = a/E satisfying (A0),(A1),(A3),(A5), scale invariance and the ordinary mediant inequality, but a is recovered from LR by (A1) and is not a signed Choquet integral (WWW Thm 1 is an iff), so the conclusion fails; note the weak (inequality) mediant property of F1 is NOT enough — the two-sided break-even additivity (equality) is what is used, and it is equivalent to the premium-weighted mediant identity. (A5) uniform continuity is needed for the bounded-variation class: by WWW Remark 3 a continuous k:[0,1]→ℝ of unbounded variation (e.g. k(t) = t sin(1/t)-type, normalized) gives a functional I_k on L^∞ that is law-invariant, comonotone-additive, norm-continuous but not uniformly continuous; LR = I_k/E satisfies (A0),(A1),(A3),(A4) and all but (A5), and the representing h is not BV. (Honest remark: dropping (A5) in favor of mere norm continuity still gives LR = I_h/I_g with h, g continuous of possibly unbounded variation, and dropping continuity altogether admits Hamel-basis pathologies for cash-additive comonotone-additive functionals, so some continuity axiom is unavoidable.) (A0) non-degeneracy is needed for uniqueness and is exactly the exclusion of LR ≡ 1: for LR ≡ 1 every pair (h,h) represents LR; conversely, as shown in Step 3, in the signed Choquet class N has empty interior whenever h ≠ g, so (A0) costs nothing beyond excluding the constant ratio. Scale invariance and translation-to-break-even are NOT axioms: they are implied (Step 1c, F1). Pointwise monotonicity of LR is never used and would contradict the conclusion unless h = g (F2). For the add-ons: monotonicity of a (resp. b) cannot be dropped if one wants h (resp. g) nondecreasing — LR = I_h/E with h = 2t − t^2 − (something signed) of bounded variation but non-monotone satisfies (A0)–(A5) and is a ratio of signed Choquet integrals with a non-monotone numerator (e.g. ρ = E + Gini mean difference, a legitimate loaded risk functional); and no axiom on LR can detect it (F2: both monotone and non-monotone numerators yield non-monotone LR). For the supplement: the Steinwart-type regularity (TV-continuity, strict local non-constancy) is required only if one wants (B1)⟺(B2)⟺(B3) outside the distortion class; inside it, Wang–Wei's theorem does the work directly on finitely supported laws, and the restriction to finitely supported laws of SIGNED positions (not only 𝒳) is used in (B4)⟺(B5): I cannot prove the dichotomy with CxLS assumed only on laws supported in [0,∞) — this is the remaining honest gap (three-point laws with negative atoms are used in Wang–Wei's necessity proof; the positive-homogeneity of the zero set reduces it to laws on [0,∞) only when the needed translates stay nonnegative).
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) The theorem characterizes the EXTENDED loss ratio: axiom (A1) requires LR to be defined on cash-shifted positions X − m ∈ L^∞ (possibly negative), exactly as Assa's Prop. 1 does; a characterization of LR restricted to 𝒳 = L^∞_+ \ {0} alone is not obtained, and I believe it cannot be of this form because on 𝒳 the pair is identified only up to a common scalar (F3) and the Greco/Wang–Young–Panjer nonnegative-cone representation theorems require monotonicity, which the intermediate β-capitals lack. (2) The hypotheses, while stated on LR, are 'hidden pair hypotheses': (A4) and (A5) are properties of the two endpoint functionals a = m_0 and b = m_∞ read off LR through (A1); the genuinely LR-level content is (A1) (affinity of the association) and (A3). The question whether some weaker, non-break-even axiom on LR (e.g. only the inequality mediant property of F1 plus quasi-convexity) could replace (A4) has a negative answer by the max(C_{h_1},C_{h_2})/E counterexample. (3) Monotonicity/positivity of the premium and of the risk measure (h, g nondecreasing, g > 0) are not derivable and must be added on the endpoints; the theorem's class is therefore 'signed Choquet pairs with unit cash slope', wider than Assa's distortion pairs. (4) In Step 3 the proof that N has empty interior for h ≠ g uses simple-function perturbations along top level sets; it is sketched, not written in full detail. (5) The elicitability supplement (B) proves (B5)⇒(B6) by a projective-line argument inside H* that I have checked case by case but not with full rigor for the H*_3 subcase with common α and c_1 ≠ c_2 (the argument relies on the sign of the jump of (h − βg)/(1−β) for β in the range of LR); the domain gap (CxLS assumed on signed finitely supported laws rather than on laws supported in [0,∞)) remains. (6) Source caveats: WWW 2020 Thm 1, Lemmas 1–2, Thm 3, Wang–Wei Thm 3.2/Cor 3.3/Prop 6.4, Gneiting Thm 3.2, Fissler–Ziegel Table 1 and Steinwart Cor 9 were read from author PDFs/arXiv in this project; Schmeidler 1986, Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida 1994 and Wang–Young–Panjer 1997 are quoted from memory. (7) No existing paper gives an iff characterization of a ratio of two Choquet integrals; the closest objects (Gneiting's ratio-of-expectations theorem, Han–Lin–Wang's diversification quotient, Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei's inter-ES differences, PELVE) are templates, so the theorem above is new in its combination and should be checked independently before being quoted as established.

=== PROPOSAL 9: Mediant–odds (Sincov–Cauchy) characterization of the loss ratio: LR is a ratio of two signed Choquet integrals with positive denominator if and only if it satisfies strict comonotone betweenness with ratio-consistent and additive mediant odds (Decision-theory / functional-equation scholar (Chew–Dekel betweenness, Fishburn SSB, Aczél–Sincov functional equations, Schmeidler/Yaari/signed Choquet). Process note: WebSearch is still disabled for this organization (HTTP 403 on this run as on the previous one); no new online verification was possible, so the theorem below is built on the digest items that other scholars read in full (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 MOR; Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 ASTIN; CMMM 2012/2015; Han–Lin–Wang 2025) plus the classical decision-theory theorems (Chew 1983, Dekel 1986, Fishburn 1983, Yaari 1987, Aczél 1966) quoted from memory.) ===
SETTING: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P); 𝒳 := L^∞_+ \ {0} (nonnegative bounded losses, not a.s. zero); LR : 𝒳 → ℝ is a given map (real-valued; it may take values ≤ 0 or ≥ 1, nothing is assumed about its range). Comonotonicity of X,Y: (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 a.s.⊗a.s. Survival-form Choquet integral for a function k:[0,1]→ℝ with k(0)=0: C_k(X) := ∫_0^∞ k(P(X>x)) dx for X ≥ 0, and on L^∞ the signed Choquet integral I_k(X) := ∫_{−∞}^0 (k(P(X>x))−k(1))dx + ∫_0^∞ k(P(X>x))dx (Wang–Wei–Willmot Def. 1 with Remark 1; I_k = C_k on L^∞_+). H := {k of bounded variation on [0,1], k(0)=0}. DERIVED OBJECTS (defined from LR only). Fair set N := {X ∈ 𝒳 : LR(X) = 1}, 𝒳_1 := 𝒳 \ N. Under axiom (L3) below, for comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳 with LR(X) ≠ LR(Y) there is a unique mediant weight w(X,Y) ∈ (0,1) with LR(X+Y) = w LR(X) + (1−w) LR(Y); the mediant odds are ω(X,Y) := w/(1−w) ∈ (0,∞), explicitly ω(X,Y) = (LR(X+Y) − LR(Y))/(LR(X) − LR(X+Y)); note ω(Y,X) = 1/ω(X,Y) automatically. Since the constant 1 is comonotone with every X and (by (L2)) LR(1)=1, the ANCHORED ODDS Π(X) := ω(X,1) = (1 − LR(X+1))/(LR(X+1) − LR(X)) are defined for every X ∈ 𝒳_1; this is the 'premium revealed by LR'. Conventions: 'strict mediant' means strict inequalities; 'uniformly continuous' refers to ‖·‖_∞.
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (L2) Break-even normalization: LR(1) = 1. [Pure normalization: it fixes h(1)=g(1)=1 in the conclusion; dropping it replaces h(1)=1 by h(1)=LR(1) ∈ ℝ and the anchor 1 by any fixed Z_0 with LR(Z_0) attained.] (A2) (L3) Strict comonotone betweenness (strict mediant property): for all comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳, LR(X) < LR(Y) implies LR(X) < LR(X+Y) < LR(Y), and LR(X) = LR(Y) implies LR(X+Y) = LR(X). [This is F1's mediant property made strict; it is Chew–Dekel betweenness on each comonotone cone and already implies LR(nX)=LR(X).] (A3) (L4) Ratio-consistent and additive mediant odds (Sincov + Cauchy, anchored at the sure loss 1): (a) ω(X,Y)·ω(Y,1) = ω(X,1) for all comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳 whose values LR(X), LR(Y), 1 are pairwise distinct; (b) ω(X+Y,1) = ω(X,1) + ω(Y,1) for all comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳 with LR(X), LR(Y), LR(X+Y) all ≠ 1. [Equivalent 'premium-weighted mediant' reading: LR(X+Y) = (Π(X)LR(X)+Π(Y)LR(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) with Π := ω(·,1) additive on comonotone pairs. (b) may be weakened to cash-additivity ω(X+c,1) = ω(X,1)+c, c>0, see whyMinimal.] (A4) (L5) Law invariance: LR(X) = LR(Y) whenever X and Y have the same distribution. (A5) (L6) Continuity: (a) LR is ‖·‖_∞-continuous on 𝒳; (b) the anchored odds X ↦ ω(X,1) are uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous on 𝒳_1. [(b) is the only non-ordinal regularity; it is exactly the hypothesis of Wang–Wei–Willmot Thm 1 and is what forces bounded variation of the distortions. It is implied by (a) plus monotonicity of ω(·,1), see supplements.] (A6) (L7) Non-degeneracy on Bernoulli losses: there is an event A ∈ F with LR(1_A) ≠ 1. [Excludes only LR ≡ 1 on indicators; needed for uniqueness and for the fair-set extension.] (A7) (Derived, NOT assumed) scale invariance LR(λX)=LR(X), λ>0, follows from (L3)+(L6a); positivity Π>0 on 𝒳 follows from (L3)–(L7).
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: None for the main theorem: every hypothesis is stated on LR (the odds ω and the anchored odds Π = ω(·,1) are explicit functions of LR-values of X, X+1, X+Y). In particular NO hypothesis is placed on the associated family {ρ_β}: the family is recovered as ρ_β = (LR − β)·Π/(1−β) and its non-monotonicity (F2) plays no role because no monotonicity is ever used. | Optional supplements (each an 'iff', each stated on the LR-DERIVED endpoints Π := ω(·,1) and ρ := LR·Π rather than on LR pointwise — this is the honest place where monotonicity/coherence enter, since by F2 they cannot be read off LR by comparing LR(X), LR(Y) for X ≤ Y): (S1) Π monotone (X ≤ Y ⇒ ω(X,1) ≤ ω(Y,1)) ⟺ g nondecreasing; under (S1) axiom (L6b) can be dropped. (S2) ρ monotone ⟺ h nondecreasing. (S3) ρ subadditive ⟺ h concave; Π subadditive ⟺ g concave; Π superadditive ⟺ g convex. (S4) ρ, Π continuous along bounded a.s. convergence ⟺ h, g continuous.
CONCLUSION: THEOREM. Let LR : 𝒳 → ℝ. The following are equivalent. (I) LR satisfies (L2)–(L7). (II) There exist h, g ∈ H (i.e. h, g : [0,1] → ℝ of bounded variation, h(0)=g(0)=0) with h(1)=g(1)=1, g(p) > 0 for every p ∈ (0,1], and h ≠ g, such that LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx for all X ∈ 𝒳. The class of pairs is thus exactly: 'normalized signed-Choquet pairs with strictly positive denominator distortion', ρ := C_h a (possibly non-monotone) signed Choquet integral and Π := C_g a signed Choquet integral that is strictly positive on 𝒳. UNIQUENESS: in (II) the pair (h,g) is uniquely determined by LR (explicit inversion: r(p) := LR(1_B) = h(p)/g(p) for P(B)=p; g(p) = (1−LR(1+c1_B))/(c(LR(1+c1_B)−r(p))) when r(p) ≠ 1; and g(p) = (h(q) − LR(1_B+1_D) g(q))/(LR(1_B+1_D) − 1) with D ⊂ B or B ⊂ D, P(D)=q, r(q) ≠ 1, when r(p)=1); moreover Π = ω(·,1) = C_g and ρ = LR·Π = C_h on 𝒳, i.e. the revealed premium and revealed risk functional are the two members of the pair. COROLLARIES (all inside the equivalence): (i) scale invariance and positivity Π>0 hold; (ii) [Assa association, F7] with ρ_β := (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (β≠1) one has LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0, ρ_β is cash-additive, positively homogeneous, law-invariant, comonotone-additive, Lipschitz with constant (TV(h)+βTV(g))/|1−β|, and ρ_0 = ρ, lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = Π; the family is generally non-monotone (F2) and is uniquely determined by LR; (iii) LR = φ(C_h/C_g) for a strictly increasing continuous φ ≠ id satisfies (L2),(L3),(L5),(L6a),(L7) but not (L4): (L4) is precisely the cardinal axiom that removes the Chew/Fishburn ordinal (linear-fractional) freedom. SUPPLEMENTS (iff, see axiomsOnPairOrFamily): (S1)–(S4) as listed; and (S5) for β≠1 the sublevel set {LR ≤ β} is convex iff h − βg is concave, {LR ≥ β} convex iff h − βg convex (Wang–Wei–Willmot Thm 3 via the cash shift ρ_β(X+c)=ρ_β(X)+c, modulo the L^∞ vs L^∞_+ test-variable subtlety), so within the class LR is quasi-convex on 𝒳 iff h concave and g convex, quasi-concave iff h convex and g concave; (S6) LR is pointwise monotone on 𝒳 iff h = g, which is excluded by (L7) (F2): no pointwise monotonicity of LR is assumed anywhere, consistent with F2.
PROOF SKETCH: Numbering: [DT n] = Decision-theory strand item n of the digest; [SC n] = Signed-Choquet/Ruodu Wang strand; [CH n] = Choquet/Schmeidler strand; [KU n] = Kusuoka strand; [AU n] = Assa/author strand.

PART A: (II) ⇒ (I). Let h,g ∈ H as in (II), ρ := C_h, Π := C_g on 𝒳 (= I_h, I_g restricted to L^∞_+). A1. Positivity: for X ∈ 𝒳, P(X>x) ∈ (0,1] for 0 ≤ x < ess sup X > 0, so the integrand g(P(X>x)) > 0 there and C_g(X) > 0; LR is real-valued. A2. (L2): C_h(1) = h(1) = 1 = g(1) = C_g(1). A3. (L3): I_h, I_g are comonotone-additive and positively homogeneous ([SC 1] Lemma 2(iii), [CH 4]; the Riemann/Stieltjes computation C_k(X) = ∫_0^1 k(1−u) dq_X(u) with q_{X+Y} = q_X + q_Y for comonotone X,Y needs no monotonicity of k). Hence for comonotone X,Y: LR(X+Y) = (ρ(X)+ρ(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) = w LR(X) + (1−w)LR(Y) with w = Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) ∈ (0,1) by A1; this gives strict betweenness and the equal-value clause. A4. (L4): by uniqueness of w when LR(X)≠LR(Y), ω(X,Y) = Π(X)/Π(Y); so ω(X,1) = Π(X) (Π(1)=1), (a) is the identity Π(X)/Π(Y)·Π(Y) = Π(X) and (b) is comonotone additivity of Π. A5. (L5): I_h, I_g are law-invariant by construction. A6. (L6): |I_k(X)−I_k(Y)| ≤ TV(k)‖X−Y‖_∞ ([SC 1] Lemma 4; [CH 5] Remark 2), so ρ, Π are Lipschitz, Π > 0, hence LR = ρ/Π is continuous on 𝒳 (a) and ω(·,1) = Π is uniformly continuous (b). A7. (L7): h ≠ g gives p_0 with h(p_0) ≠ g(p_0); on an atomless space pick A with P(A)=p_0; LR(1_A) = h(p_0)/g(p_0) ≠ 1.

PART B: (I) ⇒ (II). B0 (density of 𝒳_1 and upper-set perturbations). Fix A as in (L7), p_0 := P(A), so by (L5) LR(1_B) = LR(1_A) ≠ 1 for every B with P(B)=p_0. For any X ∈ 𝒳 (or any comonotone pair X,Y, applied to X+Y) there is B with P(B)=p_0 such that 1_B is comonotone with X (and with X,Y): B = {X > q} ∪ C with C ⊂ {X = q}, P(C) chosen using atomlessness; for comonotone X,Y a set of this form built from X+Y is comonotone with X, Y and X+Y (if ω' ∈ B, ω ∉ B then X(ω)>X(ω') would force, by comonotonicity, (X+Y)(ω) > (X+Y)(ω'), contradiction). If X ∈ N then LR(X+ε1_B) lies strictly between 1 and LR(1_B) ≠ 1 by (L3) (note LR(ε1_B) = LR(1_B) by the derived scale invariance, or directly by (L3) applied to copies), so X+ε1_B ∈ 𝒳_1 and X+ε1_B → X: 𝒳_1 is ‖·‖_∞-dense in 𝒳. If X ∉ N, X+ε1_B ∈ 𝒳_1 for small ε by (L6a).

B1 (Sincov ⇒ odds are a ratio; [DT 8](i)). Put Π(X) := ω(X,1) on 𝒳_1. For comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳_1 with LR(X)≠LR(Y), (L4a) gives ω(X,Y) = ω(X,1)/ω(Y,1) = Π(X)/Π(Y). (This is Sincov's equation with anchor 1; the general three-variable Sincov identity follows.) Π > 0 on 𝒳_1 by construction, and Π is law-invariant on 𝒳_1 because ω(X,1) is a function of LR(X), LR(X+1) and X =_d Y ⇒ X+1 =_d Y+1 (L5).

B2 (Cauchy on comonotone pairs; [DT 8](ii)). (L4b) says Π(X+Y) = Π(X)+Π(Y) for comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳_1 with X+Y ∈ 𝒳_1. Taking Y = X (comonotone with itself; LR(2X)=LR(X)≠1 by (L3)) gives Π(nX)=nΠ(X), Π(X/n)=Π(X)/n, hence Π(λX)=λΠ(X) for rational λ>0; real λ by (L6b). Consequently LR(λX)=LR(X) for all λ>0 on 𝒳_1 (and on N by B0/B4 continuity): scale invariance is derived, not assumed.

B3 (extension of Π to the fair set N). By (L6b) ω(·,1) is uniformly continuous on the dense set 𝒳_1, hence extends uniquely to a continuous Π on 𝒳 (Π(X) := lim_ε Π(X+ε1_B)). Positivity on N: for X ∈ N and B as in B0, Sincov (B1, all three LR-values 1, LR(X+ε1_B), LR(1_B) pairwise distinct) gives Π(X+ε1_B) = ω(X+ε1_B,1_B)·Π(1_B); by the explicit formula for ω and (L6a), ω(X+ε1_B,1_B) → (LR(X+1_B) − LR(1_B))/(1 − LR(X+1_B)) = ω(X,1_B) ∈ (0,∞) (both numerator and denominator are non-zero by strictness in (L3)); hence Π(X) = ω(X,1_B)Π(1_B) > 0. In particular Π(1) = ω(1,1_B)ω(1_B,1) = 1 by the automatic symmetry ω(Y,X)=1/ω(X,Y). Law invariance of Π on N: the law of X+ε1_B is determined by the law of X and p_0.

B4 (comonotone additivity of Π on all of 𝒳). Let X,Y ∈ 𝒳 be comonotone and B as in B0 (built from X+Y, P(B)=p_0). Then X_ε := X+ε1_B, Y_ε := Y+ε1_B are comonotone, X_ε+Y_ε = X+Y+2ε1_B, and for all small ε>0 the three losses X_ε, Y_ε, X_ε+Y_ε lie in 𝒳_1 (strictness of (L3) if the unperturbed loss is in N, continuity (L6a) otherwise). B2 gives Π(X_ε+Y_ε) = Π(X_ε)+Π(Y_ε); let ε → 0 and use continuity of Π (B3). Also Π(X+c) = Π(X)+cΠ(1) = Π(X)+c for c>0 (constants are comonotone with everything).

B5 (the revealed risk functional ρ := LR·Π is comonotone additive). For comonotone X,Y ∈ 𝒳_1 with X+Y ∈ 𝒳_1: if LR(X)≠LR(Y), the mediant identity with w = Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) (B1) and B4 give LR(X+Y)Π(X+Y) = Π(X)LR(X)+Π(Y)LR(Y), i.e. ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y); if LR(X)=LR(Y), (L3) gives LR(X+Y)=LR(X) and B4 gives the same. ρ is continuous on 𝒳 (product of continuous maps), so the perturbation of B4 extends additivity to all comonotone pairs of 𝒳. ρ is law-invariant (product of law-invariant maps), positively homogeneous (as in B2), and ρ(1) = LR(1)Π(1) = 1 by (L2). Uniform continuity of ρ on bounded sets follows from that of Π and continuity of LR; what is needed below is uniform continuity of the cash-extended functional, obtained as follows: ρ(X+c) = LR(X+c)Π(X+c); by B4/B5 and comonotonicity of X with c, ρ(X+c) = ρ(X)+cρ(1) = ρ(X)+c, so ρ is uniformly continuous on 𝒳 iff ρ̃ below is; this is guaranteed by (L6b) applied through the identity ρ = Π·LR and the Möbius structure LR(X+c) = (ρ(X)+c)/(Π(X)+c) (continuity of LR on {Π>0} with Π uniformly continuous and bounded below on sup-norm-bounded sets by B3) — alternatively one may simply add 'ρ uniformly continuous' to (L6b), which is the same axiom stated on LR·ω(·,1).

B6 (extension to L^∞ and the signed-Choquet representation). Define Π̃(X) := Π(X+c) − c and ρ̃(X) := ρ(X+c) − c for X ∈ L^∞ and any c > ‖X‖_∞ (independent of c by B4/B5 with constants). Π̃, ρ̃ : L^∞ → ℝ are law-invariant, comonotone-additive (X,Y comonotone ⇒ X+c, Y+c comonotone) and uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous. By Wang–Wei–Willmot Theorem 1 ([SC 1], [CH 4], [KU 8], [DT 6]; the non-law-invariant ancestor is Schmeidler's norm-continuous representation [DT 5] / Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida [CH 11] / CMMM Cor. 18 [CH 2]) there are unique g, h ∈ H with Π̃ = I_g, ρ̃ = I_h; uniqueness of the distortion is [SC 1] Lemma 1. Normalization: g(1) = I_g(1) = Π(1) = 1, h(1) = ρ(1) = 1 ([SC 1] Lemma 2(ii)). Positivity: g(p) = Π(1_B) > 0 for P(B)=p ∈ (0,1] by B3. On 𝒳, I_g = C_g and I_h = C_h, so LR(X) = ρ(X)/Π(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X). Non-degeneracy: h(p_0)/g(p_0) = LR(1_A) ≠ 1, so h ≠ g. This proves (II).

PART C: uniqueness. Suppose LR = C_{h'}/C_{g'} with another pair as in (II). Bernoulli losses give h'(p)/g'(p) = LR(1_B) = h(p)/g(p) =: r(p). Two-layer losses 1 + c1_B give (1 + c h'(p))/(1 + c g'(p)) = LR(1+c1_B); if r(p) ≠ 1 this linear equation has the unique solution g'(p) = (1−LR(1+c1_B))/(c(LR(1+c1_B) − r(p))) = g(p). If r(p) = 1, take nested B, D (D ⊂ B or B ⊂ D, P(D) = p_0, so 1_B + 1_D is a comonotone sum): LR(1_B+1_D) = (g(p)+h(p_0))/(g(p)+g(p_0)) ≠ 1 because h(p_0) ≠ g(p_0), and solving gives g'(p) = g(p). Then h' = r·g' = h. (This is F3 made explicit; it needs (L7) and atomlessness.) Since Π = ω(·,1) = C_g and ρ = LR·Π = C_h are canonical constructions from LR, the revealed pair is the representing pair.

PART D: role of betweenness vs. the cardinal axiom (decision-theory dictionary). On each comonotone cone, (L3) is Dekel's betweenness [DT 2] and yields only an IMPLICIT, ordinal representation (level sets of LR are intersections of the cone with hyperplanes through 0 — Assa's abstract association with a family of comonotone-additive functionals ρ_β, strictly decreasing in β on each level set); Chew's weak substitution / Fishburn's transitive SSB theorem [DT 1],[DT 3] would upgrade this to LR = φ(u/w) with u, w additive on the cone and (u,w) only linear-fractionally unique, φ unknown. Axiom (L4) is the Aczél–Sincov–Cauchy cardinalization [DT 8]: it fixes φ = id, pins (u,w) = (ρ,Π) on the nose, and glues all comonotone cones through the common anchor 1. None of (L3),(L4) involves monotonicity, so the failure of monotonicity of ρ_β (F2) and of LR is irrelevant; monotonicity of the endpoints is an optional add-on (S1)–(S2) via [SC 1] Lemma 2(i) / Schmeidler [DT 5]/[CH 1] / Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1. Proof of corollary (ii): ρ_β = (ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} by linearity of k ↦ I_k, and LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ(X)−βΠ(X)=0 ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0 since Π>0; Lipschitz constant from [CH 5] Remark 2. Proof of (S5): ρ_β(X+c) = ρ_β(X)+c ([SC 1] Lemma 2(ii), (h−βg)(1)/(1−β)=1), so every sublevel set of ρ_β is a translate of {ρ_β ≤ 0} = {LR ≤ β}; convexity of all sublevel sets is quasi-convexity, equivalent to concavity of h−βg by [SC 2]/[CH 4] Theorem 3 (iv)⇔(v)⇔(i). Proof of (S6): F2 (nested layers 1_D ≤ 1_B+1_D ≤ 2·1_B give h/g constant, hence = 1).
MINIMALITY: (L2) is only a normalization: without it the same proof gives LR = C_h/C_g with g(1)=1, h(1)=LR(1), using any Z_0 ∈ 𝒳 as anchor; it cannot be 'violated' in an interesting way. (L3) cannot be dropped: LR := max(C_{h_1}, C_{h_2})/E with h_1 ≠ h_2 nondecreasing, h_i(1)=1 (a coherent, law-invariant, non-comonotone-additive numerator, e.g. max(ES_α, (1+θ)E)) satisfies (L2),(L5),(L6a),(L7) and scale invariance, but for comonotone X,Y with C_{h_1}(X) > C_{h_2}(X), C_{h_1}(Y) < C_{h_2}(Y) and LR(X)=LR(Y)=β one gets LR(X+Y) = max(C_{h_1}X+C_{h_1}Y, C_{h_2}X+C_{h_2}Y)/(EX+EY) < β, violating the equal-value clause of (L3); and it is not of the form C_h/C_g: the only candidate pair by the inversion of Part C is (max(h_1,h_2), id), which fails on nested layers 1_B+1_D where max(h_1(p)+h_1(q), h_2(p)+h_2(q)) < max(h_1,h_2)(p)+max(h_1,h_2)(q). (L4) cannot be dropped — this is the Chew/Fishburn ordinal gap: LR := φ(C_h/C_g) with φ strictly increasing, continuous, φ(1)=1, nonlinear (e.g. φ(t)=t²) satisfies (L2),(L3),(L5),(L6a),(L7) and scale invariance, but is not a ratio of comonotone-additive functionals; concretely with Π(X)=Π(Y)=1, C_h/C_g(X)=4, C_h/C_g(Y)=2 and φ(t)=t²: ω(X,Y)=5/7, ω(Y,1)=5/7, ω(X,1)=5.25/9.75 ≈ 0.538 ≠ 25/49 ≈ 0.510, so (L4a) fails. Within (L4), (a) and (b) are independent of each other only partially: given (L3) and (a), the Möbius computation LR(X+Y+1) evaluated as a mediant of (X+Y,1) and of (X,Y+1) yields (Π(X+Y) − Π(X) − Π(Y))·(ρ(X)+ρ(Y) − Π(X) − Π(Y)) = 0, so (b) can be replaced by cash-additivity ω(X+c,1)=ω(X,1)+c of the anchored odds (additivity then holds off {LR(X+Y)=1} and extends by continuity); conversely (a) cannot be derived from (b)+(L3) (the φ-example violates (a) but (b) is vacuous/false there too, so the sharpest statement is: (L4) as a whole is necessary, and (b) may be weakened to cash-additivity). (L5) cannot be dropped: a non-law-invariant pair LR = ∫X dν_1/∫X dν_2 with capacities/signed set functions of bounded variation ν_i not of the form k∘P satisfies all other axioms (CMMM Cor. 18 [CH 2], MSM Thm 4.2 [CH 11]); the non-law-invariant variant of the theorem is then 'LR = ratio of two Choquet integrals w.r.t. signed set functions of bounded variation', via Schmeidler's norm-continuous representation [DT 5]/[CH 11] applied to Π̃, ρ̃ — with the caveat that these results are stated on B(Σ) (pointwise functions) and need outer continuity or bounded variation rather than mere uniform continuity. (L6b) cannot be dropped: take g continuous on [0,1], g(0)=0, g>0 on (0,1], of UNBOUNDED variation (e.g. g(t) ∝ t(1 + ½ sin(1/t))) and h = id; C_g is comonotone-additive (Stieltjes form in q_X), law-invariant and ‖·‖_∞-continuous (uniform convergence ⇒ convergence of survival functions a.e., dominated convergence), C_g > 0, so LR = E/C_g satisfies (L2)–(L5),(L6a),(L7) but g ∉ H: the conclusion 'bounded variation' fails, exactly as Wang–Wei–Willmot Remark 3 predicts (with mere norm continuity the representation persists with non-BV distortion); so (L6b) is necessary for the BV class and can be traded for a weaker class. (L6a) is used for (i) passing from rational to real homogeneity (Cauchy regularity, [DT 8](ii)) and (ii) the fair-set extension; I cannot exhibit a counterexample satisfying all other axioms (Hamel-type additive perturbations of Π destroy positivity, which (L3) then detects), so necessity of (L6a) is NOT established — it may be a convenience. (L7) cannot be dropped only because of uniqueness: LR ≡ 1 satisfies (L2)–(L6) vacuously and is represented by every h = g (positive), so (L7) is exactly the non-identifiability case of F3; (L7) is also used to make the odds construction non-vacuous. Atomlessness is needed for the upper-set construction in B0/B4 and for law invariance ⇒ distortion form (Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i) [CH 7]; on spaces with atoms comonotone-additive law-invariant functionals need not be distortions). Monotonicity of LR is NOT assumed and could not be (F2): any axiom of Schmeidler-type (iv) [DT 9] or Cherny–Madan (S1) [DT 10] on LR is inconsistent with (L7).
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) No fresh literature verification: WebSearch remained disabled (HTTP 403) in this run; the decision-theory theorems (Chew 1983, Dekel 1986, Fishburn 1983, Yaari 1987, Aczél 1966) are quoted from memory and are used only as motivation/dictionary in Part D, while the load-bearing steps use Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 Theorem 1, Lemmas 1–2, 4 and Theorem 3, which other panel members read in full. I did not find (and, given the digest, do not believe there exists) a published iff characterization of a ratio of two Choquet integrals; the closest published templates remain Han–Lin–Wang Thm 1 [DT 7] and Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei Thm 3 [SC 11]. (2) Axiom (L4) is strong: although phrased as functional equations on LR-values, it encodes 'the mediant weights are proportional to an additive premium', i.e. it is essentially the premium-weighted mediant property. Its intrinsic content is that the revealed premium Π = ω(·,1) is additive on comonotone pairs; readers may regard this as a hypothesis on Π in disguise. (3) Axiom (L6b) (uniform continuity of the anchored odds on 𝒳_1) is the least elegant hypothesis: it is a statement about a derived object near the fair set N, where the defining quotient degenerates (0/0). The supplement (S1) shows it is dispensable when Π is monotone (then Lipschitz with constant Π(1)=1 by Denneberg/CMMM Prop. 16(iii)), and the counterexample shows it is needed for bounded variation; a cleaner derivation of uniform continuity from a Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei-type 'relevance/boundedness' axiom was not carried out. (4) Fair-set bookkeeping (Steps B0–B4) is done by perturbation and continuity; it is correct in outline but tedious, and the uniform-continuity needed for ρ̃ in B6 is argued through the Möbius profile rather than assumed directly — a referee may prefer the explicit axiom 'ρ = LR·ω(·,1) uniformly continuous' added to (L6b). (5) The theorem lives in the signed class: h may be non-monotone and even negative, so LR may be negative; the actuarially relevant monotone/coherent sub-classes are only obtained through the supplements (S1)–(S3), which are stated on the derived endpoints and not on LR pointwise — this is unavoidable by F2 but means the 'pair-free' claim is partly cosmetic. (6) (S5) (quasi-convexity iff h−βg concave) inherits the Wang–Wei–Willmot test variables on L^∞; the restriction to L^∞_+ needs the translation argument sketched and is not fully checked at the boundary of 𝒳. (7) Domain: bounded losses only; an L^p version would need the ASTIN 'continuity at infinity' axiom [CH 5]/[SC 3] in place of (L6). (8) Necessity of (L6a) is not proved (no counterexample).

=== PROPOSAL 10: Möbius cash-profile characterization: the (extended) loss ratio is a projective ratio of two signed Choquet integrals — with monotone (distortion-pair), coherent (Kusuoka-pair) and quasi-convex sub-characterizations (Hirbod Assa (author) + prior-synthesis critic) ===
SETTING: SPACE AND NOTATION. (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P) with ‖·‖_∞; X =_d Y means equality in law; X,Y are comonotone if (X(ω)−X(ω'))(Y(ω)−Y(ω')) ≥ 0 for P⊗P-a.e. (ω,ω'). H := {k:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, k(0)=0}. For k ∈ H the signed Choquet integral in survival form is I_k(X) := ∫_0^∞ k(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (k(P(X>x)) − k(1))dx, X ∈ L^∞ (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020, Def. 1; P(X≥x) may replace P(X>x) on an atomless space, their Remark 1). If h is a distortion function (nondecreasing, h(0)=0, h(1)=1) then I_h = C_h is the ordinary Choquet/distortion functional of the project. The actuarial domain is 𝒳 := L^∞_+ \ {0}.

THE OBJECT. Assa (2024) Prop. 1 evaluates LR at cash-shifted positions X − m, which are not non-negative, and the association LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0 is a statement about level crossings along cash shifts. The object that carries this structure is therefore the EXTENDED loss ratio, a map LR: L^∞ → RP^1 = R ∪ {∞} (the projective line; a ratio (a:b) is naturally projective, ∞ = 'premium zero'). The original loss ratio is its restriction LR_0 := LR|_𝒳, which under the positivity add-on takes values in [0,∞).

CASH PROFILE, DEGENERATE SET, PARAMETRIZATION. For X ∈ L^∞ the cash profile is φ_X(m) := LR(X − m), m ∈ R. The fair (degenerate) set is N := {X : φ_X ≡ 1}; constants always lie in N. A pair of maps a,b: L^∞ → R is a Möbius parametrization of LR if LR(X − m) = [a(X) − m : b(X) − m] ∈ RP^1 for all X ∈ L^∞, m ∈ R, with the convention [0:0] := 1 (so X ∈ N ⟺ a(X)=b(X), and for X ∉ N the profile is the non-constant Möbius map m ↦ (a(X)−m)/(b(X)−m), a bijection R → R\{1} with [a(X)−b(X):0] = ∞ at the pole). Off N the parametrization is intrinsic: a(X) is the unique zero of φ_X ('risk capital', ρ(X)) and b(X) is the unique pole ('premium', Π(X)). For X ∉ N and β ∈ R\{1} let m_β(X) be the unique m with LR(X − m) = β; then m_β(X) = (a(X) − βb(X))/(1−β), i.e. m_β = Assa's canonical ρ_β (sign remark: with the shift X − m one gets m = +ρ_β(X); the 'm = −ρ_β(X)' of Prop. 1 corresponds to X + m). Hence 'LR admits a cash-additive family {ρ_β} in association with (1−β)ρ_β affine in β' is the same thing as 'LR admits a cash-additive Möbius parametrization', and such a family is unique (ρ'_β(X) = ρ'_β(X − ρ_β(X)) + ρ_β(X) = ρ_β(X)).

CLASSES OF PAIRS. 𝒫 := {(h,g) ∈ H×H : h(1)=g(1)=1} (signed Choquet pairs, normalized); 𝒫_mon := {(h,g) ∈ 𝒫 : h,g nondecreasing} (distortion pairs, Schmeidler/Yaari/Wang–Young–Panjer); 𝒫_act := {(h,g) ∈ 𝒫_mon : g(p) > 0 for p ∈ (0,1]} (Assa's setting, Π>0 on 𝒳); 𝒫_coh := {(h,g) ∈ 𝒫_mon : h,g concave} (coherent/Kusuoka pairs).
AXIOMS ON LR: (A1) (A1) MÖBIUS CASH PROFILE (= Assa's association with a cash-additive canonical family). LR admits a Möbius parametrization (a,b) which is cash-additive: a(X+c)=a(X)+c, b(X+c)=b(X)+c for all X ∈ L^∞, c ∈ R. [Off N this is automatic from the Möbius form, since φ_{X+c}(m)=φ_X(m−c) shifts zero and pole by c; on N it is a condition on the parametrization, see axiomsOnPairOrFamily.] Equivalent formulation on LR: for every X with φ_X ≢ 1 the profile m ↦ LR(X−m) is a non-constant Möbius map with unit-slope numerator and denominator, i.e. m_β(X) exists uniquely for every β ≠ 1 and β ↦ (1−β)m_β(X) is affine. (A2) (A3) LAW INVARIANCE. X =_d Y ⟹ a(X)=a(Y) and b(X)=b(Y). [Off N equivalent to: LR(X−m)=LR(Y−m) for all m whenever X =_d Y.] (A3) (A4) COMONOTONE ADDITIVITY OF THE PARAMETRIZATION (= comonotone betweenness/mediant property). a(X+Y)=a(X)+a(Y) and b(X+Y)=b(X)+b(Y) for all comonotone X,Y ∈ L^∞. [LEMMA (proved in proofSketch, Step 4): given (A1), on triples X,Y,X+Y ∉ N this is EQUIVALENT to the intrinsic comonotone level-closure (M): if X,Y are comonotone, X,Y,X+Y ∉ N and LR(X)=LR(Y)=β ∈ R\{1}, then LR(X+Y)=β. (M) is F1's mediant property read at a common level; it implies the premium-weighted mediant LR(X+Y) = (b(X)LR(X)+b(Y)LR(Y))/(b(X)+b(Y)) and the two-sided inequality min{LR(X),LR(Y)} ≤ LR(X+Y) ≤ max{...} whenever b(X), b(Y) have the same strict sign.] (A4) (A5) CONTINUITY. a and b are uniformly continuous with respect to ‖·‖_∞. [In the monotone sub-theorem (A5) is dropped and replaced by (A5') a and b are monotone (X ≤ Y a.s. ⟹ a(X) ≤ a(Y), b(X) ≤ b(Y)); (A5') implies (A5) since a cash-additive monotone map is 1-Lipschitz.] (A5) NOT AXIOMS BUT CONSEQUENCES: (A2) scale invariance LR(λX)=LR(X), λ>0, with a,b positively homogeneous (from (A4)+(A5) via Cauchy's equation on rays, or WWW Lemma 2(iii)); a(0)=b(0)=0, a(c)=b(c)=c, LR(c)=1 for constants; the normalization a(1)=b(1)=1, i.e. h(1)=g(1)=1, is encoded by the UNIT slope in (A1).
HYPOTHESES NOT ON LR ALONE: (N-DATA) The only hypotheses that cannot be read off LR are the values of the parametrization (a,b) on the degenerate set N = {X : LR(X−m)=1 for all m} (which contains all constants and, in the representation, equals {I_h = I_g}); (A1),(A3),(A4),(A5) restricted to inputs in N are statements about the pair (ρ,Π) = (a,b) there. A posteriori they are redundant: the uniqueness clause shows that if LR ≢ 1 the whole parametrization, including its values on N, is forced by LR restricted to L^∞ \ N (two-layer losses). I could not prove the a-priori extension from L^∞\N to N from LR-axioms alone (law-invariant extension by continuity fails on non-standard atomless spaces, and comonotone perturbations need not leave N). | (MON) Monotonicity of a (ρ) and/or of b (Π) — equivalently h, g nondecreasing — must be stated on the pair: by F2 no property of LR itself can deliver it (LR_0 is pointwise monotone only when h=g). | (POS) Positivity b(X)>0 for X ∈ 𝒳 — equivalently g>0 on (0,1] — must be stated on Π (it is what makes LR_0 finite and real-valued on 𝒳; on LR it reads 'the pole of φ_X is positive for every X ∈ 𝒳'). | (SUB) Subadditivity/concavity add-ons (a or b subadditive ⟺ h or g concave; coherent ⟺ concave and nondecreasing) are hypotheses on the pair; their LR-level shadow is the quasi-convexity/quasi-concavity statement (iv) in the conclusion.
CONCLUSION: MAIN THEOREM. Let LR: L^∞ → RP^1. The following are equivalent:
(I) [representation] there exist (h,g) ∈ 𝒫 (h,g of bounded variation, h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1) such that LR(X) = [I_h(X) : I_g(X)] for all X ∈ L^∞, i.e. LR(X) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) when I_g(X) ≠ 0, LR(X)=∞ when I_g(X)=0 ≠ I_h(X), LR(X)=1 when I_h(X)=I_g(X)=0;
(II) [axioms] LR satisfies (A1), (A3), (A4), (A5).
Under (I)/(II): a = I_h = ρ, b = I_g = Π, N = {I_h = I_g}, and the β-capitals are exactly Assa's canonical family, m_β = (I_h − βI_g)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (a signed Choquet integral with distortion (h−βg)/(1−β), F7), so that LR and {m_β} are in association, with endpoints m_0 = ρ and lim_{β→∞} m_β = Π.
UNIQUENESS. (h,g) is uniquely determined by (a,b): h(p)=a(1_B), g(p)=b(1_B) for P(B)=p (WWW Lemma 1). If LR ≢ 1 then (a,b) — including its values on N — is uniquely determined by LR: pick q_0 with LR(1_{B_0}) ≠ 1 (exists, else h=g); then h(q_0),g(q_0) are the zero and pole of φ_{1_{B_0}}, and for any p the nested two-layer loss X = 1_B + 1_D with {P(B),P(D)} = {p,q_0} satisfies X ∉ N, so h(p) = (zero of φ_X) − h(q_0), g(p) = (pole of φ_X) − g(q_0). If LR ≡ 1 then h = g is arbitrary (non-uniqueness exactly as in F3).
SUB-CHARACTERIZATIONS (each an 'iff' inside (I)):
(i) [distortion pairs] a monotone ⟺ h nondecreasing; b monotone ⟺ g nondecreasing (WWW Lemma 2(i)). Hence: LR satisfies (A1),(A3),(A4),(A5') ⟺ LR = [C_h : C_g] with (h,g) ∈ 𝒫_mon, and here no continuity axiom is needed (Schmeidler 1986 + law invariance: Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1 / Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i) / Wang–Wang–Wei Cor. 1).
(ii) [Assa's setting] b > 0 on 𝒳 ⟺ g > 0 on (0,1]. COROLLARY for the original object: a map LR_0: 𝒳 → [0,∞) is of the form C_h/C_g with (h,g) ∈ 𝒫_act if and only if LR_0 is the restriction to 𝒳 of an LR: L^∞ → RP^1 satisfying (A1),(A3),(A4),(A5') with b>0 on 𝒳; the extension and (h,g) are unique when LR_0 ≢ 1 (F3: Bernoulli losses give h/g, two-layer losses a+c·1_B give g where h ≠ g, nested layers give g elsewhere).
(iii) [coherent / Kusuoka pairs] a subadditive ⟺ h concave; a coherent (monotone, subadditive) ⟺ h concave and nondecreasing ⟺ a(X)=∫_{[0,1]} ES_s(X) μ(ds) for a unique probability μ (Kusuoka Thm 7 / JST Thm 2.3 / Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 / Shapiro Thm 3.1(ii)); same for b. For a convex but non-monotone member m_β: m_β = ∫ES_s(·)dμ_β + ∫ES_s(−·)dν_β (Wang–Wang–Wei Thm 5), a two-sided Kusuoka form.
(iv) [shape of LR; corrects F4] On D_+ := {b > 0}: LR is quasi-convex (all sublevel sets {X ∈ D_+: LR(X) ≤ β} convex) ⟺ g is convex and h − g is concave, and then LR ≥ 1 on D_+ ('conic picture'; h concave is sufficient but NOT necessary); LR is quasi-concave on D_+ ⟺ g is concave and h − g is convex, and then LR ≤ 1 on D_+ ('actuarial picture', e.g. ρ = E, Π = C_g with g concave gives quasi-CONCAVITY, not quasi-convexity).
(v) [F2] LR_0 is pointwise monotone on 𝒳 ⟺ h = g ⟺ LR ≡ 1.
(vi) [F6 downgraded] V(β,X) := m_β(X) is a functional-level (zero-set) identification of LR, but LR_0 has mixture-convex level sets — hence is identifiable/elicitable in the Nolde–Ziegel/Fissler–Ziegel sense — only if h = g (Wang–Wei 2020 Thm 3.2 + Cor. 3.3 applied to the cash-additive signed Choquet integrals I_{h−βg}).
VARIANTS. Dropping (A3) and replacing (A5) by 'of bounded variation and outer continuous on B(Σ)' yields LR = [∫X dν_1 : ∫X dν_2] for two signed set functions of bounded variation (capacities under (A5')) (CMMM 2012 Cor. 18; Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida Thm 4.2). Replacing L^∞ by a law-invariant convex cone 𝒳 ⊃ L^∞ and adding 'continuity at infinity' of a,b yields the same theorem with distortion riskmetrics (Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 Thm 1).
PROOF SKETCH: STEP 0 (two elementary facts about Möbius profiles). (a) If a ≠ b the map m ↦ (a−m)/(b−m) is a bijection R → R\{1} with zero m=a and pole m=b, so for X ∉ N the zero/pole and all level cash amounts m_β(X) = (a(X) − βb(X))/(1−β), β ≠ 1, are determined by LR. (b) Because (a,b) ↦ [a−m : b−m] is injective off the diagonal, two Möbius parametrizations of the same LR coincide off N.

STEP 1 ((I) ⟹ (II)). Let (h,g) ∈ 𝒫 and put a := I_h, b := I_g, LR := [a : b]. By WWW 2020 Lemma 2(ii) [Choquet-scholar entry 4, signed-Choquet strand entry 1, Kusuoka strand entry 8, author strand entry 1]: I_k(X+c) = I_k(X) + c·k(1); with h(1)=g(1)=1 we get LR(X − m) = [a(X)−m : b(X)−m], so (a,b) is a cash-additive Möbius parametrization: (A1). I_k is law-invariant and comonotone-additive (WWW Thm 1, 'if' part; Schmeidler 1986 for the comonotone additivity of Choquet integrals): (A3),(A4). WWW Lemma 4: |I_k(X)−I_k(Y)| ≤ TV(k)‖X−Y‖_∞, so a,b are Lipschitz: (A5). Positive homogeneity (Lemma 2(iii)) gives scale invariance of LR. The conventions at the pole and on N are exactly those of the projective ratio, and N = {I_h = I_g} = {I_{h−g} = 0}. Finally m_β = (I_h − βI_g)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} by linearity of k ↦ I_k, which is F7.

STEP 2 ((II) ⟹ (I)). By (A3),(A4),(A5) each of a and b is a law-invariant, comonotone-additive, uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous functional on L^∞ of an atomless space, hence, by WWW 2020 Theorem 1 ('only if' part; statement read verbatim by four scholars from the authors' PDF: 'I: L^∞ → R is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly norm-continuous iff I = I_k for some k ∈ H'), a = I_h and b = I_g for unique h,g ∈ H. Cash-additivity with unit slope from (A1) and Lemma 2(ii) give 1 = a(X+1) − a(X) = h(1) and likewise g(1) = 1, so (h,g) ∈ 𝒫. Since (I_h, I_g) is a Möbius parametrization of [I_h : I_g] and (a,b) is one of LR, evaluating (A1) at m = 0 gives LR(X) = [a(X) : b(X)] = [I_h(X) : I_g(X)] for every X (on N both sides equal 1 by convention). This is (I). Note that (A2) was never used: positive homogeneity of a,b follows from (A4) (a(nX) = n a(X) because X and X are comonotone, then rationals) and (A5).

STEP 3 (uniqueness). h(p) = I_h(1_B) = a(1_B) for P(B)=p (atomless, so every p is attained; WWW Lemma 1). If LR ≢ 1 then h ≠ g (else LR ≡ [I_h : I_h] = 1), so there is q_0 with (h−g)(q_0) ≠ 0, i.e. 1_{B_0} ∉ N for P(B_0)=q_0, and a(1_{B_0}), b(1_{B_0}) are the zero and pole of φ_{1_{B_0}}. For p ∈ (0,1], take nested events with P-masses p and q_0 and X = 1_B + 1_D (D ⊂ B or B ⊂ D); by comonotone additivity a(X) = h(p)+h(q_0), b(X) = g(p)+g(q_0), and a(X) − b(X) = (h−g)(p) + (h−g)(q_0) — if this vanished for both nestings it would still be ≠ 0 for at least one choice unless (h−g)(p) = −(h−g)(q_0); in that case use instead X' = 1_B + 1_{D'} with P(D') = q_0 and the sum 1_B + 1_D + 1_{D'}... simpler: X = 1_B + 2·1_D with D ⊂ B gives a(X) − b(X) = (h−g)(p) + 2(h−g)(q_0) (comonotone additivity and homogeneity), so among X_1 = 1_B + 1_D and X_2 = 1_B + 2·1_D at least one lies outside N, and from its zero/pole and the known h(q_0), g(q_0) one reads off h(p), g(p). Hence (h,g), and therefore (a,b) = (I_h,I_g) everywhere including on N, are determined by LR. This is F3 in the extended setting. If LR ≡ 1, N = L^∞ and (I_h,I_h) parametrizes LR for every h.

STEP 4 (Lemma: (A4) off N ⟺ comonotone level-closure (M)). Assume (A1). (⟸) Let X,Y be comonotone with X,Y,X+Y ∉ N and fix β ≠ 1. Put X_β := X − m_β(X), Y_β := Y − m_β(Y); they are comonotone (cash shifts preserve comonotonicity), LR(X_β)=LR(Y_β)=β, and X_β + Y_β = X+Y − (m_β(X)+m_β(Y)) ∉ N (N is translation invariant by (A1)). By (M), LR(X_β+Y_β) = β, i.e. m_β(X)+m_β(Y) is a cash level at which X+Y has ratio β; by Step 0(a) this level is unique, so m_β(X+Y) = m_β(X)+m_β(Y). Using two values β_1 ≠ β_2 and m_β = (a − βb)/(1−β), solve the 2×2 system: a and b are additive on (X,Y). (⟹) a(X+Y)/b(X+Y) = (βb(X)+βb(Y))/(b(X)+b(Y)) = β if b(X)+b(Y) ≠ 0; if b(X)+b(Y)=0 then also a(X+Y)=0, so X+Y ∈ N, excluded. The weighted mediant and, for same-sign poles, the two-sided inequality of F1 follow from the identity a(X+Y)/b(X+Y) = (a(X)+a(Y))/(b(X)+b(Y)) (mediant of two fractions with denominators of equal sign). Cross pairs with LR(X)>1>LR(Y) cannot be brought to a common level with same-sign poles (pole of X − m_β(X) is (b−a)/(1−β)), which is why the correct intrinsic axiom is the level-closure (M), not the inequality.

STEP 5 (sub-characterizations). (i) WWW Lemma 2(i): I_k increasing ⟺ k increasing; monotone + cash-additive ⟹ 1-Lipschitz, so (A5') ⟹ (A5); conversely with (A5') one may also argue directly via Schmeidler 1986 (monotone + comonotone additive on B(Σ) ⟹ Choquet w.r.t. the capacity ν(A)=a(1_A)) plus law invariance (ν = h∘P on an atomless space: Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1; Guo et al. Prop. 2(i); Wang–Wang–Wei Cor. 1), which needs no continuity at all. (ii) b(1_B) = g(p) > 0 is necessary; sufficiency: for X ∈ 𝒳, P(X>x) > 0 on [0,ε) for some ε>0, so C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx > 0; then LR_0 = C_h/C_g ∈ [0,∞) and the Corollary follows from the main theorem and F3. (iii) WWW Thm 3 (i)⟺(iii): I_k subadditive ⟺ k concave; Wang–Wang–Wei Cor. 1: coherent ⟺ concave and increasing with k(1)=1; Kusuoka/JST/FS 4.93 give the ES-mixture with unique μ (Shapiro Thm 3.1(ii)); Wang–Wang–Wei Thm 5 for convex non-monotone m_β. (iv) For β ≠ 1 write J_β := a − βb = I_{h−βg}; J_β is cash-additive with slope 1−β ≠ 0, so all its sublevel sets are translates of {J_β ≤ 0}. On D_+ = {I_g > 0}: {LR ≤ β} ∩ D_+ = {J_β ≤ 0} ∩ D_+. Necessity of 'g convex and h−g concave': let β>1 and X,Y ∈ L^∞ with J_β(X),J_β(Y) ≤ 0; for K large, X+K, Y+K ∈ D_+ and J_β(X+K) = J_β(X) + K(1−β) ≤ 0, so by convexity of {LR ≤ β} ∩ D_+ the midpoint (X+Y)/2 + K lies in it, whence J_β((X+Y)/2) ≤ 0: {J_β ≤ 0} is convex on L^∞, so J_β is quasi-convex, so (WWW Thm 3 (v)⟺(i)) h − βg is concave for every β>1; dividing by β and letting β→∞ gives −g concave (g convex), letting β↓1 gives h − g concave. Sufficiency: for β>1, h − βg = (h−g) + (1−β)g is concave, so J_β is convex (Thm 3 (i)⟹(iv)) and {J_β ≤ 0} is convex; D_+ = {I_g > 0} is convex because I_g is concave (−I_g = I_{−g}, −g concave); for β<1 the set is empty because h−g concave with (h−g)(0)=(h−g)(1)=0 forces h−g ≥ 0, hence I_{h−g} ≥ 0 (WWW Lemma 1), i.e. LR ≥ 1 on D_+; at β=1 the set {I_{h−g} = 0} ∩ D_+ is the zero-sublevel set of the convex functional I_{h−g} ≥ 0, convex. Quasi-concavity: apply the same to −LR via (h,g) ↦ roles of superlevel sets, obtaining g concave and h−g convex and LR ≤ 1 on D_+. (v) If LR_0 is pointwise monotone and g>0 on (0,1]: for D ⊂ B with P(D)=q < P(B)=p, 1_B ≤ 1_B + 1_D ≤ 2·1_B gives LR(1_B) ≤ LR(1_B+1_D) ≤ LR(2·1_B) = LR(1_B), i.e. (h(p)+h(q))/(g(p)+g(q)) = h(p)/g(p), i.e. h(q)/g(q) = h(p)/g(p); taking p=1 gives h/g ≡ 1 on (0,1], h = g, LR ≡ 1 (atomlessness is used to realize all p,q). (vi) The level set {LR_0 = β} is the zero set of I_{h−βg} intersected with 𝒳; since I_{h−βg} is cash-additive with non-zero slope, mixture-convexity of its zero set for all β in the range of LR_0 forces (Wang–Wei 2020 Thm 3.2, Cor. 3.3) each (h−βg)/(1−β) to be monotone and in the class H* (VaR-type, mean, ess-sup/ess-inf mixtures); an affine-in-β family of two distinct H*-shapes leaves H*, so h ∝ g, and h(1)=g(1) gives h=g (up to the technical restriction to non-negative losses, which the three-point-law argument of Wang–Ziegel 2015 accommodates on 𝒳 because zero sets of positively homogeneous functionals are cones).

LITERATURE USED (by digest entries): WWW 2020 MOR Thm 1, Lemmas 1, 2, 4, Thm 3 [Choquet scholar 4; signed-Choquet strand 1–2; Kusuoka strand 8; elicitability scholar 5; author strand 1]; Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 ASTIN Thm 1, Prop. 2, Cor. 1, Thm 5 [Choquet scholar 5; signed-Choquet strand 3; Kusuoka strand 9]; Schmeidler 1986 [Choquet scholar 1; insurance scholar 3; author strand 4]; Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1 [elicitability scholar 8]; Guo–Wang–Xia–Yang Prop. 2(i) [Choquet scholar 7]; Kusuoka Thm 7 / JST Thm 2.3 / FS Thm 4.93 / Shapiro Thm 3.1 [Kusuoka strand 2, 5, 11; author strand 5]; Wang–Wei 2020 Thm 3.2, Cor. 3.3 [signed-Choquet strand 4; elicitability scholar 4]; Wang–Ziegel 2015 Thm 3.1 [elicitability scholar 9]; CMMM 2012 Cor. 18 and Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida Thm 4.2 [Choquet scholar 2, 11]; Bielecki–Cialenco–Zhang Thm 4.6 / Cherny–Madan recovery formula for the cash-shift construction of the family [acceptability scholar 11; performance-ratio scholar 2]; Chew/Dekel/Fishburn betweenness for the reading of (M) as betweenness and of (A1) as the 'fanning/weak substitution' that makes the family affine [decision-theory scholar 1–3].
MINIMALITY: Each axiom is independent of the others (all other axioms hold in each example), and dropping it destroys the conclusion.
(A1) Möbius profile: LR := φ(I_h/I_g) with φ strictly increasing, non-affine, φ(1)=1 (e.g. φ(t)=t^3), for (h,g) ∈ 𝒫_act with h ≠ g. This LR is scale invariant, law invariant, continuous off poles, has the same level sets as I_h/I_g (so (M) and the two-sided mediant inequality hold), but its cash profile φ((a−m)/(b−m)) is not Möbius and LR is not a ratio of two cash-additive comonotone-additive functionals (the zero of the profile is still I_h(X) and the pole I_g(X), so a representation would force the profile to be Möbius). This is the ordinal/cardinal gap of Chew–Dekel–Fishburn: betweenness alone gives LR = φ(ρ/Π); the unit-slope Möbius structure is exactly what fixes φ = id and the normalization h(1)=g(1)=1 (a profile (a−m)/(b−κm) with κ ≠ 1 would give g(1)=κ).
(A3) law invariance: a(X) := ∫X dν for a capacity ν not of the form h∘P (e.g. ν(A) := P(A∩A_0)/P(A_0) + ... any capacity with ν(A) ≠ ν(A') for some P(A)=P(A')), b := E. Then (A1),(A4),(A5) hold, the representation is LR = [∫X dν : E X] (CMMM Cor. 18 / Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida), but a(1_A) depends on A and not only on P(A), so no distortion pair exists.
(A4) comonotone additivity: a(X) := E[X] + ½E|X − E X| (mean plus half the mean absolute deviation), b := E. Then a is law-invariant, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, 2-Lipschitz, so (A1),(A3),(A5) hold and LR is scale invariant; but a is not comonotone additive (X=1_B, Y=1_D, D ⊂ B, P(B)=½, P(D)=¼: the deviations X−EX and Y−EY have opposite signs on B\D, so MAD(X+Y) < MAD(X)+MAD(Y)). If LR were [I_h : I_g], then off N = {MAD = 0} = constants the zero/pole would force I_h = a, I_g = E, and on constants both equal c, so I_h = a everywhere, contradicting WWW Thm 1 ('only if'). Note also that LR here has the two-sided mediant inequality only on pairs where MAD is additive, so (M) genuinely fails.
(A5) continuity: let Λ be a discontinuous linear functional on the space BV(0,1) of functions of bounded variation (Hamel basis) with Λ(1)=1, and define a(X) := Λ(q_X), q_X the quantile function of X (law-invariant, comonotone additive because q_{X+Y} = q_X + q_Y for comonotone X,Y, positively homogeneous, cash-additive), b := E. Then (A1),(A3),(A4) hold and LR is scale invariant, but a is not continuous, hence not a signed Choquet integral (those are Lipschitz, Lemma 4), and no representation exists. Non-constructive, but it shows uniform continuity (or, in the monotone version, monotonicity, which gives 1-Lipschitz continuity for free) cannot be dropped; WWW Remark 3 shows that mere norm-continuity gives the integral form with h not of bounded variation.
(A2) scale invariance is REDUNDANT (implied by (A4)+(A5)); listing it as an axiom, as Assa's Prop. 1 does, costs nothing but is not needed. The normalization LR(1)=1 is likewise implied.
Atomlessness: on a finite space with non-uniform P, law invariance + comonotone additivity still give ν = h∘P but h is only defined on the finite range of P, so uniqueness of (h,g) on [0,1] fails; on a space with atoms the Bernoulli/two-layer identification of F3 and the 'every p is attained' step of Step 3 break.
Level-closure (M) vs. the two-sided inequality: the inequality min ≤ LR(X+Y) ≤ max for comonotone X,Y is a CONSEQUENCE for same-sign poles but is not the right axiom (it is vacuous or false for cross pairs with poles of opposite sign); (M) is the exact intrinsic form of (A4) off N.
Monotonicity/positivity/concavity add-ons: necessity of 'h nondecreasing' for monotone a is Lemma 2(i) (take X = 1_B ≤ 1_{B'}); 'g>0 on (0,1]' for finiteness of LR_0 is forced by X = 1_B; the shape iff in (iv) is sharp: h = g strictly convex gives LR ≡ 1 (quasi-convex) with h not concave, showing 'h concave' is not necessary, and h = id, g strictly concave gives a quasi-concave, not quasi-convex, LR (the F4 correction). What I cannot do: give an iff with hypotheses on the un-extended LR_0 alone without any reference to cash shifts (no such theorem exists in the literature searched — Han–Lin–Wang's DQ Theorem 1 and Cherny–Madan Theorem 1 both need a monotone family, impossible here by F2 — and Step 3 shows LR_0 does determine the extension when LR_0 ≢ 1, but the extension's existence is a genuine hypothesis).
SELF-DECLARED WEAKNESSES: (1) The theorem characterizes the EXTENDED loss ratio on L^∞ with projective values; this is the object implicit in Assa's Prop. 1 (cash shifts X − m), but the actuarial LR_0 on 𝒳 = L^∞_+ \ {0} is covered only through the Corollary in (ii), whose hypothesis 'LR_0 extends to an LR satisfying (A1),(A3),(A4),(A5')' is an existence assumption; the extension is unique when LR_0 ≢ 1, but I have no intrinsic test on LR_0 for its existence other than the representation itself.
(2) The degenerate set N (fair positions, always containing the constants) is a blind spot of LR: the axioms (A1),(A3)–(A5) on inputs in N are hypotheses on the parametrization (a,b) = (ρ,Π), not on LR. Uniqueness (Step 3) shows these values are forced a posteriori, but I could not prove an a-priori extension from L^∞ \ N (law-invariant continuous extension can fail on non-standard atomless spaces; comonotone perturbations need not leave N, e.g. when h−g vanishes on the range of a survival function with a large top atom).
(3) The decisive tool, WWW 2020 Theorem 1, was read verbatim by several panel scholars from the authors' PDF (high confidence), but Schmeidler 1986, Kusuoka 2001, Kou–Peng Lemma 2.1 and Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 are quoted from memory/secondary restatements (medium confidence); WebSearch was disabled and publisher sites blocked, so no new primary iff theorem on ratios of Choquet integrals was found — the claim that none exists rests on the ten scholars' searches (OpenAlex, arXiv, author PDFs), not on an exhaustive database query.
(4) Sub-characterization (iv) is proved on D_+ = {Π > 0} of L^∞, not on 𝒳 alone; the 'only if' uses large positive cash shifts, which leave 𝒳 only through D_+; restricting to 𝒳 the necessity direction is open (one would need the WWW Thm 3 test variables to be non-negative).
(5) Sub-characterization (vi) (no CxLS unless h = g) inherits a domain technicality from Wang–Wei (three-point laws on the whole real line); the transfer to non-negative losses via cones of zero sets is sketched, not fully written.
(6) The example for the necessity of (A5) is non-constructive (Hamel basis); a constructive comonotone-additive, law-invariant, cash-additive, non-uniformly-continuous functional on L^∞ is not given.
(7) The theorem is for bounded losses; the L^p / Orlicz extension (Wang–Wang–Wei Thm 1 with 'continuity at infinity') is stated as a variant without proof, and Shapiro's warning that comonotonic ⟺ single-generator fails at p = ∞ is not relevant here but the Kusuoka uniqueness in (iii) is quoted for p < ∞.
(8) Corrections to the earlier synthesis that this theorem forces: F4's 'actuarial picture' is quasi-concavity, not quasi-convexity, and even the conic picture's 'h concave' is only sufficient; F6's 'identification function' is a zero-set identification only (Nolde–Ziegel identification functions are pointwise, and LR admits one only in the mixture-linear case E[r(X)]/E[s(X)], which is never scale invariant and non-constant); Prop. 1's sign: m = +ρ_β(X) for X − m.
